A GIT construction of moduli spaces of sheaves of length 2
Yikun Qiao

Abstract
Let 𝕜 \Bbbk be an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero. Let Sch / 𝕜 \mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk denote the category of schemes of finite type over 𝕜 \Bbbk . Let B B be a connected projective scheme over 𝕜 \Bbbk and let ℒ \mathcal{L} be an ample line bundle on B B . Let τ \tau be a Harder-Narasimhan type of length 2, and let δ ∈ ℕ \delta\in\mathbb{N} . We say a pure sheaf ℰ \mathcal{E} on B B is ( τ , δ ) (\tau,\delta) -stable if its Harder-Narasimhan filtration 0 = ℰ ≤ 0 ⊊ ℰ ≤ 1 ⊊ ℰ ≤ 2 = ℰ 0=\mathcal{E}_{\leq 0}\subsetneq\mathcal{E}_{\leq 1}\subsetneq\mathcal{E}_{\leq 2}=\mathcal{E} is non-splitting, of type τ \tau , with stable subquotients, and δ = dim 𝕜 Hom 𝒪 B ​ ( ℰ 2 , ℰ 1 ) \delta=\dim_{\Bbbk}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{B}}(\mathcal{E}_{2},\mathcal{E}_{1}) for ℰ i := ℰ ≤ i / ℰ ≤ i − 1 \mathcal{E}_{i}:=\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}/\mathcal{E}_{\leq i-1} . We define a moduli functor 𝐌 τ , δ ′ \mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta} classifying ( τ , δ ) (\tau,\delta) -stable sheaves on B B and construct its coarse moduli space by non-reductive geometric invariant theory (GIT). We extend the non-reductive GIT in [ 2 ] and [ 1 ] to linear actions on non-reduced schemes, and apply our non-reductive GIT to prove that the sheafification ( 𝐌 τ , δ ′ ) ♯ (\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta})^{\sharp} on ( Sch / 𝕜 ) e ´ ​ t ​ a ​ l ​ e (\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk)_{\acute{e}tale} is represented by a quasi-projective scheme. Our methods generalise Jackson’s construction of moduli spaces of ( τ , δ ) (\tau,\delta) -stable sheaves in [ 11 ] in the category of varieties, to allow non-reduced moduli schemes.



1
Introduction

Let
\Bbbk
be an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero. Let
\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
be the category of schemes of finite type over
\Bbbk
. In this situation, a closed point of a scheme is equivalent to a
\Bbbk
-point. Let
B
be a connected projective scheme over
\Bbbk
and let
\mathcal{L}
be an ample line bundle on
B
. Let
f:B\to\mathrm{Spec}(\Bbbk)
be the structure morphism.

Let
P_{1}(t),P_{2}(t)\in\mathbb{Q}[t]
be two numerical polynomials satisfying

\frac{P_{1}(t)}{\alpha_{1,d_{1}}}>\frac{P_{2}(t)}{\alpha_{2,d_{2}}}

(1)

where
\alpha_{i,d_{i}}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
is the integer such that
\frac{\alpha_{i,d_{i}}}{d_{i}!}t^{d_{i}}
is the leading monomial of
P_{i}(t)
, and the total order on
\mathbb{Q}[t]
is the lexicographical order. Let
\tau
be a Harder-Narasimhan type of length 2 associated to Hilbert polynomials
P_{1}(t),P_{2}(t)
, in the sense that we say a pure sheaf
\mathcal{E}
on
B
has type
\tau
if
\mathcal{E}_{i}:=\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}/\mathcal{E}_{\leq i-1}
is semistable of Hilbert polynomial
P_{i}(t)
for
i=1,2
, where

0=\mathcal{E}_{\leq 0}\subsetneq\mathcal{E}_{\leq 1}\subsetneq\mathcal{E}_{\leq 2}=\mathcal{E}

(2)

is the Harder-Narasimhan filtration of
\mathcal{E}
. Let
\delta\in\mathbb{N}
, which we choose as another discrete invariant. For a pure sheaf
\mathcal{E}
of type
\tau
, we say that the space of
unipotent endomorphisms
of
\mathcal{E}
has dimension
\delta
if
\delta=\dim_{\Bbbk}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{B}}(\mathcal{E}_{2},\mathcal{E}_{1})
.

Jackson has studied the moduli problem of
(\tau,\delta)
-stable sheaves
in
[
11
]
. A sheaf
\mathcal{E}
on
B
is called
(\tau,\delta)
-stable
if

•

\mathcal{E}
has type
\tau
;

•

\mathcal{E}_{i}
is stable for
i=1,2
;

•

\mathcal{E}\not\cong\mathcal{E}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{E}_{2}
;

•

\delta=\dim\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{B}}(\mathcal{E}_{2},\mathcal{E}_{1})
.

He used non-reductive geometric invariant theory developed in
[
2
]
and
[
1
]
to construct moduli spaces of
(\tau,\delta)
-stable sheaves. However, the non-reductive GIT in
[
2
]
and
[
1
]
is for actions on
reduced
projective schemes, while the desired moduli spaces may not be necessarily reduced (See
[
3
]
Remark 7.8. ). The paper aims to extend results in
[
11
]
to allow potentially non-reduced moduli schemes.

For
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
, we call a coherent sheaf
\mathcal{E}
on
B_{T}:=B\times_{\Bbbk}T
a
flat family over
T
of
(\tau,\delta)
-stable sheaves on
B
if:

•

there is a length 2 filtration of
\mathcal{O}_{B_{T}}
-submodules

0=\mathcal{E}_{\leq 0}\subsetneq\mathcal{E}_{\leq 1}\subsetneq\mathcal{E}_{\leq 2}=\mathcal{E}

(3)

such that
\mathcal{E}_{i}:=\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}/\mathcal{E}_{\leq i-1}
is a flat family over
T
of stable sheaves of Hilbert polynomial
P_{i}(t)
for
i=1,2
;

•

\mathcal{E}_{\leq 1}\subsetneq\mathcal{E}_{\leq 2}
does not split at any closed
t\in T
;

•

for every closed subscheme
T^{\prime}\hookrightarrow T
, the following
\mathcal{O}_{T^{\prime}}
-module is locally free of rank
\delta

(B_{T^{\prime}}\to T^{\prime})_{*}\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{O}_{B_{T^{\prime}}}}\big(\mathcal{E}|_{B_{T^{\prime}}},\mathcal{E}_{1}|_{B_{T^{\prime}}}\big).

(4)

We define a moduli functor
\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta}:(\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk)^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Set}
which to
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
assigns the set

\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta}(T):=\Big\{\begin{matrix}\textrm{isomorphism classes of flat families}\\
\textrm{over }T\textrm{ of }(\tau,\delta)\textrm{-stable sheaves on }B\end{matrix}\Big\}.

(5)

The final result of this paper is the following.

Theorem
.

Let
\Bbbk
be an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero. Let
\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
denote the category of schemes of finite type over
\Bbbk
. Let
\tau
be a Harder-Narasimhan type of length 2. Let
\delta\in\mathbb{N}
. Then there exists a quasi-projective scheme
M_{\tau,\delta}\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
representing
(\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta})^{\sharp}
, the sheafification of
\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta}
with respect to the étale topology. In particular, the moduli functor
\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta}
has a coarse moduli space
M_{\tau,\delta}
.

In Section 2, we first introduce the notion of
flat families of
(\tau,\delta)
-stable sheaves on
B
parametrised by schemes (Definition
2.3
), which generalises the notion of
(\tau,\delta)
-stable sheaves. We consider the moduli functor
\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta}
sending
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
to the set of isomorphism classes of flat families over
T
of
(\tau,\delta)
-stable sheaves on
B
. Next, we set up the non-reductive GIT problem in the category of schemes. For
m\gg 0
and
V:=\Bbbk^{P(m)}
, there exist locally closed subschemes
Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{(s)s}}\subseteq Y_{\tau}\subseteq\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
, where
V(-m):=V\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{L}^{-m}
. The schemes
Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{(s)s}}\subseteq Y_{\tau}
are determined by their functors of points. For example, a morphism
T\to Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}
is equivalent to an isomorphism class of a flat family over
T
of quotients of
V(-m):=V\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{L}^{-m}

q:V(-m)_{T}\to\mathcal{E},\quad V(-m)_{T}:=(B_{T}\to B)^{*}\big(V(-m)\big)

(6)

denoted by
[(\mathcal{E},q)]
, such that
\mathcal{E}
is a flat family over
T
of sheaves of type
\tau
with stable subquotients. Let
\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\subseteq\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
denote the scheme theoretic closure. Let
\mathrm{P}_{\tau}\subseteq\mathrm{SL}(V)
be the parabolic subgroup stabilising the partial flat
0=V_{\leq 0}\subseteq V_{\leq 1}\subseteq V_{\leq 2}=V
, where
V_{i}:=\Bbbk^{P_{i}(m)}
and
V_{\leq i}:=V_{1}\oplus\cdots\oplus V_{i}
. Recall that
\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
has a very ample line bundle
\mathcal{M}
. The non-reductive GIT problem we consider is

\mathrm{P}_{\tau}\curvearrowright\big(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}},\mathcal{M}\big|_{\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}}\big).

(7)

This is an enhancement of the non-reductive GIT problem considered in
[
11
]
, which was essentially the reduction of the linearisation above.

In Section 3, we develop the non-reductive GIT for groups of the form
\hat{U}:=U\rtimes\mathbb{G}_{m}
, where
U:=(\mathbb{G}_{a})^{r}
is additive and
\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\mathrm{Aut}(U)
induces a representation of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
on
\mathfrak{u}:=\mathrm{Lie}(U)
with a unique weight which is positive. Non-reductive GIT for linear
\hat{U}
-actions on reduced projective schemes has been considered in
[
2
]
,
[
1
]
and
[
6
]
. We follow their ideas and generalise to linear
\hat{U}
-actions on non-reduced projective schemes. Let
X
be a projective scheme and let
L
be an ample line bundle on
X
. Let
\hat{U}
act linearly on
X
with respect to
L
.

There is a morphism
\phi:\Omega_{X/\Bbbk}\to\mathfrak{u}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{X}
and we call
\phi
the
infinitesimal action of
\mathfrak{u}
on
X
. There are important Condition
UU
and Condition
WUU
, generalising
[
6
]
Assumption 4.40 and 4.42 for
\hat{U}\curvearrowright(X,L)
. Condition
UU
assumes that
X^{0}_{\min}\neq\emptyset
and that
\mathrm{coker}(\phi)|_{X^{0}_{\min}}
is locally free of constant rank. Condition
WUU
assumes roughly that
\mathrm{coker}(\phi)
is locally free of the lowest possible rank on an open subset which intersects with
Z_{\min}:=(X^{0}_{\min})^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}
, where
Z_{\min}
is defined as the
fixed point subscheme
in the sense of
[
4
]
. These conditions reduce to
[
6
]
Assumption 4.40 and 4.42 when
X
is reduced.

When Condition
UU
holds, the open subscheme
X^{0}_{\min}
has a quasi-projective universal geometric quotient by
U
(Theorem
3.24
), and
X^{0}_{\min}\setminus UZ_{\min}
has a projective universal geometric quotient by
\hat{U}
(Theorem
3.27
). Moreover the quotient
X^{0}_{\min}/U
represents the associated quotient sheaf on
(\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk)_{\acute{e}tale}
(Corollary
3.26
). When Condition
WUU
holds, we construct a
\hat{U}
-equivariant blowing up
b:\widetilde{X}\to X
such that Condition
UU
holds for
\hat{U}\curvearrowright\big(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{L}\big)
, where
\widetilde{L}:=\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{X}}(-E)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{X}}}b^{*}L^{a}
for
a\gg 0
and
E\subseteq\widetilde{X}
is the exceptional divisor.

In general, we only assume
X^{0}_{\min}\neq\emptyset
. We consider the
stratification by unipotent stabilisers

\coprod_{\delta\in\mathbb{N}}S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})\to X^{0}_{\min}
, where
S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})\subseteq X^{0}_{\min}
is the locally closed subscheme which is universal to the property that
\mathrm{coker}(\phi)|_{S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})}
is locally free of rank
\delta
. If
S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})\cap Z_{\min}\neq\emptyset
, then Condition
WUU
holds for
\hat{U}\curvearrowright\big(\overline{S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})},L\big|_{\overline{S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})}}\big)
, where
\overline{S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})}\subseteq X
is the scheme theoretic closure. We can apply our results with Condition
WUU
to get, for example, Theorem
3.33
and Theorem
3.34
.

In Section 4, we apply our non-reductive GIT for the moduli problem
\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta}
. We have defined
Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{(s)s}}\subseteq Y_{\tau}
in Section 2. Let
Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}:=S_{\delta}(Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}})\setminus\mathrm{U}_{\tau}Z_{\min}(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}})
, which is open in
S_{\delta}(Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}})
. Similar to
Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{(s)s}}\subseteq Y_{\tau}
, we can descibe
Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}\subseteq S_{\delta}(Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}})
by their functors of points. For a morphism
T\to\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
, it factors through
Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}
if the associated class of families
[(\mathcal{E},q)]
over
T
satisfies that
\mathcal{E}
is a flat family over
T
of
(\tau,\delta)
-stable sheaves (Lemma
4.2
). This links the moduli problem
\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta}
with the GIT problem in Section 2. By Theorem
3.34
, the action
\mathrm{P}_{\tau}\curvearrowright Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}
has a quasi-projective universal geometric quotient
Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}/\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
. The quotient
Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}\to Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}/\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
is the composition of a
\mathrm{U}_{\tau}
-quotient and a
\mathrm{P}_{\tau}/\mathrm{U}_{\tau}
-quotient. In both stages, the GIT quotients represent the associated quotient sheaves on
(\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk)_{\acute{e}tale}
. This observation together with a standard categorical argument (for example
[
9
]
Lemma 4.3.1) shows that
(\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta})^{\sharp}\cong Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}/\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
.

2
The moduli problem and the GIT problem

Recall that
\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
is the category of schemes of finite type over
\Bbbk
, and
B
is a projective scheme over
\Bbbk
with an ample line bundle
\mathcal{L}
. Recall
P_{i}(t),P(t)\in\mathbb{Q}[t]
are prescribed Hilbert polynomials.

For an integer
n\in\mathbb{Z}
and a vector space
V
, let
V(-n):=V\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{L}^{-n}
denote the coherent sheaf on
B
twisted from the constant sheaf by
\mathcal{L}^{-n}
. For
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
and
\mathcal{F}
a coherent sheaf on
B
, let
B_{T}:=B\times_{\Bbbk}T
denote the base change of
B
and let
\mathcal{F}_{T}:=(B_{T}\to B)^{*}\mathcal{F}
denote the base change of
\mathcal{F}
. Consider
\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-n)/B/\Bbbk}^{P,\mathcal{L}}
, the Quot scheme parametrising quotients
q:V(-n)\to\mathcal{E}
such that the
\mathcal{E}
has Hilbert polynomial
P(t)\in\mathbb{Q}[t]
with respect to
\mathcal{L}
.

2.1
The moduli problem

Let
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
. Recall that
\tau=(P_{1},P_{2})
is a Harder-Narasimhan type, and we say a coherent sheaf
\mathcal{E}
on
B
has type
\tau
if and only if
\mathcal{E}
is pure and there exists a length 2 filtration

0=\mathcal{E}_{\leq 0}\subsetneq\mathcal{E}_{\leq 1}\subsetneq\mathcal{E}_{\leq 2}=\mathcal{E}

(8)

such that
\mathcal{E}_{i}:=\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}/\mathcal{E}_{\leq i-1}
is semistable of Hilbert polynomial
P_{i}(t)
for
i=1,2
.

We extend the notion of type
\tau
Harder-Narasimhan filtration for flat families over
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
.

Definition 2.1
.

For
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
, let
\mathcal{E}
be a coherent
\mathcal{O}_{B_{T}}
-module flat over
T
. A
type
\tau
Harder-Narasimhan filtration
of
\mathcal{E}
is a filtration of coherent
\mathcal{O}_{B_{T}}
-modules

0=\mathcal{E}_{\leq 0}\subsetneq\mathcal{E}_{\leq 1}\subsetneq\mathcal{E}_{\leq 2}=\mathcal{E}

(9)

such that
\mathcal{E}_{i}:=\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}/\mathcal{E}_{\leq i-1}
is a flat family over
T
of semistable sheaves of Hilbert polynomial
P_{i}(t)
for
i=1,2
.

We say
\mathcal{E}
is a
flat family over
T
of type
\tau
sheaves on
B
if it admits a type
\tau
Harder-Narasimhan filtration.

Lemma 2.2
.

For
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
and a coherent
\mathcal{O}_{B_{T}}
-module
\mathcal{E}
. If a type
\tau
Harder-Narasimhan filtration exits, then the filtration is unique.

For a morphism
f:S\to T
in
\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
and a coherent
\mathcal{O}_{B_{T}}
-module
\mathcal{E}
with type
\tau
Harder-Narasimhan filtration
\{\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}\}_{i=0}^{2}
, the pullback
(1_{B}\times f)^{*}\mathcal{E}
has a type
\tau
Harder-Narasimhan filtration, which is the pullback of
\{\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}\}_{i=0}^{2}
.

Proof.

This is a special case of
[
13
]
Theorem 5, where the filtration
\{\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}\}_{i=0}^{2}
is called the
relative
Harder-Narasimhan filtration of
\mathcal{E}
.
∎

Definition 2.3
.

For
\delta\in\mathbb{N}
and
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
, a
flat family over
T
of
(\tau,\delta)
-stable sheaves on
B
is a flat family over
T
of type
\tau
sheaves
\mathcal{E}
, which has a Harder-Narasimhan filtration

0=\mathcal{E}_{\leq 0}\subsetneq\mathcal{E}_{\leq 1}\subsetneq\mathcal{E}_{\leq 2}=\mathcal{E}

(10)

satisfying:

•

\mathcal{E}_{i}=\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}/\mathcal{E}_{\leq i-1}
is a flat family over
T
of stable sheaves of Hilbert polynomial
P_{i}(t)\in\mathbb{Q}[t]
, for
i=1,2
;

•

\mathcal{E}_{\leq 1}\subseteq\mathcal{E}_{\leq 2}
does not split at any closed point
t\in T
;

•

for every closed subscheme
T^{\prime}\subseteq T
, the quasi-coherent
\mathcal{O}_{T^{\prime}}
-module

(B_{T^{\prime}}\to T^{\prime})_{*}\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{O}_{B_{T^{\prime}}}}\big(\mathcal{E}_{2}|_{B_{T^{\prime}}},\mathcal{E}_{1}|_{B_{T^{\prime}}}\big)

(11)

is locally free of rank
\delta
.

Remark 2.4
.

Let
T=\mathrm{Spec}(\Bbbk)
and let
d\in\mathbb{N}
. A flat family over
T
of type
\tau
sheaves is exactly a pure
\mathcal{O}_{B}
-module
\mathcal{E}
of Harder-Narasimhan type
\tau
. A flat family over
T
of
(\tau,\delta)
-stable sheaves is exact a pure
\mathcal{O}_{B}
-module
\mathcal{E}
of type
\tau
such that
\mathcal{E}_{1},\mathcal{E}_{2}
are stable and
\mathcal{E}\not\cong\mathcal{E}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{E}_{2}
and
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{B}}(\mathcal{E}_{2},\mathcal{E}_{1})
has dimension
\delta
.

The moduli functor
\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta}
is defined as follows

\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta}:(\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk)^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Set}

(12)

which sends
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
to the set

\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta}(T):=\Big\{\begin{matrix}\textrm{isomorphism classes of flat families}\\
\textrm{over }T\textrm{ of }(\tau,\delta)\textrm{-stable sheaves on }B\end{matrix}\Big\}

(13)

and for a morphism
g:T^{\prime}\to T
, the map

\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta}(g):\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta}(T)\to\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta}(T^{\prime})

(14)

sends the isomorphism class of
\mathcal{E}
to the isomorphism class of the class of
\mathcal{E}|_{B_{T^{\prime}}}
, where
\mathcal{E}|_{\mathcal{B}_{T^{\prime}}}:=(1_{B}\times g)^{*}\mathcal{E}
. Let
\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
act trivially on
\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta}
.

Consider the étale site
(\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk)_{\acute{e}tale}
. We will show that the sheafification
(\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta})^{\sharp}
is representable (Theorem
4.6
). The representative is the coarse moduli space of
\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta}
. The moduli space can be constructed by non-reductive GIT, which is quasi-projective.

2.2
Simpson’s and Jackson’s moduli spaces

We introduce Simpson’s moduli spaces of stable and semistable sheaves (
[
14
]
) and Jackson’s moduli spaces of sheaves of Harder-Narasimhan length 2 (
[
11
]
). These moduli spaces were constructed by reductive GIT and non-reductive GIT respectively. These moduli spaces were coarse moduli spaces of naturally defined moduli functors, so they are independent of the way we set up the GIT problems. Moduli spaces of stable sheaves have natural completions, the corresponding moduli spaces of semistable sheaves. In
[
11
]
, completions of the moduli spaces of sheaves of Harder-Narasimhan length 2 were described as non-reductive quotients of blow-ups of the associated GIT problem. However, it is unclear now whether the completion is independent of the GIT problem, and there is no natural moduli functor associated to the completion yet.

2.2.1
Moduli of stable and semistable sheaves

Let
B
be a connected projective scheme over
\Bbbk
with an ample line bundle
\mathcal{L}
. Let
P(t)\in\mathbb{Q}[t]
be a numerical polynomial. For
j\in\mathbb{Z}
and a coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on
B
, we denote
\mathcal{F}(j):=\mathcal{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{B}}\mathcal{L}^{j}
. Moduli spaces of semistable pure sheaves on
B
of fixed Hilbert polynomials were constructed by Simpson in
[
14
]
. A more recent reference is
[
9
]
.

Let
\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
be the category of schemes of finite type over
\Bbbk
. For
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
, a
family over
T
of (semi)stable sheaves of Hilbert polynomial
P(t)
is a coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on
B_{T}:=B\times_{\Bbbk}T
such that
\mathcal{F}
is flat over
T
and
\mathcal{F}_{s}
is a (semi)stable sheaf on
B
of Hilbert polynomial
P(t)
for every closed point
s\in T
, where
\mathcal{F}_{s}
is the pullback of
\mathcal{F}
along
B\cong B\times_{\Bbbk}\mathrm{Spec}(\kappa(s))\hookrightarrow B_{T}
. Two families
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
on
T
are called equivalent if there exists a line bundle
\mathcal{A}
on
T
such that
\mathcal{F}\cong\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{B_{T}}}(f_{T})^{*}\mathcal{A}
, where
f_{T}:B_{T}\to T
is the pullback of
f:B\to\mathrm{Spec}(\Bbbk)
. With the notions of families and their equivalence relations, we consider the moduli functors

\begin{split}\mathbf{M}_{B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L},\mathrm{ss}}:&(\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk)^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Set}\\
\mathbf{M}_{B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L},\mathrm{s}}:&(\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk)^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Set}\end{split}

(15)

which associate to
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
the sets

\begin{split}\mathbf{M}_{B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L},\mathrm{(s)s}}(T):=&\bigg\{\begin{matrix}\textrm{families on }T\textrm{ of (semi)stable sheaves}\\
\textrm{of Hilbert polynomial }P(t)\end{matrix}\bigg\}\bigg/\textrm{equivalence relation}\end{split}

(16)

and the functors on morphisms are naturally pullbacks of coherent sheaves. These functors are both corepresentable and
\mathbf{M}_{B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L},\mathrm{s}}
has a coarse moduli space.

By
[
14
]
Theorem 1.1 and
[
9
]
Theorem 3.3.7, the collection of semistable sheaves with Hilbert polynomial
P(t)
is bounded. By
[
9
]
Lemma 1.7.6, their regularities are uniformly bounded above. For
m\gg 0
, all semistable sheaves
\mathcal{F}
of Hilbert polynomial
P(t)
satisfy:

•

\dim H^{0}(B,\mathcal{F}(m))=P(m)
;

•

H^{0}(B,\mathcal{F}(m))\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{L}^{-m}\to\mathcal{F}
is surjective.

Let
V=\Bbbk^{P(m)}
. There is a non-canonical linear isomorphism
V\cong H^{0}(B,\mathcal{F}(m))
. With one such isomorphism, we have a surjective morphism

q:V(-m)\to\mathcal{F}

(17)

which represents a
\Bbbk
-point in
\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
.

Let
R\subseteq\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
be the open subscheme whose closed points are classes
[(\mathcal{F},q)]
such that:

(1)

\mathcal{F}
is semistable;

(2)

the induced map
V\cong H^{0}(B,V)\to H^{0}(B,\mathcal{F}(m))
is an isomorphism.

Let
R^{\mathrm{s}}\subseteq R
be the open subset of classes
[(\mathcal{F},q)]
such that
\mathcal{F}
is stable. Condition
(2)
above is open since the function
[(\mathcal{F},q)]\mapsto\dim H^{0}(B,\ker(q)(m))
is upper semi-continuous and the condition that
\dim H^{0}(B,\ker(q)(m))=0
is equivalent to the condition
(2)
. Condition
(1)
above is open by
[
9
]
Proposition 2.3.1. This justifies the claim that
R\subseteq\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
is open, and similarly
R^{\mathrm{s}}\subseteq R
is open.

Recall that for
M\gg m\gg 0
, there is a closed immersion

\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Grass}\big(V\otimes_{\Bbbk}H,P(M)\big)

(18)

where
H:=H^{0}(B,\mathcal{L}^{M-m})
. This is the classifying morphism of the quotient on
\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}

V\otimes_{\Bbbk}H^{0}(B,\mathcal{L}^{M-m})\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Quot}}=f_{\mathrm{Quot},*}\big(V_{\mathrm{Quot}}(M-m)\big)\to f_{\mathrm{Quot},*}\mathcal{U}(M)

(19)

where
V(-m)_{\mathrm{Quot}}\to\mathcal{U}
is the universal quotient on
B_{\mathrm{Quot}}=B\times_{\Bbbk}\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
, and
f_{\mathrm{Quot}}:B_{\mathrm{Quot}}\to\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
.

The Plücker immersion is a closed immersion

\mathrm{Grass}\big(V\otimes_{\Bbbk}H,P(M)\big)\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{N}:=\mathbb{P}\bigg(\Big(\bigwedge^{P(M)}\big(V\otimes_{\Bbbk}H\big)\Big)^{*}\bigg).

(20)

The very ample line bundle
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}(1)
pulls back to a very ample line bundle on
\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
, denoted by
\mathcal{M}
. Then we have

\mathcal{M}=\det\bigg(f_{\mathrm{Quot},*}\Big(\mathcal{U}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{B_{\mathrm{Quot}}}}\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{Quot}}^{\otimes M}\Big)\bigg).

(21)

There is an action of
\mathrm{GL}(V)
on
\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
, which is determined by maps

h_{\mathrm{GL}(V)}(T)\times h_{\mathrm{Quot}}(T)\to h_{\mathrm{Quot}}(T),\quad T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk.

(22)

Let
(g,q)\in h_{\mathrm{GL}(V)}(T)\times h_{\mathrm{Quot}}(T)
. The morphism
g:T\to\mathrm{GL}(V)
corresponds to an invertible morphism
g:V\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}\to V\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}
on
T
. Let
q:V(-m)_{T}\to\mathcal{F}
denote the family over
T
of quotients. We set the image of
(g,q)
to be the following family of quotients

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V(-m)_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V(-m)_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{-1}(-m)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}

(23)

It is easy to check the above maps are natural in
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
and each defines a group action. They define an algebraic group action
\mathrm{GL}(V)\curvearrowright\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
. Moreover, the action by
\mathrm{GL}(V)
is linear with respect to
\mathcal{M}
. See for example
[
9
]
Section 4.3, where the authors considered right actions.

We consider the linear action of the subgroup
\mathrm{SL}(V)
. It is easy to see
R
is invariant under
\mathrm{SL}(V)
. Let
\overline{R}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
be the scheme theoretic image of
R\to\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
, which is a subscheme on the closure of
R
by
Lemma 01R8
. The linearisation on
\mathcal{M}
pulls back to a linearisation
\mathcal{M}|_{\overline{R}}
. We can apply reductive GIT for the linear action of
\mathrm{SL}(V)
on
\overline{R}
with respect to
\mathcal{M}|_{\overline{R}}
. Note that
\mathcal{M}
depends on
M\gg m\gg 0
.

Theorem 2.5

(
[
9
]
Theorem 4.3.3, Theorem 4.3.4)
.

For
M\gg m\gg 0
, we have

•

GIT (semi)stable points are isomorphism classes
[(\mathcal{F},q)]\in\overline{R}
such that
\mathcal{F}
is

(semi)stable and
q:V(-m)\to\mathcal{E}
induces an isomorphism
V\cong H^{0}(B,\mathcal{E}(m))
, i.e.

\overline{R}^{\mathrm{SL}(V),\mathcal{M},\mathrm{ss}}=R,\quad\overline{R}^{\mathrm{SL}(V),\mathcal{M},\mathrm{s}}=R^{\mathrm{s}};

(24)

•

Two semistable points
[(\mathcal{F},q)],[(\mathcal{F}^{\prime},q^{\prime})]\in R
are (GIT)
S
-equivalent if and only if
\mathrm{gr}(\mathcal{F})\cong\mathrm{gr}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
, where
\mathrm{gr}(\mathcal{F})
denotes the associated graded sheaf of the Jordan-Hölder filtration of
\mathcal{F}
;

•

A semistable point
[(\mathcal{F},q)]\in R
has closed
\mathrm{SL}(V)
-orbit in
R
if and only if
\mathcal{F}
is polystable, i.e.
\mathcal{F}
is a direct sum of stable sheaves;

•

\overline{R}/\!/\mathrm{SL}(V)
corepresents
\mathbf{M}_{B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L},\mathrm{ss}}
and
R^{\mathrm{s}}/\mathrm{SL}(V)
corepresents
\mathbf{M}_{B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L},\mathrm{s}}
. Moreover
R^{\mathrm{s}}/\mathrm{SL}(V)
is a coarse moduli space of
\mathbf{M}_{B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L},\mathrm{s}}
.

Remark 2.6
.

The coarse moduli space of
\mathbf{M}_{B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L},\mathrm{s}}
has a natural compactification, which is the corepresentative of
\mathbf{M}_{B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L},\mathrm{ss}}
, which is independent of the GIT problem.

Remark 2.7
.

If there exists a semistable sheaf
\mathcal{F}
of Hilbert polynomial
P(t)
that is not polystable, then
\mathbf{M}_{B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L},\mathrm{ss}}
does not have a coarse moduli space, since
\eta:\mathbf{M}_{B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L},\mathrm{ss}}\to h_{\overline{R}/\!/\mathrm{SL}(V)}
is not bijective on
\mathrm{Spec}(\Bbbk)
.

2.2.2
Moduli of unstable sheaves

Let
\tau
be a Harder-Narasimhan type of length 2. Let
P_{i}(t)
for
1\leq i\leq 2
be the Hilbert polynomials associated to
\tau
in the sense that if
\mathcal{E}
is a pure sheaf of type
\tau
on
B
, then
P_{i}(t)
is the Hilbert polynomial of
\mathrm{HN}_{i}(\mathcal{E})/\mathrm{HN}_{i-1}(\mathcal{E})
for
1\leq i\leq 2
. We have that
P(t):=P_{1}(t)+P_{2}(t)
is the Hilbert polynomial of
\mathcal{E}
. Let
V_{i}:=\Bbbk^{P_{i}(m)}
for
1\leq i\leq 2
and let
V:=V_{1}\oplus V_{2}
. Denote
V_{\leq i}:=V_{1}\oplus\cdots\oplus V_{i}
. Let
\mathrm{P}_{\tau}\subseteq\mathrm{SL}(V)
be the parabolic subgroup of
g\in\mathrm{SL}(V)
stabilising the partial flag
0=V_{\leq 0}\subseteq V_{\leq 1}\subseteq V_{\leq 2}=V
.

It turns out that it is useful to refine the classification problem according to the dimension of the space of unipotent endomorphisms. In
[
11
]
Definition 7.12 and
[
7
]
Definition 4.18, the so-called
\tau
-stability
of pure sheaves was defined. In
[
7
]
Definition 6.1, the so-called
refined Harder-Narasimhan type
was defined, which encodes information on the dimension of the space of unipotent endomorphisms.

Theorem 1.1 in
[
14
]
shows that sheaves of fixed Harder-Narasimhan type are bounded. In the proof of
[
8
]
Proposition 6.8, a locally closed subscheme
Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{ss}}\subseteq\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
was described as a parameter space of sheaves of type
\tau
. Closed points in
Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{ss}}
are exactly isomorphism classes
[(\mathcal{E},q)]\in\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
such that each
\mathcal{E}_{i}
is semistable with Hilbert polynomial
P_{i}(t)
for
1\leq i\leq 2
, where
\mathcal{E}_{i}:=q\big(V_{\leq i}(-m)\big)\big/q\big(V_{\leq i-1}(-m)\big)
. In the same manner, one can define locally closed subschemes
Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}
and
Y_{\tau}
such that
Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}\subseteq Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{ss}}\subseteq Y_{\tau}\subseteq\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
. Closed points in
Y_{\tau}\subseteq\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
are classes
[(\mathcal{E},q)]
such that
\mathcal{E}_{i}
has Hilbert polynomial
P_{i}(t)
, and closed points in
Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}
are classes
[(\mathcal{E},q)]\in Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{ss}}
such that
\mathcal{E}_{i}
is stable. The locally closed subsets
Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{(s)s}}\subseteq Y_{\tau}
were studied in
[
8
]
,
[
10
]
,
[
11
]
and
[
7
]
, where moduli spaces of
\tau
-stable
sheaves were constructed as parabolic GIT quotients of
Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}
.

We will recover the locally closed subschemes
Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{(s)s}}\subseteq Y_{\tau}\subseteq\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
and actions of
\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
on them from a more functorial perspective in this subsection. From this new perspective, we do not only see closed points but also view
T
-valued points of
Y^{\mathrm{(s)s}}\subseteq Y_{\tau}
as families over
T
of quotients.

For
Y_{\tau}
, we describe the associated functor of points
\mathbf{Y}_{\tau}\subseteq\mathbf{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
as a
sub-functor represented by locally closed subschemes
. Let
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
and let
[(\mathcal{E},q)]\in\mathbf{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}(T)
be an isomorphism class of a quotient

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V(-m)_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}

(25)

Define the following notation for
1\leq i\leq 2

\begin{split}\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}:=&q\big(V_{\leq i}(-m)_{T}\big)\subseteq\mathcal{E}\\
\mathcal{E}_{i}:=&\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}/\mathcal{E}_{\leq i-1}.\end{split}

(26)

There is a morphism
q_{i}:V_{i}(-m)_{T}\to\mathcal{E}_{i}
such that the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\leq i-1}(-m)_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\leq i}(-m)_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{i}(-m)_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{\leq i-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{\leq i-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{\leq i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{i}}

(27)

where
q_{\leq i}:=q|_{V_{\leq i}(-m)_{T}}
and then the Five lemma shows the surjectivity of
q_{i}
.

The subset
\mathbf{Y}_{\tau}(T)
consists of
[(\mathcal{E},q)]
such that each
\mathcal{E}_{i}
is flat over
T
with Hilbert polynomial
P_{i}(t)
for
1\leq i\leq 2
. By
Lemma 00HM
and the fact that
\mathcal{E}
is flat over
T
, we have that each
\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}
is flat over
T
if each
\mathcal{E}_{i}
is flat over
T
. Since flatness and Hilbert polynomials are preserved under pullbacks, we have that
\mathbf{Y}_{\tau}
defines a sub-functor of
\mathbf{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
.

The following theorem:
flattening stratification
is important in the construction of Quot schemes. Here we apply it to show that
\mathbf{Y}_{\tau}\subseteq\mathbf{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
is represented by locally closed subschemes.

Theorem 2.8

(Theorem 2.1.5,
[
9
]
)
.

Let
X\to T
be a projective morphism of schemes of finite type over
\Bbbk
. Let
\mathcal{L}
be a line bundle on
X
which is ample relative to
X\to T
. Let
\mathcal{F}
be a coherent sheaf on
X
. Then:

•

The set of Hilbert polynomials is finite

\mathcal{P}:=\bigg\{P(t)\in\mathbb{Q}[t]:\begin{matrix}P(t)\textrm{ is the Hilbert polynomial of }\mathcal{F}\\
\textrm{at some closed point of }T\end{matrix}\bigg\};

(28)

•

For each
P(t)\in\mathcal{P}
the following functor is represented by a locally closed subscheme
T_{P}\subseteq T

\mathbf{Flat}_{\mathcal{F}/X/T}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}:(\mathrm{Sch}/T)^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Set},\quad T^{\prime}\mapsto\begin{cases}\{\mathrm{pt}\},&\begin{matrix}\mathcal{F}_{T^{\prime}}\textrm{ is flat over }T^{\prime}\\
\textrm{ with Hilbert polynomial }P(t)\end{matrix}\\
\emptyset,&\textrm{otherwise}\end{cases}

(29)

where
\mathrm{Sch}/T
is the category of schemes of finite type over
T
, and
\mathcal{F}_{T^{\prime}}
is the pullback of
\mathcal{F}
along
T^{\prime}\to T
;

•

The morphism
\coprod_{P\in\mathcal{P}}T_{P}\to T
is bijective on points.

Lemma 2.9
.

\mathbf{Y}_{\tau}\subseteq\mathbf{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
is representable by locally closed subschemes.

Proof.

Let
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
and consider the following diagram in
\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Y}_{\tau}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}

(30)

where
\xi:h_{T}\to\mathbf{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
is identified with a class
[(\mathcal{E},q)]\in\mathbf{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}(T)
by the Yoneda lemma.

Recall
\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}:=q\big(V_{\leq i}(-m)_{T}\big)
and
\mathcal{E}_{i}:=\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}/\mathcal{E}_{\leq i-1}
. For each
1\leq i\leq 2
consider the functor
\mathbf{Flat}_{\mathcal{E}_{i}/B_{T}/T}^{P_{i}(t),\mathcal{L}_{T}}
defined in Theorem
2.8
, which is represented by a locally closed subscheme
T_{\xi,P_{i}}\subseteq T
. Let

T_{\xi}:=T_{\xi,P_{1}}\times_{T}T_{\xi,P_{2}}

(31)

which is a locally closed subscheme of
T
.

For
T^{\prime}\to T
in
\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
, denote
\mathcal{E}|_{T^{\prime}}
the pullback of
\mathcal{E}
along
B_{T^{\prime}}\to B_{T}
, and similar for
\mathcal{E}_{i},\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}
. We have

\begin{split}&T^{\prime}\to T\textrm{ factors through }T^{\prime}\to T_{\xi}\to T\\
\textrm{if and only if}\quad&T^{\prime}\to T\textrm{ factors though }T_{\xi,P_{i}}\textrm{ for each }1\leq i\leq 2\\
\textrm{if and only if}\quad&\begin{cases}\mathcal{E}_{i}|_{T^{\prime}}\textrm{ is flat over }T^{\prime}\\
\textrm{with Hilbert polynomial }P_{i}(t)\end{cases}\textrm{ for }1\leq i\leq 2\\
\textrm{if and only if}\quad&(T^{\prime}\to T)^{*}\xi\in\mathbf{Y}_{\tau}(T^{\prime}).\end{split}

(32)

The above equivalences show the following diagram is Cartesian

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{T_{\xi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Y}_{\tau}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}

(33)

which proves the representability of
\mathbf{Y}_{\tau}\subseteq\mathbf{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
by locally closed subschemes.
∎

Since
\mathbf{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
is represented by
\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
, by Lemma
2.9
, there is a locally closed subscheme
Y_{\tau}\subseteq\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
representing
\mathbf{Y}_{\tau}
.

Consider
\prod_{i=1}^{2}\mathrm{Quot}_{V_{i}(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P_{i}(t),\mathcal{L}}
, where the fibre product is over
\Bbbk
. There is a morphism
\prod_{i=1}^{2}\mathrm{Quot}_{V_{i}(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P_{i}(t),\mathcal{L}}\to\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
. From their functors of points, we easily see that this morphism is a monomorphism, and it is proper by
Lemma 01W6
, thus a closed immersion by
Lemma 04XV
. The closed immersion
\prod_{i=1}^{2}\mathrm{Quot}_{V_{i}(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P_{i}(t),\mathcal{L}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
factors through
Y_{\tau}
, which is easily seen from the associated functors of points. There is a retraction
p_{\tau}:Y_{\tau}\to\prod_{i=1}^{2}\mathrm{Quot}_{V_{i}(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P_{i}(t),\mathcal{L}}
such that the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{i=1}^{2}\mathrm{Quot}_{V_{i}(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P_{i}(t),\mathcal{L}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{\tau}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{i=1}^{2}\mathrm{Quot}_{V_{i}(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P_{i}(t),\mathcal{L}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\tau}}

(34)

where
p_{\tau}
sends an isomorphism class
[(\mathcal{E},q)]
of families over
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
to
\Big[\Big(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{2}\mathcal{E}_{i},\bigoplus_{i=1}^{2}q_{i}\Big)\Big]
, where
\mathcal{E}_{i}
and
q_{i}
are defined in equation (
26
) and equation (
27
).

Consider
(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{2}\to\mathrm{GL}(V)
such that
(t_{1},t_{2})\mapsto t_{1}1_{V_{1}}\oplus t_{2}1_{V_{2}}\in\mathrm{GL}(V)
. Then
(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{2}
acts on
Y_{\tau}
. With this
(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{2}
-action, we have that
\prod_{i=1}^{2}\mathrm{Quot}_{V_{i}(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P_{i}(t),\mathcal{L}}
is the
fixed point subscheme

(Y_{\tau})^{(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{2}}\hookrightarrow Y_{\tau}
in the sense of
[
4
]
. It is easy to see if we consider
\lambda:\mathbb{G}_{m}\to(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{2}
given by
t\mapsto(t^{w_{1}},t^{w_{2}})
such that
w_{1}>w_{2}
, then the fixed point subscheme of the action of
\lambda
is also
\prod_{i=1}^{2}\mathrm{Quot}_{V_{i}(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P_{i}(t),\mathcal{L}}

\prod_{i=1}^{2}\mathrm{Quot}_{V_{i}(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P_{i}(t),\mathcal{L}}\cong(Y_{\tau})^{(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{2}}=(Y_{\tau})^{\lambda}.

(35)

We then describe the open subschemes
Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}\subseteq Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{ss}}\subseteq Y_{\tau}
. For
1\leq i\leq 2
, let
R_{i}^{\mathrm{(s)s}}\subseteq\mathrm{Quot}_{V_{i}(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P_{i}(t),\mathcal{L}}
be the open subscheme whose closed points are
[(\mathcal{E}_{i},q_{i})]
such that
\mathcal{E}_{i}
is a (semi)stable pure sheaf on
B
and
q:V_{i}(-m)\to\mathcal{E}_{i}
induces an isomorphism
V_{i}\cong H^{0}(B,\mathcal{E}_{i}(m))
(cf. Theorem
2.5
). We define
Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{(s)s}}
as the fibre product indicated in the diagram below

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{(s)s}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{\tau}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{i=1}^{2}R_{i}^{\mathrm{(s)s}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{i=1}^{2}\mathrm{Quot}_{V_{i}(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P_{i}(t),\mathcal{L}}.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\tau}^{\mathrm{(s)s}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\tau}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}

(36)

We have that
Y_{\tau}^{(s)s}\subseteq Y_{\tau}
is open. So
p_{\tau}^{\mathrm{(s)s}}:Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{(s)s}}\to\prod_{i=1}^{2}R_{i}^{\mathrm{(s)s}}
are also retractions as base changes of
p_{\tau}
. In particular,
p_{\tau}
and
p_{\tau}^{\mathrm{(s)s}}
are surjective.

There is a
\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
-action on
Y_{\tau}
induced from the
\mathrm{SL}(V)
-action on
\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
. Similarly there are
\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
-actions on
Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}\subseteq Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{ss}}
. These
\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
-actions are linearised with respect to the restrictions of
\mathcal{M}
on
\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
, where
\mathcal{M}
is the ample line bundle described in (
21
).

In
[
10
]
and
[
11
]
, Jackson considered the induced reduced subscheme structure on
Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}
for a Harder-Narasimhan type
\tau
of length 2, and stratify this reduced subscheme further according to the dimension of the stabiliser in the unipotent radical of
\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
. Non-reductive GIT could be applied to each stratum and the following theorem was proved.

Definition 2.10

(cf.
[
11
]
Definition 7.1.2,
[
7
]
Definition 6.1)
.

Let
B
be a connected projective scheme over
\Bbbk
with an ample line bundle
\mathcal{L}
. Let
\tau
be a Harder-Narasimhan type of length 2. Let
\delta\in\mathbb{N}
. A pure sheaf
\mathcal{E}
on
B
is called
(\tau,\delta)
-stable
if:

•

\mathcal{E}
has Harder-Narasimhan type
\tau
;

•

if
\{\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}\}_{i=0}^{2}
is the Harder-Narasimhan filtration, then
\mathcal{E}_{i}:=\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}/\mathcal{E}_{\leq i-1}
is stable for
i=1,2
;

•

\mathcal{E}\not\cong\mathcal{E}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{E}_{2}
;

•

\dim\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{B}}(\mathcal{E}_{2},\mathcal{E}_{1})=\delta
.

Theorem 2.11

(
[
11
]
Theorem 7.1.3)
.

Let
B
be a connected projective scheme over
\Bbbk
with an ample line bundle
\mathcal{L}
. Let
\tau
be a Harder-Narasimhan type of length 2 and let
\delta\in\mathbb{N}
. Then there exists a quasi-projective moduli space
M_{\tau,\delta}
of
(\tau,\delta)
-stable sheaves on
B
, in the sense that closed points in
M_{\tau,\delta}
are exactly isomorphism classes of
(\tau,\delta)
-stable sheaves.

Remark 2.12
.

The moduli spaces
M_{\tau,\delta}
above are reduced schemes since they were constructed by the classic U-hat theorem (cf. Theorem 2.20,
[
2
]
), which only produces reduced quotient schemes.

The non-reductive GIT problem considered in
[
11
]
to prove Theorem
2.11
was for the linearisation

\mathrm{P}_{\tau}\curvearrowright\big(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}},\;\mathcal{M}\big|_{\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}}\big)

(37)

where
\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\subseteq\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
is the scheme theoretic closure of
Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}
in
\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
. In Appendix
A
, we study the boundary of
Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}\subseteq\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}
and Proposition
A.2
shows that when applying our main theorem in non-reductive GIT (Theorem
3.33
).

For each
\delta\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists a locally closed subset
Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}
(See equation (
187
) for its definition), whose closed points are
[(\mathcal{E},q)]
such that
\mathcal{E}
is
(\tau,\alpha)
-stable. Each of
Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}
has a quasi-projective universal geometric quotient by
\mathrm{P}_{\tau}

Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}\to Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}/\mathrm{P}_{\tau}.

(38)

Note that in
[
11
]
, only closed points of
Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}\subseteq Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}\subseteq\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}
were described, and scheme structures were implicitly the reduced ones. However by
[
3
]
Remark 7.8, even for rank 2 unstable bundles over curves, the moduli space need not be reduced.

One key fact peculiar to length two is the following, which fails for higher lengths.

Lemma 2.13

(
[
11
]
Corollary 7.2.6)
.

For any closed point
x\in Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{ss}}
, we have

\dim\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathrm{U}_{\tau}}(x)=\dim\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathrm{U}_{\tau}}(p_{\tau}(x))

(39)

where
p_{\tau}:Y_{\tau}\to\prod_{i=1}^{2}\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P_{i}(t),\mathcal{L}}
is the retraction and
\mathrm{U}_{\tau}\subseteq\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
is the unipotent radical.

We will consider the non-reduced version of the GIT problem above. This requires:

•

an extension of non-reductive GIT developed in
[
2
]
,
[
1
]
to linear actions on non-reduced schemes;

•

a scheme theoretic description of
Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}
.

We will address these in the following two sections respectively.

3
Non-reductive GIT for graded additive groups

Let
U
be an additive group, i.e.
U\cong(\mathbb{G}_{a})^{r}
for
r:=\dim U
. Let
\lambda:\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\mathrm{Aut}(U)
be a 1-parameter subgroup acting on
U
by conjugation such that the induced representation of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
on
\mathfrak{u}:=\mathrm{Lie}(U)
has a unique positive weight. Let
\hat{U}:=U\rtimes_{\lambda}\mathbb{G}_{m}
be the semi-direct product of
U
and the 1PS
\lambda
. We will consider group actions by
\hat{U}
.

3.1
Some definitions for group actions

All schemes are Noetherian over
\Bbbk
. Let
G
be an affine algebraic group. Let
X
be a scheme acted on by
G
via the action morphism
\sigma:G\times X\to X
. Let
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
be the dual of the Lie algebra of
G
.

3.1.1
Infinitesimal stabilisers of scheme-valued points

Definition 3.1
.

Let
\sigma:G\times X\to X
be an action. Let
t:T\to X
be a morphism in
\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
. The
stabiliser
of the
T
-point
t
, denoted by
\mathrm{Stab}_{G}(t)
or
\mathrm{Stab}_{G}(T)
, is defined as the following fibre product

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Stab}_{G}(t)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G\times T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times T.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(t,1_{T})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\sigma\circ(\mathrm{pr}_{0},t\circ\mathrm{pr}_{1}),\;\mathrm{pr}_{1}\big)}

(40)

The
infinitesimal stabiliser
of the
T
-point
t
, denoted by
\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathfrak{g}}(t)
or
\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathfrak{g}}(T)
, is defined as the normal sheaf of the closed immersion
e^{\prime\prime}:T\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Stab}_{G}(t)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Stab}_{G}(t)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G\times T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times T}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(t,1_{T})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\sigma\circ(\mathrm{pr}_{0},t\circ\mathrm{pr}_{1}),\;\mathrm{pr}_{1}\big)}

(41)

induced by the closed immersion
e^{\prime}:T\hookrightarrow G\times T
at the identity
e:\mathrm{Spec}(\Bbbk)\hookrightarrow G

\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathfrak{g}}(t):=\mathcal{N}_{e^{\prime\prime}}\equiv\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{O}_{T}}\big(\mathcal{C}_{T/\mathrm{Stab}_{G}(t)},\mathcal{O}_{T}\big).

(42)

Consider the following diagram for a morphism
t:T\to X
in
\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Stab}_{G}(t)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G\times T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times T}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\sigma\circ(\mathrm{pr_{G}},t\circ\mathrm{pr}_{T}),\mathrm{pr}_{T}\big)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(t,1_{T})}

(43)

where horizontal morphisms are all immersions. By
Lemma 01R3
, there is a chain of conormal sheaves on
T

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{(t,1_{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{T/\mathrm{Stab}_{G}(t)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}

(44)

which is exact since
\mathrm{Stab}_{G}(t)\to G\times T
is an immersion and
\mathrm{Stab}_{G}(t)\cong(G\times T)\times_{X\times T}T
. The following diagram is Cartesian

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(t,1_{T})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{X}\times t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta}

(45)

where
\delta:X\to X\times X
is the diagonal morphism, and horizontal morphisms are immersions. By
Lemma 01R4
, there is a surjective morphism of conormal sheaves

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t^{*}\Omega_{X/\Bbbk}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{(t,1_{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}

(46)

Then the following sequence is exact

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t^{*}\Omega_{X/\Bbbk}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{T/\mathrm{Stab}_{G}(t)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}

(47)

Definition 3.2
.

Let
\sigma:G\times X\to X
be an action. Choose
t:T\to X
to be the identity morphism
1_{X}:X\to X
. We call
\phi:\Omega_{X/\Bbbk}\to\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{X}
constructed above the
infinitesimal action of
\mathfrak{g}
on
X
.

Lemma 3.3
.

Let
f:X_{1}\to X_{2}
be an immersion of schemes. Let
G
act on
X_{1},X_{2}
such that
f
is
G
-invariant. Let
\phi_{i}:\Omega_{X_{i}/\Bbbk}\to\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{X_{i}}
be the infinitesimal action of
\mathfrak{g}
on
X_{i}
for
i=1,2
. Then we have
\mathrm{coker}(\phi_{1})\cong f^{*}\mathrm{coker}(\phi_{2})
fitting into the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}\Omega_{X_{2}/\Bbbk}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}f^{*}\mathcal{O}_{X_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}\mathrm{coker}(\phi_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{X_{1}/\Bbbk}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{coker}(\phi_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}\phi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{1}}

(48)

Proof.

The morphism
f
is
G
-invariant, so the left square commutes. The left square induces a morphism
f^{*}\mathrm{coker}(\phi_{2})\to\mathrm{coker}(\phi_{1})
. Since
f
is an immersion, we have that
f^{*}\Omega_{X_{2}/\Bbbk}\to\Omega_{X_{1}/\Bbbk}
is surjective. Therefore
f^{*}\mathrm{coker}(\phi_{2})\to\mathrm{coker}(\phi_{1})
is an isomorphism by the Five lemma.
∎

Lemma 3.4
.

Let
G
act on
X
. In the category
\mathrm{Sch}/X
, we have

(1)

The formation of
\mathrm{Stab}_{G}(t)
commutes with arbitrary base change, in the sense that if
T^{\prime}\to T
is a morphism in
\mathrm{Sch}/X
between

and

, then the following are Cartesian

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

(49)

(2)

The formation of
\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathfrak{g}}(t)
commutes with flat base change, in the sense that if
T^{\prime}\to T
is a flat morphism in
\mathrm{Sch}/X
between
t^{\prime}:T^{\prime}\to X
and
t:T\to X
, then

(T^{\prime}\to T)^{*}\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathfrak{g}}(t)\cong\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathfrak{g}}(t^{\prime}).

(50)

(3)

The formation of sequence (
47
) commutes with arbitrary base change in the sense that if
T^{\prime}\to T
is a morphism in
\mathrm{Sch}/X
between
t^{\prime}:T^{\prime}\to X
and
t:T\to X
, then the sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t^{*}\Omega_{X/\Bbbk}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{T/\mathrm{Stab}_{G}(t)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

(51)

pulls back along
T^{\prime}\to T
to the sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(t^{\prime})^{*}\Omega_{X/\Bbbk}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{T^{\prime}/\mathrm{Stab}_{G}(t^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}

(52)

Therefore sequence (
47
) is the cokernel sequence of
t^{*}\phi
for
\phi:\Omega_{X/\Bbbk}\to\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{X}
.

Proof.

It is routine to check
(1)
and
(3)
. Note that in
(2)
we need flatness since
\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathfrak{g}}(t)=\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{O}_{T}}\big(\mathcal{C}_{T/\mathrm{Stab}_{G}(t)},\mathcal{O}_{T}\big)
and the formation of dual sheaves commutes with flat base change by
Lemma 0C6I
.
∎

Lemma 3.5
.

Let
G
act on
X
. Let
\phi:\Omega_{X/\Bbbk}\to\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{X}
be the infinitesimal action. Let
t:T\to X
be a
T
-point. Let
\delta\in\mathbb{N}
. The following are equivalent:

(1)

t^{*}\mathrm{coker}(\phi)
is locally free of rank
\delta
on
T
;

(2)

\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathfrak{g}}(t^{\prime})
is locally free of rank
\delta
on
T^{\prime}
for all
T^{\prime}\to T
, where
t^{\prime}
is the composition
t^{\prime}:T^{\prime}\to T\xrightarrow{t}X
;

(3)

the same in
(2)
with
T^{\prime}\to T
ranging over all closed subschemes of
T
.

Proof.

Assume
(1)
. Then
(t^{\prime})^{*}\mathrm{coker}(\phi)
is locally free of rank
\delta
. Recall
\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathfrak{g}}(t^{\prime})=\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{O}_{T^{\prime}}}\big((t^{\prime})^{*}\mathrm{coker}(\phi),\mathcal{O}_{T^{\prime}}\big)
, which is locally free of rank
\delta
on
T^{\prime}
. This proves
(1)\implies(2)
.

Obviously
(2)\implies(3)
.

Assume
(3)
. For
k\in\mathbb{N}
, let
Z_{k}\subseteq T
denote the closed subscheme associated to the
k
th Fitting ideal of
t^{*}\mathrm{coker}(\phi)
. For
S_{k}:=Z_{k-1}\setminus Z_{k}
, we have
\mathrm{coker}(\phi)|_{S_{k}}:=(S_{k}\to T)^{*}t^{*}\mathrm{coker}(\phi)
is locally free of rank
k
by
Lemma 05P8
. Then
\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathfrak{g}}(S_{k})=\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{O}_{S_{k}}}\big(\mathrm{coker}(\phi)|_{S_{k}},\mathcal{O}_{S_{k}}\big)
is locally free of rank
k
on
S_{k}
. Condition
(3)
implies that
\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathfrak{g}}(Z_{k-1})
is locally free of rank
\delta
on
Z_{k-1}
. Since
S_{k}\to Z_{k-1}
is an open immersion, we have
\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathfrak{g}}(Z_{k-1})|_{S_{k}}\cong\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathfrak{g}}(S_{k})
by Lemma
3.4
. Therefore if
k\neq\delta
, then
S_{k}=\emptyset
. Then
Z_{\delta-1}=T
and
Z_{\delta}=\emptyset
.

Condition
(3)
implies that
\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathfrak{g}}(T)
is locally free of rank
\delta
on
T
.
Lemma 05P8
implies that
t^{*}\mathrm{coker}(\phi)
is locally generated by
\leq\delta
sections. Let
W\subseteq T
be an affine open subset such that
\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathfrak{g}}(T)|_{W}
is free of rank
\delta
and such that there exists an exact sequence of
\mathcal{O}_{W}
-modules for some
N\in\mathbb{N}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{W}^{\oplus N}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{W}^{\oplus\delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{coker}(\phi)|_{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\zeta}

(53)

where
\mathrm{coker}(\phi)|_{W}:=t^{*}\mathrm{coker}(\phi)|_{W}
. The sequence of dual
\mathcal{O}_{W}
-modules is exact

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathfrak{g}}(W)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{W}^{\oplus\delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{W}^{\oplus N}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\zeta^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta^{*}}

(54)

We have that
\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathfrak{g}}(W)
is free of rank
\delta
since
\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathfrak{g}}(W)\cong\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathfrak{g}}(T)|_{W}
by Lemma
3.4
. It is well known that
\det(\zeta^{*})\in\mathcal{O}_{W}(W)
is a non-zerodivisor when
\zeta^{*}
is an injective map of finite free modules of the same rank
\delta
. Moreover,
\mathrm{coker}(\zeta^{*})
is annihilated by
\det(\zeta^{*})
and
\mathrm{coker}(\zeta^{*})\cong\mathrm{im}(\eta^{*})\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{W}^{\oplus N}
is contained in a torsion free module. Therefore
\mathrm{coker}(\zeta^{*})\cong\mathrm{im}(\eta^{*})=0
, i.e.
\eta=0
, which implies that
\zeta:\mathcal{O}_{W}^{\oplus\delta}\to\mathrm{coker}(\phi)|_{W}
is an isomorphism. This proves
(1)
.
∎

For the infinitesimal action
\phi:\Omega_{X/\Bbbk}\to\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{X}
, let
\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(\phi)\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{X}
denote the
k
th Fitting ideal of
\mathrm{coker}(\phi)
.

Lemma 3.6
.

Let
G
act on
X
. Let
\phi:\Omega_{X/\Bbbk}\to\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{X}
be the infinitesimal action. Let
\mathbb{K}
be a field. Let
x:\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{K})\to X
be a
\mathbb{K}
-point. Let
k\in\mathbb{Z}
. The following are equivalent:

(1)

\dim_{\mathbb{K}}\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathfrak{g}}(x)>k
;

(2)

\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(\phi)\subseteq\ker(x^{\sharp})
, where
x^{\sharp}:\mathcal{O}_{X}\to x_{*}(\mathbb{K})
is the morphism of sheaves of rings associated to
x
.

Proof.

Let
r:=\dim_{\Bbbk}\mathfrak{g}
. When
k\geq r
or
k\leq-1
, both
(1)
and
(2)
are false or both are true. We only need to consider when
0\leq k<r
.

Let
\mathrm{Spec}(A)\subseteq X
be an open neighbourhood of
x\in X
. Let
x_{A}:\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{K})\to\mathrm{Spec}(A)
be the morphism when
x
is viewed as a
\mathbb{K}
-point of
\mathrm{Spec}(A)
. On
\mathrm{Spec}(A)
, the infinitesimal action
\phi:\Omega_{X/\Bbbk}\to\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{X}
corresponds to a map of
A
-modules

\phi_{A}:\Omega_{A/\Bbbk}\to\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}A.

(55)

Choose a surjective map

\alpha:A^{\oplus J}\to\Omega_{A/\Bbbk}

(56)

for an index set
J
. Choose a basis
(u_{1},\cdots,u_{r})
of
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
. Let
M=(m_{ij})_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq r\\
j\in J\end{subarray}}
be the matrix with entries
m_{ij}\in A
such that

(\phi_{A}\circ\alpha)(e_{j})=\sum_{i=1}^{r}u_{i}\otimes m_{ij},\quad\textrm{for all }j\in J.

(57)

Then the ideal
\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(\phi_{A})\subseteq A
is generated by
(r-k)\times(r-k)
-minors of
M
.

Let
x_{A}^{\sharp}:A\to\mathbb{K}
be the ring map corresponding to the point
x_{A}:\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{K})\to\mathrm{Spec}(A)
. The base change of
\phi_{A}\circ\alpha
along
x_{A}^{\sharp}:A\to\mathbb{K}
is

x_{A}^{*}(\phi_{A}\circ\alpha):\mathbb{K}^{\oplus J}\to\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathbb{K}

(58)

where
\Bbbk\to\mathbb{K}
corresponds to
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{K})\xrightarrow{x}X\to\mathrm{Spec}(\Bbbk)
. Then the linear map
x_{A}^{*}(\phi_{A}\circ\alpha)
is represented by the matrix
x_{A}^{\sharp}(M):=\big(x_{A}^{\sharp}(m_{ij})\big)
with entries in
\mathbb{K}
. Note that
\xi\in\mathfrak{g}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathbb{K}
stabilises
x
if and only if
\mathrm{im}\big(x_{A}^{*}(\phi_{A}\circ\alpha)\big)\subseteq\xi^{\perp}
.

We have

\begin{split}(1)\quad\iff&\quad\textrm{there exist independent }\xi_{0},\cdots,\xi_{k}\in\mathfrak{g}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathbb{K}\textrm{ stabilising }x\\
\iff&\quad\textrm{there exist independent }\xi_{0},\cdots,\xi_{k}\in\mathfrak{g}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathbb{K}\\
&\qquad\textrm{such that }\mathrm{im}\big(x_{A}^{*}(\phi_{A}\circ\alpha)\big)\subseteq(\xi_{0},\cdots,\xi_{k})^{\perp}\\
\iff&\quad\dim_{\mathbb{K}}\mathrm{im}\big(x_{A}^{*}(\phi_{A}\circ\alpha)\big)<r-k\\
\iff&\quad\mathrm{rank}(x_{A}^{\sharp}M)<r-k\\
\iff&\quad\textrm{any }(r-k)\times(r-k)\textrm{-minor of }x_{A}^{\sharp}M\textrm{ vanishes}\\
\iff&\quad\textrm{any }(r-k)\times(r-k)\textrm{-minor of }M\textrm{ is in }\ker(x_{A}^{\sharp})\\
\iff&\quad\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(\phi_{A})\subseteq\ker(x_{A}^{\sharp})\\
\iff&\quad(2)\end{split}

(59)

∎

Remark 3.7
.

If
\mathrm{coker}(\phi)
is locally free of rank
k
on
X
, then
\mathrm{Fit}_{k-1}(\phi)=0
and
\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(\phi)=\mathcal{O}_{X}
. By Lemma
3.6
, this implies
\dim_{\mathbb{K}}\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathfrak{g}}(x)=k
for any field
\mathbb{K}
and
\mathbb{K}
-point
x\in X
. Conversely, if
\dim_{\mathbb{K}}\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathfrak{g}}(x)=k
for any
\mathbb{K}
and
\mathbb{K}
-point
x\in X
, then we only have
\mathrm{Fit}_{k-1}(\phi)\subseteq\mathrm{nil}(X)
and
\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(\phi)=\mathcal{O}_{X}
, where
\mathrm{nil}(X)\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{X}
is the nilpotent radical.

3.1.2
Scheme-valued infinitesimal transformations

Let
T
be a scheme over
\Bbbk
. Recall that a
T
-valued point of
X
is a morphism
t:T\to X
. We will define
T
-valued infinitesimal transformations in
G
and when a
T
-valued infinitesimal transformation stabilises a
T
-valued point.

Let
\Bbbk[\epsilon]:=\Bbbk[t]/t^{2}
be the algebra of dual numbers. For
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
, let
T[\epsilon]:=T\times_{\Bbbk}\mathrm{Spec}(\Bbbk[\epsilon])
. The underlying topological spaces of
T
and
T[\epsilon]
are the same. We can think of
T[\epsilon]
as a scheme on the topological space
|T|
with the structure sheaf

\mathcal{O}_{T[\epsilon]}:=\mathcal{O}_{T}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\Bbbk[\epsilon]\cong\mathcal{O}_{T}\oplus\epsilon\mathcal{O}_{T}.

(60)

There is a natural closed immersion
T\hookrightarrow T[\epsilon]
, corresponding to
\mathcal{O}_{T[\epsilon]}\xrightarrow{\epsilon=0}\mathcal{O}_{T}
.

Definition 3.8
.

Let
G
be an affine algebraic group over
\Bbbk
. A
T
-valued infinitesimal transformation in
G
is a morphism
\rho:T[\epsilon]\to G
satisfying the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T[\epsilon]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Spec}(\Bbbk)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}

(61)

Definition 3.9
.

Let
G
act on
X
. Let
t:T\to X
be a
T
-valued point of
X
, and let
\rho:T[\epsilon]\to G
be a
T
-valued infinitesimal transformation in
G
. We say that
\rho

stabilises

t
if the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T[\epsilon]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G\times X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\rho,t\circ\mathrm{pr})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}

(62)

Remark 3.10
.

If we take
T=\mathrm{Spec}(\Bbbk)
, then
T
-valued points of
X
are
\Bbbk
-points, and
T
-valued infinitesimal transformations in
G
are vectors in the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
. For a
T
-valued point
x\in X
and a
T
-valued infinitesimal transformation
\xi\in\mathfrak{g}
, the definition of when
\xi
stabilises
x
in Definition
3.9
is equivalent to the requirement that the pullback of
\xi:\Omega_{X/\Bbbk}\to\mathcal{O}_{X}
along
x:\mathrm{Spec}(\Bbbk)\hookrightarrow X
is zero.

Lemma 3.11
.

Consider the following diagram in
\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
such that
i:Z\to Y
is an immersion and
j:T\hookrightarrow T[\epsilon]
is the closed immersion via
\epsilon=0

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T[\epsilon]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}

(63)

Then
\rho:T[\epsilon]\to Y
factors through
i:Z\to Y
if and only if the morphism
g^{*}\mathcal{C}_{Z/Y}\to\epsilon\mathcal{O}_{T}
in
Lemma 01R3
is zero, where
\epsilon\mathcal{O}_{T}\cong\mathcal{C}_{T/T[\epsilon]}
.

Proof.

Assume without loss of generality that
T,Z,Y
are affine schemes, and
i:Z\hookrightarrow Y
is a closed immersion. Let
T=\mathrm{Spec}(A)
,
Y=\mathrm{Spec}(R)
and
Z=\mathrm{Spec}(R/I)
. Let
\varphi:R/I\to A
correspond to
g:T\to Z
, and let
\psi:R\to A[\epsilon]
corresponds to
\rho:T[\epsilon]\to Y
.

Since
\rho\circ j=i\circ g
, the right square in the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R/I}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A[\epsilon]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi|_{I}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}

(64)

and thus
\psi|_{I}:I\to\epsilon A
exists so that the left square commutes.

The morphism
g^{*}\mathcal{C}_{Z/Y}\to\epsilon\mathcal{O}_{T}
corresponds to the following

\eta:I/I^{2}\otimes_{R/I,\varphi}A\to\epsilon A,\quad\overline{h}\otimes 1\mapsto\psi|_{I}(h).

(65)

Then we have
\eta=0
if and only if
\psi(I)=0
.

The morphism
\rho:T[\epsilon]\to Y
factors as
T[\epsilon]\to Z\to Y
if and only if the ring map
\psi:R\to A[\epsilon]
factors as
R\to R/I\to A[\epsilon]
, which is equivalent to
\psi(I)=0
, and thus equivalent to
\eta=0
. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 3.12
.

Let
\sigma:G\times X\to X
be an action. Let
t:T\to X
be a
T
-valued point. Then we have the following commutative diagram of
\Gamma(T,\mathcal{O}_{T})
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{\rho:\rho\textrm{ is a }T\textrm{-valued infinitesimal transformation}\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma(T,\mathfrak{g}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Big\{\begin{matrix}\rho:\;\rho\textrm{ is a }T\textrm{-valued infinitesimal transformation}\\
\textrm{and }\rho\textrm{ stabilises }t\end{matrix}\Big\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma\big(T,\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathfrak{g}}(t)\big)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}

(66)

where both horizontal maps are bijections and the top map sends
\rho:T[\epsilon]\to G
to the morphism of conormal sheaves
C_{\rho}:\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}\to\epsilon\mathcal{O}_{T}
by applying
Lemma 01R3
to the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T[\epsilon]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G\times T.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(e,1_{T})}

(67)

Moreover, the maps are natural for
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/X
.

Proof.

It is easy to see the maps are
\Gamma(T,\mathcal{O}_{T})
-linear and are natural for
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/X
.

We construct the inverse of the top map. Let
\eta\in\Gamma(T,\mathfrak{g}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T})
. We can think
\eta
as a map of
\Gamma(T,\mathcal{O}_{T})
-modules

\eta:\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\Gamma(T,\mathcal{O}_{T})\to\Gamma(T,\mathcal{O}_{T}).

(68)

Consider the ring map
\rho^{*}:\mathcal{O}(G)\to\Gamma(T,\mathcal{O}_{T})[\epsilon]
such that for
h\in\mathcal{O}(G)

\rho^{*}(h)=e^{*}(h)\oplus\epsilon\;\eta\big(\overline{h-e^{*}(h)}\otimes 1\big)\in\Gamma(T,\mathcal{O}_{T})\oplus\epsilon\Gamma(T,\mathcal{O}_{T})\cong\Gamma(T[\epsilon],\mathcal{O}_{T[\epsilon]})

(69)

where
h-e^{*}(h)\in\mathfrak{m}_{e}:=\ker(e^{*})
represents
\overline{h-e^{*}(h)}\in\mathfrak{m}_{e}/\mathfrak{m}_{e}^{2}\cong\mathfrak{g}^{*}
. The ring map
\rho^{*}
corresponds to a morphism
\rho:T[\epsilon]\to G
. It is easy to check that the map
\eta\mapsto\rho
provides the required inverse. This establishes the top bijection.

Let
\rho:T[\epsilon]\to G
be a
T
-valued infinitesimal transformation. The following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T[\epsilon]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G\times X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Spec}(\Bbbk)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\rho,t\circ\mathrm{pr}_{T})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\sigma,\mathrm{pr}_{X})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta}

(70)

where
\gamma:X\to G\times X
is the pullback of
e:\mathrm{Spec}(\Bbbk)\hookrightarrow G
in the bottom left parallelogram,
\delta:X\to X\times X
is the diagonal morphism,
\mathrm{pr}_{T}:T[\epsilon]\to T
is the projection and
\mathrm{pr}_{X}:G\times X\to X
is the projection.

Apply
Lemma 01R3
to the top square and the composition of the top square and bottom left parallelogram to obtain two morphisms between conormal sheaves

\begin{split}\mathcal{C}_{(\rho,t\circ\mathrm{pr}_{T})}:&\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}\to\epsilon\mathcal{O}_{T}\\
\mathcal{C}_{\rho}:&\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}\to\epsilon\mathcal{O}_{T}.\end{split}

(71)

such that
\mathcal{C}_{\rho}=\mathcal{C}_{(\rho,t\circ\mathrm{pr}_{T})}\circ t^{*}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pr}_{G}}
, where
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pr}_{G}}:\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{X}\to\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{X}
is the morphism of conormal sheaves associated to the bottom right parallelogram. Since the bottom left parallelogram is Cartesian with southwest arrows flat, by
Lemma 01R4
, we have
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pr}_{G}}=1_{\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{X}}
. Then
\mathcal{C}_{\rho}=\mathcal{C}_{(\rho,t\circ\mathrm{pr}_{T})}
.

We have that
\rho
stabilises
t:T\to X
if and only if the dashed arrow below exists in the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T[\epsilon]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\sigma\circ(\rho,t\circ\mathrm{pr}_{T}),\;t\circ\mathrm{pr}_{T}\big)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta}

(72)

where the square (
72
) is the composition of the top and bottom right parallelogram in diagram (
70
). Note that
\phi:\Omega_{X/\Bbbk}\to\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{X}
is the morphism of conormal sheaves associated to the bottom right parallelogram. By Lemma
3.11
, we have that the dashed arrow above exists if and only if
t^{*}\Omega_{X/\Bbbk}\to\epsilon\mathcal{O}_{T}
is zero, i.e. the following composition is zero

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t^{*}\Omega_{X/\Bbbk}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon\mathcal{O}_{T}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t^{*}\phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{(\rho,t\circ\mathrm{pr}_{T})}}

(73)

Therefore
\rho
stabilises
t
if and only if
\mathcal{C}_{\rho}\circ t^{*}\phi=\mathcal{C}_{(\rho,t\circ\mathrm{pr}_{T})}\circ t^{*}\phi=0
. Recall that
\rho\mapsto\mathcal{C}_{\rho}
defines a bijection

\{\rho:\rho\textrm{ is a }T\textrm{-valued infinitesimal transformation}\}\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{T}}(\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T},\epsilon\mathcal{O}_{T}).

(74)

Then the images of those
\rho
which stabilise
t
constitute the kernel of

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{T}}(\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T},\mathcal{O}_{T})\to\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{T}}(t^{*}\Omega_{X/\Bbbk},\mathcal{O}_{T})

(75)

which is
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{T}}(t^{*}\mathrm{coker}(\phi),\mathcal{O}_{T})\cong\Gamma\big(T,\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathfrak{g}}(t)\big)
. This establishes the bottom bijection in the lemma.
∎

3.2
Some conditions for the linearisation

From now on, all schemes are of finite type over
\Bbbk
. Let
X
be a projective scheme and let
L
be an ample line bundle on
X
. Let
\hat{U}
act on
X
linearly with respect to
L
. In
[
7
]
, conditions called
Upstairs Unipotent Stabiliser
(
[
7
]
Assumption 4.40) and
Weak Upstairs Unipotent Stabiliser
(
[
7
]
Assumption 4.42) were considered for the linearisation
\hat{U}\curvearrowright(X,L)
, when
X
is reduced. We will generalise these conditions for potentially non-reduced
X
. Firstly, a non-emptiness condition is required, which always holds when
X
is reduced but can fail in non-reduced cases.

3.2.1
A non-emptiness condition

We will define an open subscheme
X^{0}_{\min}\subseteq X
and a closed subscheme
Z_{\min}\hookrightarrow X
. We need a condition to ensure that
X^{0}_{\min}\neq\emptyset
and
Z_{\min}\neq\emptyset
.

We first consider when
\hat{U}
acts on projective spaces. Let
V
be a finite dimensional representation of
\hat{U}
. For any
w\in\mathbb{Z}
denote

V_{\lambda=w}:=\{v\in V:\lambda(t).v=t^{w}v,\;\textrm{for all closed point }t\in\mathbb{G}_{m}\}.

(76)

We call
w\in\mathbb{Z}
a
weight
if
V_{\lambda=w}\neq 0
, and we call
V_{\lambda=w}\subseteq V
the weight subspace of the weight
w
with respect to
\lambda:\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\hat{U}
. Let
V_{\lambda=\min}\subseteq V
denote the weight subspace whose weight is the minimum. If
V\neq 0
, then
V_{\min}\neq 0
. Let
w_{\min}
denote the minimal weight. For
v\in V
, decompose according to
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-weights

v=v_{\lambda=\min}+v_{\lambda>\min},\quad v_{\lambda=\min}\in V_{\lambda=\min},\;v_{\lambda>\min}\in\bigoplus_{w>w_{\min}}V_{\lambda=w}.

(77)

Define locally closed subschemes
\mathbb{P}(V)^{0}_{\min}\subseteq\mathbb{P}(V)
and
Z_{\min}\big(\mathbb{P}(V)\big)\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}(V)
whose sets of closed points are

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}(V)^{0}_{\min}\overset{\mathrm{cl}}{=\!=}&\big\{[v]\in\mathbb{P}(V):v_{\lambda=\min}\neq 0\big\}\\
Z_{\min}\big(\mathbb{P}(V)\big)\overset{\mathrm{cl}}{=\!=}&\big\{[v]\in\mathbb{P}(V):v_{\lambda>\min}=0\big\}\end{split}

(78)

where
\overset{\mathrm{cl}}{=\!=}
means they have the same closed points. It is easy to see that
\mathbb{P}(V)^{0}_{\min}
is an open subscheme and
Z_{\min}\big(\mathbb{P}(V)\big)
is a closed subscheme, and
Z_{\min}\big(\mathbb{P}(V)\big)\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}(V)^{0}_{\min}
.

The non-emptiness condition for projective spaces is vacuous, since if
\mathbb{P}(V)\neq\emptyset
, then
\mathbb{P}(V)^{0}_{\min}\neq\emptyset
and
Z_{\min}\big(\mathbb{P}(V)\big)\neq\emptyset
. It is vacuous if
X
is reduced.

Then we consider the linearisation
\hat{U}\curvearrowright(X,L)
. There exists
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
such that
H^{0}(X,L^{m})
generates
\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbb{N}}H^{0}(X,L^{mr})
. We can define
\mathbb{P}\big(H^{0}(X,L^{m})^{*}\big)^{0}_{\min}
and
Z_{\min}\big(\mathbb{P}\big(H^{0}(X,L^{m})^{*}\big)\big)
as above.

Lemma 3.13
.

For the linearisation
\hat{U}\curvearrowright(X,L)
, if
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
is such that
H^{0}(X,L^{m})
generates
\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbb{N}}H^{0}(X,L^{mr})
and

X\cap\mathbb{P}\big(H^{0}(X,L^{m})^{*}\big)^{0}_{\min}\neq\emptyset

(79)

where
X\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}\big(H^{0}(X,L^{m})^{*}\big)
is a closed immersion, then for all
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
we have

X\cap\mathbb{P}\big(H^{0}(X,L^{m})^{*}\big)^{0}_{\min}=X\cap\mathbb{P}\big(H^{0}(X,L^{mn})^{*}\big)^{0}_{\min}.

(80)

Proof.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
be as in the lemma. The linear system
H^{0}(X,L^{mn})
induces a closed immersion
X\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}\big(H^{0}(X,L^{mn})^{*}\big)
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
.

The open subscheme
X\cap\mathbb{P}\big(H^{0}(X,L^{m})^{*}\big)^{0}_{\min}
is the non-vanishing locus of
H^{0}(X,L^{m})_{\lambda=\max}
, where
H^{0}(X,L^{m})_{\lambda=\max}
is the weight subspace of
H^{0}(X,L^{m})
with respect to
\lambda
whose weight is the maximum. Similarly, the open subscheme
X\cap\mathbb{P}\big(H^{0}(X,L^{mn})^{*}\big)^{0}_{\min}
is the non-vanishing locus of
H^{0}(X,L^{mn})_{\lambda=\max}
.

Let
w_{\max}
denote the maximal weight of
\lambda
on
H^{0}(X,L^{m})
. The condition that
X\cap\mathbb{P}\big(H^{0}(X,L^{m})^{*}\big)^{0}_{\min}\neq\emptyset
implies that there exists
f\in H^{0}(X,L^{m})_{\lambda=\max}
, which is not nilpotent in
\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}H^{0}(X,L^{r})
. Therefore
f^{n}\in H^{0}(X,L^{mn})\setminus\{0\}
. Since
H^{0}(X,L^{m})
generates
\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}H^{0}(X,L^{mr})
, we have that the maximal weight on
H^{0}(X,L^{mn})
is the weight of
f^{n}
, i.e.
nw_{\max}
. We have the following surjective map

\mathrm{Sym}^{n}H^{0}(X,L^{m})_{\lambda=\max}\to H^{0}(X,L^{mn})_{\lambda=\max}.

(81)

Therefore the non-vanishing locus of
H^{0}(X,L^{mn})_{\lambda=\max}
and the non-vanishing locus of
H^{0}(X,L^{m})_{\lambda=\max}
coincide. This proves the lemma.
∎

Condition 3.14
.

For the linearisation
\hat{U}\curvearrowright(X,L)
, assume that

X\cap\mathbb{P}\big(H^{0}(X,L^{m})^{*}\big)^{0}_{\min}\neq\emptyset

(82)

for sufficiently divisible
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
. Denote

X^{0}_{\min}:=X\cap\mathbb{P}\big(H^{0}(X,L^{m})^{*}\big).

(83)

Remark 3.15
.

Lemma
3.13
implies that
X^{0}_{\min}
does not depend on
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
when
m
is sufficiently divisible and when Condition
3.14
holds.

The opposite of Condition
3.14
is the condition that
H^{0}(X,L^{m})_{\lambda=\max}
is nilpotent for all sufficiently divisible
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
. When
X
is non-empty and reduced, then Condition
3.14
always holds.

With Condition
3.14
, we also have that
X^{0}_{\min}
is the non-vanishing locus of
\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}H^{0}(X,L^{r})_{\lambda=\max}
. Define the
fixed point subscheme

Z_{\min}\hookrightarrow X^{0}_{\min}

Z_{\min}:=\big(X^{0}_{\min}\big)^{\lambda}.

(84)

Then
Z_{\min}=\mathrm{Proj}\Big(\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}H^{0}(X,L^{r})_{\lambda=\max}\Big)
.

3.2.2
Upstairs Unipotent Stabiliser conditions

Let
\phi:\Omega_{X/\Bbbk}\to\mathfrak{u}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{X}
be the infinitesimal action of
\mathfrak{u}
on
X
(Definition
3.2
), and let
\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(\phi)\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{X}
denote the
k
th Fitting ideal of
\mathrm{coker}(\phi)
. The following conditions generalise
[
6
]
Assumption 4.40 and Assumption 4.42.

Condition 3.16

(Upstairs Unipotent Stabiliser)
.

For the linearisation
\hat{U}\curvearrowright(X,L)
satisfying Condition
3.14
, we say the
upstairs unipotent stabiliser condition
(Condition UU) holds if
\mathrm{coker}(\phi)
is locally free of constant rank on
X^{0}_{\min}
.

Remark 3.17
.

By Lemma
3.6
, Condition
UU
implies that
\dim\mathrm{Stab}_{U}(x)
is constant for
\Bbbk
-points
x\in X^{0}_{\min}
. When
X^{0}_{\min}
is reduced, the converse is true.

Condition 3.18

(Weak Upstairs Unipotent Stabiliser)
.

For the linearisation
\hat{U}\curvearrowright(X,L)
satisfying Condition
3.14
,
we say the
weak upstairs unipotent stabiliser condition
(Condition WUU) holds if
\mathrm{coker}(\phi)
is locally free of rank
k
on an open subscheme which meets
Z_{\min}
, where
k:=\min\{i\in\mathbb{N}:\mathrm{Fit}_{i}(\phi)\neq 0\}
.

3.3
Theorem with Condition
UU

When the linearisation
\hat{U}\curvearrowright(X,L)
satisfies Condition
UU
, the open subscheme
X^{0}_{\min}
admits a geometric quotient by
U
, and the open subscheme
X^{0}_{\min}\setminus UZ_{\min}
admits a projective geometric quotient by
\hat{U}
. We will prove these statements.

Let
r:=\dim U
. Without loss of generality assume
r>0
. Let
\phi:\Omega_{X/\Bbbk}\to\mathfrak{u}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{X}
be the infinitesimal action of
\mathfrak{u}
. Condition
UU
implies that
X^{0}_{\min}\neq\emptyset
.

3.3.1
Reduction to affine case

Condition
UU
assumes that
\mathrm{coker}(\phi)
is locally free of rank
k
on
X^{0}_{\min}
for some integer
0\leq k\leq r
. The quotient
\mathfrak{u}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{X^{0}_{\min}}\to\mathrm{coker}(\phi)|_{X^{0}_{\min}}
corresponds to a morphism

\Theta:X^{0}_{\min}\to\mathrm{Grass}(\mathfrak{u}^{*},k)

(85)

where
\mathrm{Grass}(\mathfrak{u}^{*},k)
is the Grassmannian of
k
-dimensional quotients of
\mathfrak{u}^{*}
. Let
\iota:Z_{\min}\hookrightarrow X^{0}_{\min}
be the closed immersion.

There exists a finite family of
k
-dimensional vector subspaces
\big\{\mathfrak{s}_{i}^{*}\subseteq\mathfrak{u}^{*}\}_{i=1}^{s}
such that

\mathrm{Grass}(\mathfrak{u}^{*},k)=\bigcup_{i=1}^{s}\mathrm{Grass}(\mathfrak{u}^{*},k)_{\mathfrak{s}_{i}^{*}}

(86)

where
\mathrm{Grass}(\mathfrak{u}^{*},k)_{\mathfrak{s}_{i}^{*}}\subseteq\mathrm{Grass}(\mathfrak{u}^{*},k)
is the open subscheme whose closed points are quotients
\mathfrak{u}^{*}\to Q
such that the composition
\mathfrak{s}_{i}^{*}\subseteq\mathfrak{u}^{*}\to Q
is an isomorphism.

Open subsets
Z_{\min,f}\subseteq Z_{\min}
for
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
f\in H^{0}(X,L^{m})_{\lambda=\max}
form a basis of the Zariski topology of
Z_{\min}
. We can choose a sufficiently divisible
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
f_{1},\cdots,f_{t}\in H^{0}(X,L^{m})_{\lambda=\max}
such that
\{Z_{\min,f_{i}}\}_{i=1}^{t}
is a covering of
Z_{\min}
, and the covering is subordinate to the covering of
\mathrm{Grass}(\mathfrak{u}^{*},k)
, in the sense that for any
1\leq i\leq t
, there exists
1\leq j\leq s
such that

Z_{\min,f_{i}}\subseteq\Theta^{-1}\Big(\mathrm{Grass}(\mathfrak{u}^{*},k)_{\mathfrak{s}_{j}^{*}}\Big).

(87)

The morphism
\Theta
is
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant and
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant, so

\begin{split}&Z_{\min,f_{i}}\subseteq\Theta^{-1}\Big(\mathrm{Grass}(\mathfrak{u}^{*},k)_{\mathfrak{s}_{j}^{*}}\Big)\\
\textrm{if and only if }\;&X_{f_{i}}\subseteq\Theta^{-1}\Big(\mathrm{Grass}(\mathfrak{u}^{*},k)_{\mathfrak{s}_{j}^{*}}\Big).\end{split}

(88)

We have achieved the following:

•

There exists
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
f_{1},\cdots,f_{t}\in H^{0}(X,L^{m})_{\lambda=\max}
such that
X^{0}_{\min}
is covered by
\{X_{f_{i}}\}_{i=1}^{t}
;

•

For each
X_{f_{i}}
