, there exists a linear subspace
\mathfrak{s}_{i}^{*}\subseteq\mathfrak{u}^{*}
such that
\mathfrak{s}_{i}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{X_{f_{i}}}\cong\mathrm{coker}(\phi)|_{X_{f_{i}}}
as follows

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{s}_{i}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{X_{f_{i}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{X_{f_{i}}/\Bbbk}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{u}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{X_{f_{i}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{coker}(\phi)|_{X_{f_{i}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi|_{X_{f_{i}}}}

(89)

3.3.2
Affine theory with Condition
UU

We work on each of
X_{f_{i}}
and construct a universal geometric quotient by
U
.

Let
A
be a finitely generated commutative
\Bbbk
-algebra. Let
\hat{U}
act on
\mathrm{Spec}(A)
. Assume the following conditions for
\hat{U}\curvearrowright\mathrm{Spec}(A)
:

(1)

If
A_{\lambda=w}\subseteq A
denotes the weight subspace whose
\lambda
-weight is
w\in\mathbb{Z}
for the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-representation on
A
, then
A_{\lambda=w}=0
for
w>0
;

(2)

There exists a linear subspace
\mathfrak{s}^{*}\subseteq\mathfrak{u}^{*}
such that
\mathfrak{s}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}A\cong\mathrm{coker}(\phi_{A})
in the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{s}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{A/\Bbbk}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{u}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{coker}(\phi_{A})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{A}}

(90)

The action
\hat{U}\curvearrowright X_{f_{i}}
satisfies these conditions.

Remark 3.19
.

If
\rho:A\to\Bbbk[t,t^{-1}]\otimes_{\Bbbk}A
denotes the co-action map of
\mathbb{G}_{m}\curvearrowright\mathrm{Spec}(A)
, then
A_{\lambda=w}=\{f\in A:\rho(f)=t^{-w}\otimes f\}
. Condition
(1)
above is equivalent to that
\rho
factors through
\Bbbk[t]\otimes_{\Bbbk}A

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bbbk[t,t^{-1}]\otimes_{\Bbbk}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bbbk[t]\otimes_{\Bbbk}A.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}

(91)

Geometrically, Condition
(1)
is equivalent to that the action morphism
\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\mathrm{Spec}(A)\to\mathrm{Spec}(A)
extends to a morphism
\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\mathrm{Spec}(A)\to\mathrm{Spec}(A)
.

Let
\mathfrak{u}\to\mathfrak{s}
denote the surjective linear map dual to
\mathfrak{s}^{*}\subseteq\mathfrak{u}^{*}
. Let
\mathfrak{u}^{\prime}:=\ker(\mathfrak{u}\to\mathfrak{s})
. Let
\sigma^{*}:A\to\mathcal{O}(U)\otimes_{\Bbbk}A
denote the co-action map associated to
U\curvearrowright\mathrm{Spec}(A)
. Define sub-rings invariant under
\mathfrak{u}^{\prime}
and
\mathfrak{u}

\begin{split}A^{\mathfrak{u}^{\prime}}:=&\{f\in A:\xi.f=0\textrm{ for all }\xi\in\mathfrak{u}^{\prime}\}\\
A^{\mathfrak{u}}:=&\{f\in A:\xi.f=0\textrm{ for all }\xi\in\mathfrak{u}\}\end{split}

(92)

where
\xi:A\to A
is the
\Bbbk
-derivation defined as follows

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}(U)\otimes_{\Bbbk}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{u}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exp^{*}\otimes 1_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\xi\otimes 1_{A}}

(93)

for
\exp^{*}:\mathcal{O}(U)\to\mathfrak{u}^{*}
sending
f
to
\overline{f-f(e)}\in\mathfrak{m}_{e}/\mathfrak{m}_{e}^{2}\cong\mathfrak{u}^{*}
. It is easy to check
A^{\mathfrak{u}}\subseteq A^{\mathfrak{u}^{\prime}}
are sub-rings.

Lemma 3.20
.

We have
A^{\mathfrak{u}}=A^{\mathfrak{u}^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

Let
\{\xi_{1},\cdots,\xi_{r}\}
be a basis of
\mathfrak{u}
such that
\xi_{1},\cdots,\xi_{r-k}\in\mathfrak{u}^{\prime}
. Let
\{u_{1},\cdots,u_{r}\}
be the basis of
\mathfrak{u}^{*}
dual to
\{\xi_{1},\cdots,\xi_{r}\}
. Then
\{\xi_{r-k+1},\cdots,\xi_{r}\}
is a basis of
\mathfrak{s}^{*}
. The infinitesimal action
\phi_{A}:\Omega_{A/\Bbbk}\to\mathfrak{u}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}A
is determined by the equation

\phi_{A}(\mathrm{d}f)=\sum_{i=1}^{r}u_{i}\otimes\xi_{i}.f,\quad f\in A.

(94)

For
\xi\in\mathfrak{u}
and
f\in A
we have

\langle\phi_{A}(\mathrm{d}f),\;\xi\rangle=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\langle u_{i},\xi\rangle\otimes\xi_{i}.f=\xi.f

(95)

and therefore we have

\begin{split}A^{\mathfrak{u}^{\prime}}=&\{f\in A:\phi_{A}(\mathrm{d}f)\subseteq\mathfrak{s}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}A\}\\
A^{\mathfrak{u}}=&\{f\in A:\phi_{A}(\mathrm{d}f)=0\}.\end{split}

(96)

By diagram (
90
), we have
\mathrm{im}(\phi_{A})\cap(\mathfrak{s}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}A)=0
. The lemma is proved.
∎

We first construct the quotient of
\mathrm{Spec}(A)
by the subgroup
U^{\prime}:=\mathrm{exp}(\mathfrak{u}^{\prime})\subseteq U
.

Lemma 3.21
.

There exists
\xi_{1},\cdots,\xi_{r-k}\in\mathfrak{u}^{\prime}
and
x_{1},\cdots,x_{r-k}\in A
such that

•

\xi_{i}.x_{j}=\delta_{ij}
for
1\leq i,j\leq r-k
;

•

\xi.x_{j}\in A_{\lambda=0}
for
\xi\in\mathfrak{u}
and
1\leq j\leq r-k
.

Proof.

Let
w\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
be the unique
\lambda
-weight on
\mathfrak{u}
. Let
\{\xi_{1},\cdots,\xi_{r}\}
be a basis of
\mathfrak{u}
such that
\xi_{1},\cdots,\xi_{r-k}\in\mathfrak{u}^{\prime}
. Let
\{u_{1},\cdots,u_{r}\}
be the basis of
\mathfrak{u}^{*}
dual to
\{\xi_{1},\cdots,\xi_{r}\}
.

By diagram (
90
), the following composition is surjective

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{A/\Bbbk}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{u}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathfrak{u}^{\prime})^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}A.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{A}}

(97)

Then for
1\leq i\leq r-k
, there exists
\omega_{i}\in\Omega_{A/\Bbbk}
such that

\phi_{A}(\omega_{i})\in u_{i}\otimes 1+\mathfrak{s}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}A.

(98)

Write
\omega_{i}=\sum_{n,\mu,\nu}a^{(i)}_{n,\mu}\;\mathrm{d}b^{(i)}_{n,\nu}
for
a^{(i)}_{n,\mu}\in A_{\lambda=\mu},b^{(i)}_{n,\nu}\in A_{\lambda=\nu}
. Then we have

\begin{split}\phi_{A}(\omega_{i})=&\sum_{j=1}^{r}\sum_{n,\mu,\nu}u_{j}\otimes a^{(i)}_{n,\mu}\;\xi_{j}.b^{(i)}_{n,\nu}\\
=&\sum_{j=1}^{r}\sum_{n}u_{j}\otimes a^{(i)}_{n,0}\;\xi_{j}.b^{(i)}_{n,-w}+\sum_{j=1}^{r}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n,\mu,\nu\\
\mu<0\\
\textrm{or }\nu<-w\end{subarray}}u_{j}\otimes a^{(i)}_{n,\mu}\;\xi_{j}.b^{(i)}_{n,\nu}.\end{split}

(99)

Let
x_{i}:=\sum_{n}a^{(i)}_{n,0}b^{(i)}_{n,-w}\in A_{\lambda=-w}
for
1\leq i\leq r-k
. Since
x_{i}\in A_{\lambda=-w}
and any
\xi\in\mathfrak{u}\setminus\{0\}
has weight
w\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, we have
\xi.x_{i}\in A_{\lambda=0}
. This checks the second condition in the lemma.

Let
p:A\to A_{\lambda=0}
be the projection onto the weight subspace of weight 0, and let
q:\mathfrak{u}^{*}\to(\mathfrak{u}^{\prime})^{*}
be the surjective linear map. Then in
(\mathfrak{u}^{\prime})^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}A_{\lambda=0}

\begin{split}u_{i}\otimes 1=&(q\otimes p)\circ\phi_{A}(\omega_{i})\\
=&(q\otimes p)\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{r}\sum_{n}u_{j}\otimes a^{(i)}_{n,0}\;\xi_{j}.b^{(i)}_{n,-w}+\sum_{j=1}^{r}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n,\mu,\nu\\
\mu<0\\
\textrm{or }\nu<-w\end{subarray}}u_{j}\otimes a^{(i)}_{n,\mu}\;\xi_{j}.b^{(i)}_{n,\nu}\Big)\\
=&\sum_{j=1}^{r-k}u_{j}\otimes\xi_{j}.\Big(\sum_{n}a^{(i)}_{n,0}b^{(i)}_{n,-w}\Big)\\
=&\sum_{j=1}^{r-k}u_{j}\otimes\xi_{j}.x_{i}\end{split}

(100)

that is
\xi_{i}.x_{j}=\delta_{ij}
for
1\leq i,j\leq r-k
. This checks the first condition in the lemma.
∎

Proposition 3.22
.

Let
\mathrm{Spec}(A)
be an algebraic affine scheme over
\Bbbk
. Let
\hat{U}
act on
\mathrm{Spec}(A)
. Assume the action
\hat{U}\curvearrowright\mathrm{Spec}(A)
satisfies:

(1)

A_{\lambda=w}=0
if
w>0
;

(2)

There exists a subspace
\mathfrak{s}^{*}\subseteq\mathfrak{u}^{*}
such that
\mathfrak{s}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}A\cong\mathrm{coker}(\phi_{A})
in the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{s}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{A/\Bbbk}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{u}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{coker}(\phi_{A})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{A}}

(101)

where
\phi_{A}
is the infinitesimal action of
\mathfrak{u}
on
\mathrm{Spec}(A)
.

Then:

•

A
is a polynomial ring over
A^{\mathfrak{u}}
;

•

The affine morphism
\mathrm{Spec}(A)\to\mathrm{Spec}(A^{\mathfrak{u}})
is a universal geometric quotient by
U
.

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{u}^{\prime}:=\ker(\mathfrak{u}\to\mathfrak{s})
, where
\mathfrak{u}\to\mathfrak{s}
is dual to
\mathfrak{s}^{*}\subseteq\mathfrak{u}^{*}
. We can apply
[
5
]
Theorem 3.10 for the action of
\mathfrak{u}^{\prime}
on
A
. Lemma
3.21
shows that Condition
(3)
in
[
5
]
Theorem 3.10 is satisfied and then we have Condition
(6)
in that theorem that
\mathrm{Spec}(A)\to\mathrm{Spec}(A^{\mathfrak{u}^{\prime}})
is a trivial geometric quotient by
U^{\prime}=\exp(\mathfrak{u}^{\prime})
. Actually according to the proof of
[
5
]
Theorem 3.10, we have that
x_{1},\cdots,x_{r-k}\in A
are algebraically independent over
A^{\mathfrak{u}^{\prime}}
and
A=A^{\mathfrak{u}^{\prime}}[x_{1},\cdots,x_{r-k}]
. Since
A^{\mathfrak{u}}=A^{\mathfrak{u}^{\prime}}
by Lemma
3.20
, we have

A=A^{\mathfrak{u}}[x_{1},\cdots,x_{r-k}].

(102)

This proves that
A
is a polynomial ring over
A^{\mathfrak{u}}
. The statement that
\mathrm{Spec}(A)\to\mathrm{Spec}(A^{\mathfrak{u}})
is a universal geometric quotient follows immediately.
∎

Corollary 3.23
.

Let
\hat{U}\curvearrowright\mathrm{Spec}(A)
and
\mathfrak{s}^{*}\subseteq\mathfrak{u}^{*}
be as in Proposition
3.22
. Let
X:=\mathrm{Spec}(A)
and
Y:=\mathrm{Spec}(A^{\mathfrak{u}})
. Then
Y
represents the quotient presheaf
X/_{p}U

X/_{p}U:(\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk)^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Set},\quad T\mapsto\frac{\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk}(T,X)}{\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk}(T,U)}.

(103)

Moreover for any morphism
Y^{\prime}\to Y
in
\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
, we have that
Y^{\prime}
represents the quotient sheaf
(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}
on
(\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk)_{\acute{e}tale}
(See
Definition 02VG
for quotient sheaves), where
X^{\prime}:=X\times_{Y}Y^{\prime}
, and
(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}
is the sheafification of
X^{\prime}/_{p}U
.

Proof.

For a scheme, we think it as a sheaf on
(\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk)_{\acute{e}tale}
via its functor of points. Let
\pi:X\to Y
be the geometric quotient in Proposition
3.22
. Since
\pi:X\to Y
is
U
-invariant, the map
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk}(T,X)\to\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk}(T,Y)
factors through
(X/_{p}U)(T)
for any
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
. This gives a morphism
\eta:X/_{p}U\to Y
of presheaves.

By Lemma
3.21
and Proposition
3.22
, we can choose a basis
\xi_{1},\cdots,\xi_{r}
of
\mathfrak{u}
and
x_{1},\cdots,x_{r-k}\in A
such that:

•

A=A^{\mathfrak{u}}[x_{1},\cdots,x_{r-k}]
and
x_{1},\cdots,x_{r-k}
are algebraically independent over
A^{\mathfrak{u}}
;

•

\xi_{i}.x_{j}=\delta_{ij}
if
1\leq i,j\leq r-k
.

Let
u_{1},\cdots,u_{r}\in\mathfrak{u}^{*}
be the basis dual to
\xi_{1},\cdots,\xi_{r}
. Lift
u_{i}\in\mathfrak{u}^{*}
to
u_{i}\in\mathfrak{m}_{e}\subseteq\mathcal{O}(U)
. We identify
\mathcal{O}(U)\cong\Bbbk[u_{1},\cdots,u_{r}]
. Then for
1\leq i\leq r-k

\sigma^{*}:A\to\mathcal{O}(U)\otimes_{\Bbbk}A,\quad x_{i}\mapsto 1\otimes x_{i}+u_{i}\otimes 1+\mathrm{err}_{i},\quad\mathrm{err}_{i}\in(u_{r-k+1}\otimes 1,\cdots,u_{r}\otimes 1).

(104)

We first show that
\eta:X/_{p}U\to Y
is injective. Let
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
. Let
g_{i}:T\to X
for
i=1,2
be two morphisms such that
\eta_{T}([g_{1}])=\eta_{T}([g_{2}])=h
, i.e.
h=\pi\circ g_{1}=\pi\circ g_{2}
. We identify
\mathcal{O}(U)\cong\Bbbk[u_{1},\cdots,u_{r}]
and define a ring map

l^{*}:\Bbbk[u_{1},\cdots,u_{r}]\to\Gamma(T,\mathcal{O}_{T}),\quad u_{i}\mapsto\begin{cases}g_{2}^{*}(x_{i})-g_{1}^{*}(x_{i}),&\quad 1\leq i\leq r-k\\
0,&\quad r-k<i\leq r.\end{cases}

(105)

Then the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma(T,\mathcal{O}_{T})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma(T,\mathcal{O}_{T})\otimes_{\Bbbk}\Gamma(T,\mathcal{O}_{T})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}(U)\otimes_{\Bbbk}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{2}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l^{*}\otimes g_{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma^{*}}

(106)

which corresponds to a commutative diagram of schemes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\times T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U\times X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l\times g_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma}

(107)

where
l:T\to U
corresponds to the ring map
l^{*}:\mathcal{O}(U)\to\Gamma(T,\mathcal{O}_{T})
. Then
g_{2}=\sigma(l,g_{1})
for this
l:T\to U
. This implies
[g_{1}]=[g_{2}]\in(X/_{p}U)(T)
, so
\eta
is injective.

We then show
\eta
is surjective as a morphism of presheaves. Let
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
. Let
h:T\to Y
be in
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk}(T,Y)
. Let
g^{*}:A\to\Gamma(T,\mathcal{O}_{T})
be the composition
A=A^{\mathfrak{u}}[x_{1},\cdots,x_{r-k}]\to A^{\mathfrak{u}}[x_{1},\cdots,x_{r-k}]/(x_{1},\cdots,x_{r-k})\cong A^{\mathfrak{u}}\xrightarrow{h^{*}}\Gamma(T,\mathcal{O}_{T})
. Let
g:T\to X
correspond to
g^{*}:A\to\Gamma(T,\mathcal{O}_{T})
. Then
\eta_{T}([g])=h
. This shows that
\eta_{T}
is surjective. Then
\eta:X/_{p}U\cong Y
.

We first prove that if
Y^{\prime}\to Y
is a morphism between affine schemes, then
X^{\prime}/_{p}U\cong Y^{\prime}
as presheaves. Let
Y^{\prime}\to Y
be a morphism with
Y^{\prime}
affine with coordinate ring
B
. Then
X^{\prime}=X\times_{Y}Y^{\prime}
is affine with coordinate ring
A\otimes_{A^{\mathfrak{u}}}B=B[x_{1},\cdots,x_{r-k}]
. The same argument as above with
A^{\mathfrak{u}}
replaced by
B
shows that
X^{\prime}/_{p}U\to Y^{\prime}
is injective and surjective as presheaves.

Next, we consider a general morphism
Y^{\prime}\to Y
in
\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
. Choose a covering
\{Y^{\prime}_{j}\to Y^{\prime}\}_{j\in J}
in
(\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk)_{\acute{e}tale}
such that each of
Y^{\prime}_{j}
is affine. Let
B_{j}
be the coordinate ring of
Y^{\prime}_{j}
. The composition
Y^{\prime}_{j}\to Y^{\prime}\to Y
corresponds to a ring map
A^{\mathfrak{u}}\to B_{j}
. Let
X^{\prime}_{j}:=X\times_{Y}Y^{\prime}_{j}
. Then
X^{\prime}_{j}
is affine with coordinate ring
A\otimes_{A^{\mathfrak{u}}}B_{j}\cong B_{j}[x_{1},\cdots,x_{r-k}]
.

We construct a morphism of presheaves
Y^{\prime}\to(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}
. By the Yoneda lemma, this is equivalent to an element in
(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}(Y^{\prime})
. The sheaf property of
(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}
implies that the following is an equaliser diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}(Y^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\prod_{j\in J}(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}(Y^{\prime}_{j})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\prod_{k,l\in J}(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}\big(Y^{\prime}_{k}\times_{Y^{\prime}}Y^{\prime}_{l}\big).}

(108)

We have that
X^{\prime}_{j}/_{p}U\cong Y^{\prime}_{j}
as presheaves. Let
\zeta_{j}\in(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}(Y^{\prime}_{j})
be elements corresponding to morphisms of presheaves
\zeta_{j}:Y^{\prime}_{j}\to(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}
as follows

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}_{j}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}_{j}/_{p}U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}/_{p}U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\zeta_{j}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

(109)

where
X^{\prime}_{j}/_{p}U\to X^{\prime}/_{p}U
is induced by
X^{\prime}_{j}\to X^{\prime}
, which is the base change of
Y^{\prime}_{j}\to Y^{\prime}
. We will show the collection
\{\zeta_{j}\}_{j\in J}
is a descent datum with respect to
\{Y^{\prime}_{j}\to Y^{\prime}\}_{j\in J}
.

Choose
k,l\in J
. Denote projections and base changes of covering morphisms

\begin{split}\mathrm{pr}_{k}:&Y^{\prime}_{k}\times_{Y^{\prime}}Y^{\prime}_{k}\to Y^{\prime}_{k},\quad\mathrm{pr}_{l}:Y^{\prime}_{k}\times_{Y^{\prime}}Y^{\prime}_{l}\to Y^{\prime}_{l}\\
\iota_{k}:&X^{\prime}_{k}\to X^{\prime},\quad\iota_{l}:X^{\prime}_{l}\to X^{\prime}.\end{split}

(110)

Since
X^{\prime}_{k}/_{p}U\cong Y^{\prime}_{k}
, the quotient
X^{\prime}_{k}\to Y^{\prime}_{k}
has sections. Let
\sigma:Y^{\prime}_{k}\to X^{\prime}_{k}
be a section. The following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}_{k}\times_{Y^{\prime}}Y^{\prime}_{l}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}_{l}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma\circ\mathrm{pr}_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{k}}

(111)

Then there is a morphism
\varrho:Y^{\prime}_{k}\times_{Y^{\prime}}Y^{\prime}_{k}\to X^{\prime}_{l}=X^{\prime}\times_{Y^{\prime}}Y^{\prime}_{l}
fitting into the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}_{k}\times_{Y^{\prime}}Y^{\prime}_{l}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}_{l}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}_{l}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varrho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{k}\circ\sigma_{k}\circ\mathrm{pr}_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{l}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{l}}

(112)

Let
\sigma,\varrho
be as above. Then the following diagram of presheaves commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}_{k}\times_{Y^{\prime}}Y^{\prime}_{l}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}_{k}/_{p}U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}_{l}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}_{l}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}_{l}/_{p}U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}/_{p}U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma\circ\mathrm{pr}_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varrho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\zeta_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\zeta_{l}}

(113)

Then for
\zeta_{k}\in(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}(Y^{\prime}_{k})
and
\zeta_{l}\in(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}(Y^{\prime}_{l})
we have

\mathrm{pr}_{k}^{*}(\zeta_{k})=\mathrm{pr}_{l}^{*}(\zeta_{l})\in(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}\big(Y^{\prime}_{k}\times_{Y^{\prime}}Y^{\prime}_{l}\big).

(114)

This holds for all
k,l\in J
, so
\{\zeta_{j}\}_{j\in J}
is a descent datum. Since
(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}
is a sheaf, there is a unique
\zeta\in(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}(Y^{\prime})
such that
\zeta|_{Y^{\prime}_{j}}=\zeta_{j}
. This element
\zeta
does not depend on the covering
\{Y^{\prime}_{j}\to Y^{\prime}\}_{j\in J}
, because
\zeta
does not change when the covering is refined and any two coverings have a common refinement. The element
\zeta\in(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}(Y^{\prime})
is equivalent to a morphism
\zeta:Y^{\prime}\to(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}
of sheaves by the Yoneda lemma. We omit the verification that
\zeta
is the inverse of
(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}\to Y^{\prime}
.
∎

3.3.3
Gluing affine quotients

We can glue the affine quotients into a quasi-projective quotient.

Theorem 3.24
.

For the linearisation
\hat{U}\curvearrowright(X,L)
, assume Condition
UU
. Then
X^{0}_{\min}
admits a universal geometric quotient by
U
in
\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk

\pi:X^{0}_{\min}\to Y.

(115)

and
\pi
is locally isomorphic to
\mathbb{A}^{r-k}\times_{\Bbbk}Y
over
Y
, where
r=\dim U
and
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
is the rank of
\mathrm{coker}(\phi)|_{X^{0}_{\min}}
.

Moreover there exists an ample line bundle
M
on
Y
such that
\pi^{*}M\cong L^{m}|_{X^{0}_{\min}}
for some
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
.

Proof.

Recall that the linearisation
\hat{U}\curvearrowright(X,L)
satisfies Condition
UU
. At the end of Section
3.3.1
, we have achieved the situation where:

•

There exists
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
f_{1},\cdots,f_{t}\in H^{0}(X,L^{m})_{\lambda=\max}
such that
X^{0}_{\min}
is covered by
\{X_{f_{i}}\}_{i=1}^{t}
;

•

For each
X_{f_{i}}
, there exists a linear subspace
\mathfrak{s}_{i}^{*}\subseteq\mathfrak{u}^{*}
such that
\mathfrak{s}_{i}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{X_{f_{i}}}\cong\mathrm{coker}(\phi)|_{X_{f_{i}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{s}_{i}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{X_{f_{i}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{X_{f_{i}}/\Bbbk}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{u}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{X_{f_{i}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{coker}(\phi)|_{X_{f_{i}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi|_{X_{f_{i}}}}

(116)

We can apply Proposition
3.22
to each of
X_{f_{i}}
. For each
1\leq i\leq i\leq t
, there exists a universal geometric quotient
\pi_{i}:X_{f_{i}}\to Y_{i}:=\mathrm{Spec}\big(\mathcal{O}(X_{f_{i}})^{U}\big)
.

We first glue the affine schemes
Y_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq t
. For
1\leq i,j\leq t
, consider
f_{j}/f_{i}\in\mathcal{O}(X_{f_{i}})
. We have
f_{j}/f_{i}\in\mathcal{O}(X_{f_{i}})^{U}
. Since
\pi_{i}:X_{f_{i}}\to Y_{i}
is a geometric quotient by
U
, we have
\pi_{i}^{*}:\mathcal{O}(Y_{i})\cong\mathcal{O}(X_{f_{i}})^{U}
. Then there exists a unique
g_{ji}\in\mathcal{O}(Y_{i})
such that
\pi_{i}^{*}(g_{ji})=f_{j}/f_{i}
. Let
Y_{ij}=Y_{i}\setminus\mathbb{V}(g_{ji})
be a principal affine open subscheme. Then
\pi_{i}^{-1}(Y_{ij})=X_{f_{i}f_{j}}=\pi_{j}^{-1}(Y_{ji})
. Since
\pi_{i},\pi_{j}
are universal geometric quotients, both
Y_{ij}
and
Y_{ji}
are geometric quotients of
X_{f_{i}f_{j}}
. Therefore canonically
Y_{ij}\cong Y_{ji}
and this isomorphism is the unique morphism such that the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{f_{i}f_{j}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{f_{j}f_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{ij}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{ji}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{i}|_{X_{f_{i}f_{j}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{j}|_{X_{f_{j}f_{i}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

(117)

It follows easily that
\{Y_{i}\}_{i=1}^{t}
glue to a scheme
Y
containing each
Y_{i}
as an affine open subscheme. Moreover the morphisms
\pi_{i}:X_{f_{i}}\to Y_{i}
glue to a morphism

\pi:X^{0}_{\min}\to Y

(118)

which is an affine universal geometric quotient by
U
.

We then construct an ample line bundle
M
on
Y
such that
\pi^{*}M\cong L^{m}|_{X^{0}_{\min}}
, where
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
is the integer such that
f_{i}\in H^{0}(X,L^{m})_{\lambda=\max}
. Recall functions
g_{ji}\in\mathcal{O}(Y_{i})
are invertible on
Y_{ij}
, i.e.
g_{ji}|_{Y_{ij}}\in\mathcal{O}(Y_{ij})^{\times}
. They satisfy the cocycle condition
g_{ij}g_{jk}g_{ki}=1
in
\mathcal{O}(Y_{i}\cap Y_{j}\cap Y_{k})
for all
1\leq i,j,k\leq t
. Then there exists a line bundle
M
whose transition functions are
\{g_{ij}\}_{i,j=1}^{t}
. It follows that
\pi^{*}M
and
L^{m}|_{X^{0}_{\min}}
both have transition functions
\{f_{j}/f_{i}\}_{i,j=1}^{t}
with respect to the covering
\{X_{f_{i}}\}_{i=1}^{t}
. Therefore
\pi^{*}M\cong L^{m}|_{X^{0}_{\min}}
.

It remains to show that
M
is ample. The scheme
Y=\bigcup_{i=1}^{t}Y_{i}
is quasi-compact since it is a finite union of affine schemes. We will construct sections
g^{(i)}\in\Gamma(Y,M)
for
1\leq i\leq t
such that each
Y_{g^{(i)}}=Y_{i}
is affine. Fix
i
. Consider
g^{(i)}_{j}:=g_{ji}\in\mathcal{O}(Y_{j})
for
1\leq j\leq t
. Then
\{g^{(i)}_{j}\}_{j=1}^{t}
define a global section
g^{(i)}\in\Gamma(Y,M)
since

g^{(i)}_{j_{1}}=g_{j_{1}j_{2}}g^{(i)}_{j_{2}},\quad\textrm{ for }1\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq t.

(119)

For each
j
, we have

Y_{j}\cap Y_{g^{(i)}}=(Y_{j})_{g^{(i)}_{j}}=(Y_{j})_{g_{ji}}=Y_{ji}=Y_{j}\cap Y_{i}

(120)

which proves that
Y_{g^{(i)}}=Y_{i}
. This proves that
M
is ample (
Definition 01PS
).
∎

There is a more concrete description of an immersion of the quotient
Y=X^{0}_{\min}/U
into a projective space.

Lemma 3.25
.

Assume Condition
UU
. Let
\pi:X^{0}_{\min}\to Y
be the quotient in Theorem
3.24
. Then for sufficiently divisible
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, there exists a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}\big(H^{0}(X,L^{m})^{*}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{0}_{\min}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}\big(H^{0}(X,L^{m})^{*}\big)^{0}_{\min}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}\Big(\big(H^{0}(X,L^{m})^{U}\big)^{*}\Big)^{0}_{\min}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}

(121)

Proof.

According to the proof of Theorem
3.24
, there exists
m_{1}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
such that:

•

H^{0}(X,L^{m_{1}})
generates
\bigoplus_{d\geq 0}H^{0}(X,L^{dm_{1}})
;

•

there exist
t\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
f_{1},\cdots,f_{t}\in H^{0}(X,L^{m_{1}})_{\lambda=\max}
;

•

X^{0}_{\min}=\bigcup_{i=1}^{t}X_{f_{i}}
;

•

for each
1\leq i\leq t
, the invariant subring
\mathcal{O}(X_{f_{i}})^{U}
is finitely generated.

Then there exists
m_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
such that for each
1\leq i\leq t
, we can choose
n_{i}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
generators of
\mathcal{O}(X_{f_{i}})^{U}
of the form

\frac{g^{(i)}_{j}}{f_{i}^{m_{2}}}\in\mathcal{O}(X_{f_{i}})^{U}\quad j=1,\cdots,n_{i}

(122)

for
g^{(i)}_{j}\in H^{0}(X,L^{m_{1}m_{2}})
. Since these finitely many generators
\frac{g^{(i)}_{j}}{f_{i}^{m_{2}}}
are invariant under
\mathfrak{u}
and
\dim\mathfrak{u}<\infty
, there exists
m_{3}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
such that for all
i,j
we have
f_{i}^{m_{3}}g^{(i)}_{j}\in H^{0}(X,L^{m_{1}(m_{2}+m_{3})})^{U}
.

Let
m=m_{1}(m_{2}+m_{3})\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
. The above shows that for each
1\leq i\leq t
, there is a surjective map

\frac{H^{0}(X,L^{m})^{U}}{f_{i}^{m_{2}+m_{3}}}\to\mathcal{O}(X_{f_{i}})^{U}

(123)

and then the following ring map is surjective for
1\leq i\leq t

\Big(\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}H^{0}(X,L^{m})^{U}\Big)_{(f_{i}^{m_{2}+m_{3}})}\to\mathcal{O}(X_{f_{i}})^{U}

(124)

which corresponds to a closed immersion

X_{f_{i}}/U\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}\Big(\big(H^{0}(X,L^{m})^{U}\big)^{*}\Big)_{f_{i}^{m_{2}+m_{3}}}.

(125)

By the construction of
\pi:X^{0}_{\min}\to Y
, we have that
Y
is glued from
X_{f_{i}}/U
. The above closed immersions for
1\leq i\leq t
glue to a closed immersion

Y\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}\Big(\big(H^{0}(X,L^{m})^{U}\big)^{*}\Big)^{0}_{\min}.

(126)

The closed immersions
X\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}\big(H^{0}(X,L^{m})^{*}\big)
and
X^{0}_{\min}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}\big(H^{0}(X,L^{m})^{*}\big)^{0}_{\min}
are naturally defined. It is easy to see these three closed immersions fit into the commutative diagram as stated.
∎

Corollary 3.26
.

For the linearisation
\hat{U}\curvearrowright(X,L)
, assume Condition
UU
. Let
\pi:X^{0}_{\min}\to Y
be the quotient in Theorem
3.24
. Then
Y
represents the quotient sheaf
(X^{0}_{\min}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}
on
(\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk)_{\acute{e}tale}
(
Definition 03BD
).

Moreover, for any morphism
Y^{\prime}\to Y
in
\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
, the quotient sheaf
\big(X^{\prime}/_{p}U\big)^{\sharp}
is represented by
Y^{\prime}
, where
X^{\prime}:=X^{0}_{\min}\times_{Y}Y^{\prime}
.

Proof.

According to the proof of Theorem
3.24
, the quotient
\pi:X^{0}_{\min}\to Y
is glued from
\pi|_{X_{f_{i}}}:X_{f_{i}}\to Y_{i}:=X_{f_{i}}/U
for some
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
f_{1},\cdots,f_{t}\in H^{0}(X,L^{m})_{\lambda=\max}
. Let
\{Y_{j}\to Y\}_{j\in J}
denote this Zariski covering. which is also a covering in
(\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk)_{\acute{e}tale}
. Let
\{X_{j}\to X^{0}_{\min}\}_{j\in J}
be the base change covering of
\{Y_{j}\to Y\}
. By Corollary
3.23
, we have
(X_{j}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}\cong Y_{j}
for all
j\in J
, and the representability is stable under base change, in the sense that
\big(X_{j}\times_{Y_{j}}W\big/_{p}U\big)^{\sharp}\cong W
for any morphism
W\to Y_{j}
in
\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
.

Let
Y^{\prime}\to Y
be a morphism in
\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
. Denote

X^{\prime}:=X\times_{Y}Y^{\prime},\quad Y^{\prime}_{j}:=Y_{j}\times_{Y}Y^{\prime},\quad X^{\prime}_{j}:=X^{\prime}\times_{Y^{\prime}}Y^{\prime}_{j}.

(127)

Then
X^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime}
is a geometric quotient as a base change of a universal geometric quotient. The quotient induces a morphism
(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}\to Y^{\prime}
of sheaves. We will construct an element
\zeta\in(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}(Y^{\prime})
such that the corresponding morphism
Y^{\prime}\to(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}
is inverse to
(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}\to Y^{\prime}
.

By Corollary
3.23
, we have
(X^{\prime}_{j}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}\cong Y^{\prime}_{j}
, which is the base change of
(X_{j}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}\cong Y_{j}
along
Y^{\prime}_{j}\to Y_{j}
. The morphism
X^{\prime}_{j}\to(X^{\prime}_{j}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}
is surjective as a morphism of sheaves, so there exists a covering
\{\varphi_{j}:\widetilde{Y^{\prime}_{j}}\to Y^{\prime}_{j}\}
in
(\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk)_{\acute{e}tale}
and a morphism
\sigma_{j}:\widetilde{Y^{\prime}_{j}}\to X^{\prime}_{j}
fitting into the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{Y^{\prime}_{j}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}_{j}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}_{j}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X^{\prime}_{j}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma_{j}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{j}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

(128)

The composition of
\{\varphi_{j}:\widetilde{Y^{\prime}_{j}}\to Y^{\prime}_{j}\}
and
\{Y^{\prime}_{j}\to Y^{\prime}\}
is also a covering
\{\widetilde{Y^{\prime}_{j}}\to Y^{\prime}\}_{j\in J}
in
(\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk)_{\acute{e}tale}
. Since
(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}
is a sheaf, the following is an equaliser diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}(Y^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\prod_{j\in J}(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}\big(\widetilde{Y^{\prime}_{j}}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\prod_{k,l\in J}(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}\big(\widetilde{Y^{\prime}_{k}}\times_{Y^{\prime}}\widetilde{Y^{\prime}_{l}}\big).}

(129)

Let
\zeta_{j}\in(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}\big(\widetilde{Y^{\prime}_{j}}\big)
be the element corresponding to the morphism
\widetilde{Y^{\prime}_{j}}\xrightarrow{\varphi_{j}}Y^{\prime}_{j}\cong(X^{\prime}_{j}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}\to(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}
. We will show
\{\zeta_{j}\}_{j\in J}
is a descent datum with respect to
\{\widetilde{Y^{\prime}_{j}}\to Y^{\prime}\}_{j\in J}
.

Fix
k,l\in J
. The following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{Y^{\prime}_{k}}\times_{Y^{\prime}}\widetilde{Y^{\prime}_{l}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{Y^{\prime}_{k}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{Y^{\prime}_{l}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X^{\prime}_{k}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}_{l}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathrm{pr}_{k}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathrm{pr}_{l}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

(130)

There exists a unique morphism
\varrho:\widetilde{Y^{\prime}_{k}}\times_{Y^{\prime}}\widetilde{Y^{\prime}_{l}}\to X^{\prime}_{l}=X^{\prime}\times_{Y^{\prime}}Y^{\prime}_{l}
fitting into the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{Y^{\prime}_{k}}\times_{Y^{\prime}}\widetilde{Y^{\prime}_{l}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}_{l}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}_{l}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{k}\circ\sigma_{k}\circ\widetilde{\mathrm{pr}_{k}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varrho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{l}\circ\widetilde{\mathrm{pr}_{l}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{l}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

(131)

The following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{Y^{\prime}_{k}}\times_{Y^{\prime}}\widetilde{Y^{\prime}_{l}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{Y^{\prime}_{k}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}_{l}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X^{\prime}_{k}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{Y^{\prime}_{l}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}_{l}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X^{\prime}_{l}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathrm{pr}_{k}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathrm{pr}_{l}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma_{k}\circ\widetilde{\mathrm{pr}_{k}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varrho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\zeta_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\zeta_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

(132)

Therefore

\widetilde{\mathrm{pr}_{k}}^{*}(\zeta_{k})=\widetilde{\mathrm{pr}_{l}}^{*}(\zeta_{l})\in(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}\big(\widetilde{Y^{\prime}_{k}}\times_{Y^{\prime}}\widetilde{Y^{\prime}_{l}}\big)

(133)

which holds for all
k,l\in J
. Then
\{\zeta_{j}\}_{j\in J}
is a descent datum. There exists a unique
\zeta\in(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}(Y^{\prime})
such that
\zeta\big|_{\widetilde{Y^{\prime}_{j}}}=\zeta_{j}
. We omit the verification that
\zeta
does not depend on the covering
\{\widetilde{Y^{\prime}_{j}}\to Y^{\prime}\}_{j\in J}
and that the corresponding morphism
\zeta:Y^{\prime}\to(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}
is inverse to
(X^{\prime}/_{p}U)^{\sharp}\to Y^{\prime}
.
∎

3.3.4
Quotients by
\hat{U}
and parabolic groups

With Condition
UU
, there is a quasi-projective geometric quotient
\pi:X^{0}_{\min}\to X^{0}_{\min}/U
by
U
. To obtain a quotient by
\hat{U}
, we consider the residual linear action of
\lambda
on
X^{0}_{\min}/U
.

Theorem 3.27

(
[
1
]
)
.

Let
X
be a projective scheme over
\Bbbk
with an ample line bundle
L
. Let
\hat{U}
act on
X
linearly with respect to
L
. Assume Condition
UU
for
\hat{U}\curvearrowright(X,L)
. Then the subset
X^{0}_{\min}\setminus UZ_{\min}
is
\hat{U}
-invariant open, and it admits a projective universal geometric quotient by
\hat{U}
.

Remark 3.28
.

Condition
UU
implies
X^{0}_{\min}\neq\emptyset
. However it is possible that
X^{0}_{\min}\setminus UZ_{\min}=\emptyset
.

Proof.

By Theorem
3.24
, we have a universal geometric quotient by
U

\pi:X^{0}_{\min}\to Y.

(134)

By Lemma
3.25
, there exists
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
such that for
W:=H^{0}(X,L^{m})^{U}
, we have an immersion
Y\to\mathbb{P}(W^{*})
. Consider
Y
as a locally closed subscheme of
\mathbb{P}(W^{*})
. Let
\overline{Y}\subseteq\mathbb{P}(W^{*})
be the scheme theoretic image of
Y\subseteq\mathbb{P}(W^{*})
. Then
\overline{Y}\subseteq\mathbb{P}(W^{*})
is
\lambda
-invariant and
Y=\overline{Y}\cap\mathbb{P}(W^{*})^{0}_{\min}
.

There is a natural linearisation
\lambda\curvearrowright\big(\mathbb{P}(W^{*}),\mathcal{O}(1)\big)
, induced from the linear representation
\lambda\curvearrowright H^{0}(X,L^{m})^{U}
. We will consider a twisted of the linearisation
\lambda\curvearrowright\big(\overline{Y},\mathcal{O}(1)|_{\overline{Y}}\big)
.

Let
\max(\lambda,W),\mathrm{2nd}\max(\lambda,W)\in\mathbb{Z}
denote the maximal and next maximal weights of
\lambda\curvearrowright W
. Let
\theta\in\mathbb{Q}
be such that

\mathrm{2nd}\max(\lambda,W)<-\theta<\max(\lambda,W).

(135)

Consider
\theta\in\mathbb{Q}
as a rational character of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
. Denote
\mathcal{O}(1)_{\theta}
the linearisation twisted by
\theta
. Then we have

\mathbb{P}(W^{*})^{\lambda,\mathcal{O}(1)_{\theta},\mathrm{ss}}=\mathbb{P}(W^{*})^{\lambda,\mathcal{O}(1)_{\theta},\mathrm{s}}=\mathbb{P}(W^{*})^{0}_{\min}\big\backslash\mathbb{P}\big((W_{\lambda=\max})^{*}\big).

(136)

Then by
[
12
]
Theorem 1.19

\overline{Y}^{\lambda,\mathcal{O}(1)_{\theta},\mathrm{(s)s}}=\overline{Y}\cap\mathbb{P}(W^{*})^{\lambda,\mathcal{O}(1)_{\theta},\mathrm{(s)s}}=Y\cap\Big(\mathbb{P}(W^{*})^{0}_{\min}\big\backslash\mathbb{P}\big((W_{\lambda=\max})^{*}\big)\Big).

(137)

There is a projective universal geometric quotient by
\lambda

\overline{Y}^{\lambda,\mathcal{O}(1)_{\theta},\mathrm{(s)s}}\to\overline{Y}/\!/\lambda.

(138)

The subset
X^{0}_{\min}\setminus UZ_{\min}\subseteq X^{0}_{\min}
is open since it is the preimage of
\overline{Y}^{\lambda,\mathcal{O}(1)_{\theta},\mathrm{(s)s}}\subseteq Y
along
\pi

X^{0}_{\min}\setminus UZ_{\min}=\pi^{-1}\bigg(Y\cap\Big(\mathbb{P}(W^{*})^{0}_{\min}\big\backslash\mathbb{P}\big((W_{\lambda=\max})^{*}\big)\Big)\bigg).

(139)

Then
X^{0}_{\min}\setminus UZ_{\min}\to\overline{Y}^{\lambda,\mathcal{O}(1)_{\theta},\mathrm{(s)s}}
is a universal geometric quotient by
U
as a base change of
\pi:X^{0}_{\min}\to Y
. The composition

X^{0}_{\min}\setminus UZ_{\min}\to\overline{Y}^{\lambda,\mathcal{O}(1)_{\theta},\mathrm{(s)s}}\to\overline{Y}/\!/\lambda

(140)

is then a projective universal geometric quotient by
\hat{U}
.
∎

We then consider a special type of parabolic group. Let
n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
. Consider the parabolic subgroup
P\subseteq\mathrm{SL}(n_{1}+n_{2})

P=\bigg\{\begin{pmatrix}g_{11}&g_{12}\\
&g_{22}\end{pmatrix}:\begin{matrix}g_{ii}\in\mathrm{GL}(n_{i})\textrm{ for }i=1,2\\
\det(g_{11})\det(g_{22})=1\end{matrix}\bigg\}.

(141)

Let
U\subseteq P
be the unipotent radical,
T=\mathbb{G}_{m}
and
K=\mathrm{SL}(n_{1})\times\mathrm{SL}(n_{2})
. Then there is a surjective homomorphism with finite kernel

U\rtimes(T\times K)\to P,\quad(u,t,k)\mapsto u\begin{pmatrix}t^{\frac{n_{2}}{(n_{1},n_{2})}}I_{n_{1}}&\\
&t^{-\frac{n_{1}}{(n_{1},n_{2})}}I_{n_{2}}\end{pmatrix}k

(142)

where
(n_{1},n_{2}):=\gcd(n_{1},n_{2})
.

Let
X
be a projective scheme with an ample line bundle
L
. Let
P
act on
X
linearly with respect to
L
. We can consider the linearisation
U\rtimes(T\times K)\curvearrowright(X,L)
via the above homomorphism onto
P
. In
U\rtimes(T\times K)
, the subgroup
U\rtimes T
has the form
\hat{U}
. We can first apply our theorems for
\hat{U}
-linearisations and then consider the residual linearisation by
K
.

Theorem 3.29
.

Let
U\rtimes(T\times K)\to P
be as above. Let
X
be a projective scheme with an ample line bundle
L
. Let
P
act on
X
linearly with respect to
L
. Assume Condition
UU
for
\hat{U}\curvearrowright(X,L)
, where
\hat{U}:=U\rtimes T
. Let
Z_{\min}^{K,\mathrm{(s)s}}\subseteq Z_{\min}
denote the
K
-(semi)stable locus of
K\curvearrowright\big(Z_{\min},L|_{Z_{\min}}\big)
. Let
p_{\lambda}:X^{0}_{\min}\to Z_{\min}
be the retraction. Then the following
P
-invariant open subset

\big(X^{0}_{\min}\setminus UZ_{\min}\big)\cap p_{\lambda}^{-1}\big(Z_{\min}^{K,\mathrm{s}}\big)

(143)

has a quasi-projective universal geometric quotient by
P
. Moreover if
Z_{\min}^{K,\mathrm{s}}=Z_{\min}^{K,\mathrm{ss}}
, then the quotient is projective.

Proof.

Assume
Z_{\min}^{K,\mathrm{s}}\neq\emptyset
. Let
\pi:X^{0}_{\min}\to Y
be the quotient in Theorem
3.24
. By Lemma
3.25
, there exists
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
such that for
W:=H^{0}(X,L^{m})^{U}
, we can view
Y
as a locally closed subscheme
Y\subseteq\mathbb{P}(W^{*})
. Let
\overline{Y}\subseteq\mathbb{P}(W^{*})
be the scheme theoretic image of
Y\subseteq\mathbb{P}(W^{*})
. Then
\overline{Y}\subseteq\mathbb{P}(W^{*})
is a
T\times K
-invariant closed subscheme.

Characters of
T\times K
are characters of
T=\mathbb{G}_{m}
. Rational characters are represented by rational numbers. Let
\max(\lambda,W)\in\mathbb{Z}
be the maximal weight of
\lambda\curvearrowright W
. Let
\epsilon\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
. Let
\theta\in\mathbb{Q}
represents a rational character of
T\times K
such that

\max(\lambda,W)-\epsilon<-\theta<\max(\lambda,W).

(144)

Let
\mathbb{P}(W^{*})^{T\times K,\widehat{\mathrm{s}}}\subseteq\mathbb{P}(W^{*})^{T\times K,\widehat{\mathrm{ss}}}\subseteq\mathbb{P}(W^{*})
denote open subsets whose closed points are the following respectively

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}(W^{*})^{T\times K,\widehat{\mathrm{s}}}\overset{\mathrm{cl}}{=\!=}&\Big\{[v]\in\mathbb{P}(W^{*}):v\neq v_{\lambda=\max}\neq 0,\;[v_{\lambda=\max}]\in\mathbb{P}\big((W^{*})_{\lambda=\max}\big)^{K,\mathrm{s}}\Big\}\\
\mathbb{P}(W^{*})^{T\times K,\widehat{\mathrm{ss}}}\overset{\mathrm{cl}}{=\!=}&\Big\{[v]\in\mathbb{P}(W^{*}):v\neq v_{\lambda=\max}\neq 0,\;[v_{\lambda=\max}]\in\mathbb{P}\big((W^{*})_{\lambda=\max}\big)^{K,\mathrm{ss}}\Big\}\end{split}

(145)

where
\overset{\mathrm{cl}}{=\!=}
denotes that two sides have the same closed points.

When
\epsilon>0
is sufficiently small, for the twisted linearisation
T\times K\curvearrowright\big(\mathbb{P}(W^{*}),\mathcal{O}(1)_{\theta}\big)
, we have by the Hilbert-Mumford criterion

\mathbb{P}(W^{*})^{T\times K,\widehat{\mathrm{s}}}\subseteq\mathbb{P}(W^{*})^{T\times K,\mathcal{O}(1)_{\theta},\mathrm{s}}\subseteq\mathbb{P}(W^{*})^{T\times K,\mathcal{O}(1)_{\theta},\mathrm{ss}}\subseteq\mathbb{P}(W^{*})^{T\times K,\widehat{\mathrm{ss}}}.

(146)

Note that the four subsets above are all contained in
\mathbb{P}(W^{*})^{0}_{\min}
. Recall that
Y=\overline{Y}\cap\mathbb{P}(W^{*})^{0}_{\min}
. Then their intersections with
\overline{Y}
form the following chain

Y\cap\mathbb{P}(W^{*})^{T\times K,\widehat{\mathrm{s}}}\subseteq\overline{Y}^{T\times K,\mathcal{O}(1)_{\theta},\mathrm{s}}\subseteq\overline{Y}^{T\times K,\mathcal{O}(1)_{\theta},\mathrm{ss}}\subseteq Y\cap\mathbb{P}(W^{*})^{T\times K,\widehat{\mathrm{ss}}}.

(147)

The stable locus
\overline{Y}^{T\times K,\mathcal{O}(1)_{\theta},\mathrm{s}}
has a quasi-projective universal geometric quotient by
T\times K
, and so does the
T\times K
-invariant open subset
Y\cap\mathbb{P}(W^{*})^{T\times K,\widehat{\mathrm{s}}}
. The preimage of
Y\cap\mathbb{P}(W^{*})^{T\times K,\widehat{\mathrm{s}}}
along
\pi
is

\pi^{-1}\big(Y\cap\mathbb{P}(W^{*})^{T\times K,\widehat{\mathrm{s}}}\big)=\big(X^{0}_{\min}\setminus UZ_{\min}\big)\cap p_{\lambda}^{-1}\big(Z_{\min}^{K,\mathrm{s}}\big).

(148)

Therefore the composition

\big(X^{0}_{\min}\setminus UZ_{\min}\big)\cap p_{\lambda}^{-1}\big(Z_{\min}^{K,\mathrm{s}}\big)\to Y\cap\mathbb{P}(W^{*})^{T\times K,\widehat{\mathrm{s}}}\to\big(Y\cap\mathbb{P}(W^{*})^{T\times K,\widehat{\mathrm{s}}}\big)\big/T\times K

(149)

is a quasi-projective universal geometric quotient by
U\rtimes(T\times K)
, the same as a quotient by
P
.

If
Z_{\min}^{K,\mathrm{s}}=Z_{\min}^{K,\mathrm{ss}}
, then we have

Y\cap\mathbb{P}(W^{*})^{T\times K,\widehat{\mathrm{s}}}=Y\cap\mathbb{P}(W^{*})^{T\times K,\widehat{\mathrm{ss}}}

(150)

since

Y\cap\mathbb{P}\big((W^{*})_{\lambda=\max}\big)^{K,\mathrm{s}}=\pi\big(Z_{\min}^{K,\mathrm{s}}\big)=\pi\big(Z_{\min}^{K,\mathrm{s}}\big)=Y\cap\mathbb{P}\big((W^{*})_{\lambda=\max}\big)^{K,\mathrm{ss}}.

(151)

Then four sets in the chain (
147
) are the same. In this situation the
P
-quotient is projective

\Big(\big(X^{0}_{\min}\setminus UZ_{\min}\big)\cap p_{\lambda}^{-1}\big(Z_{\min}^{K,\mathrm{s}}\big)\Big)\Big/P\cong\overline{Y}\big/\!\big/_{\theta}(T\times K).

(152)

∎

3.4
Theorem with Condition
WUU

For the linearisation
\hat{U}\curvearrowright(X,L)
, Condition
WUU
is weaker than Condition
UU
. When Condition
WUU
is satisfied, we can show that there exists a
\hat{U}
-equivariant blow-up
\widetilde{X}\to X
and an ample linearisation
\hat{U}\curvearrowright(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{L})
, such that Condition
UU
is satisfied.

3.4.1
The blow-up centre

Let
\iota:Z_{\min}\hookrightarrow X
denote the closed immersion and let
\iota^{\sharp}:\mathcal{O}_{X}\to\iota_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z_{\min}}
denote the associated morphism between sheaves of rings. Then
\ker(\iota^{\sharp})\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{X}
is the sheaf of ideals associated to
Z_{\min}\hookrightarrow X
. Recall that
k\in\mathbb{N}
is the integer such that
\mathrm{Fit}_{k-1}(\phi)=0
and
\dim\mathrm{Stab}_{U}(z)=k
for some closed point
z\in Z_{\min}
. Let
\mathcal{I}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{X}
be the sheaf of ideals

\mathcal{I}:=\ker(\iota^{\sharp})+\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(\phi)\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{X}.

(153)

Let
\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{I})\hookrightarrow X
denote the closed subscheme associated to
\mathcal{I}
. Then scheme theoretically
\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{I})=Z_{\min}\cap\mathbb{V}(\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(\phi))
.

Let
\sigma:U\times_{\Bbbk}X\to X
denote the action morphism. Since
U
is affine, we have
\sigma_{*}(\mathcal{O}_{U\times_{\Bbbk}X})\cong\mathcal{O}(U)\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{X}
. Let
\mathcal{J}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{X}
be the kernel of the following composition

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma_{*}(\mathcal{O}_{U\times_{\Bbbk}X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}(U)\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}(U)\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{X}/\mathcal{I}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

(154)

The closed subscheme
\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{J})\hookrightarrow X
is the scheme theoretic image of

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U\times_{\Bbbk}\big(Z_{\min}\cap\mathbb{V}(\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(\phi))\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U\times_{\Bbbk}\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U\times_{\Bbbk}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma}

(155)

which is the scheme theoretic version of the closure of the
U
-sweep of
\{z\in Z_{\min}:\dim\mathrm{Stab}_{U}(z)>k\}
.

We will choose
\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{J})\hookrightarrow X
to be the centre of the blow-up. Condition
WUU
implies that
X\setminus\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{J})\neq\emptyset
, so
\widetilde{X}\neq\emptyset
. When Condition
UU
holds, we have
\mathcal{I}=\mathcal{J}=\mathcal{O}_{X}
and the centre is empty. Blow-ups are constructed locally. Since we will only consider
X^{0}_{\min}
, we only need the centre to be a closed subscheme
C\hookrightarrow X^{0}_{\min}
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
be such that
H^{0}(X,L^{m})
generates
\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{N}}H^{0}(X,L^{mn})
. By the existence of
z\in Z_{\min}
and Lemma
3.6
, there exists
f\in H^{0}(X,L^{m})_{\lambda=\max}
such that
\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(\phi)(X_{f})_{\lambda=0}\not\subseteq\mathrm{rad}(\mathcal{O}(X_{f}))
.

Let
f\in H^{0}(X,L^{m})_{\lambda=\max}
such that
X_{f}\neq\emptyset
. Since
X_{f}
is
U
-invariant and scheme theoretic images are constructed locally, we have that
J_{f}:=\mathcal{J}(X_{f})\subseteq\mathcal{O}(X_{f})
is the kernel of the following composition

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}(X_{f})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}(U)\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}(X_{f})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}(U)\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}(X_{f})/I_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\sigma|_{X_{f}})^{*}}

(156)

where
I_{f}:=\mathcal{I}(X_{f})=\mathcal{O}(X_{f})_{\lambda<0}+\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(\phi)(X_{f})
.

Let
\pi:\widetilde{X}\to X
be the blow-up. Let
E\hookrightarrow\widetilde{X}
be the exceptional divisor. Then
\mathcal{O}(-E)
is relatively ample with respect to
\pi
. For
x\ll y\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, let

\widetilde{L}_{x,y}:=\mathcal{O}(-E)^{\otimes x}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{X}}}\pi^{*}L^{\otimes y}

(157)

be a line bundle on
\widetilde{X}
. Since
x\ll y
we have that
\widetilde{L}_{x,y}
is ample by
Lemma 0892
. All constructions are equivariant under
\hat{U}
, and then there is a natural linearisation

\hat{U}\curvearrowright\big(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{L}_{x,y}\big).

(158)

The
blow-up algebra
associated to the ideal and ring
J_{f}\subseteq\mathcal{O}(X_{f})
is the following graded
\mathcal{O}(X_{f})
-algebra

\mathrm{Bl}_{J_{f}}\big((\mathcal{O}(X_{f})\big):=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{N}}(J_{f})^{n}=\mathcal{O}(X_{f})\oplus J_{f}\oplus(J_{f})^{2}\oplus\cdots

(159)

where the summand
(J_{f})^{n}
has degree
n
. For
a\in(J_{f})^{1}
of degree one, the
affine blow-up algebra
is the homogeneous localisation

\mathcal{O}(X_{f})\Big[\frac{J_{f}}{a}\Big]:=\Big(\mathrm{Bl}_{J_{f}}\big(\mathcal{O}(X_{f})\big)\Big)_{(a)}.

(160)

Denote

\widetilde{X}_{f,a}:=\mathrm{Spec}\Big(\mathcal{O}(X_{f})\Big[\frac{J_{f}}{a}\Big]\Big)\subseteq\widetilde{X}.

(161)

Then
\{\widetilde{X}_{f,a}\}_{f,a}
form an affine open covering of
\widetilde{X}
for
f\in H^{0}(X,L^{m})
and
a\in J_{f}
.
We have

\pi^{-1}(X_{f})=\bigcup_{a\in J_{f}}\widetilde{X}_{f,a}.

(162)

It is easy to see

\widetilde{X}^{0}_{\min}=\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in H^{0}(X,L^{m})_{\lambda=\max}\\
a\in(J_{f})_{\lambda=0}\end{subarray}}\widetilde{X}_{f,a}

(163)

and we can choose the sub-covering
\big\{\widetilde{X}_{f,a}\big\}
for
(f,a)
such that
a\notin\mathrm{rad}(\mathcal{O}(X_{f}))
and
a\in(J_{f})_{\lambda=0}=\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(\phi)(X_{f})_{\lambda=0}
is of the form

a=\det\begin{pmatrix}\xi_{i_{1}}.g_{1}&\cdots&\xi_{i_{1}}.g_{r-k}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\xi_{i_{r-k}}.g_{1}&\cdots&\xi_{i_{r-k}}.g_{r-k}\end{pmatrix}

(164)

for
1\leq i_{1}<\cdots<i_{r-k}\leq r
and
g_{1},\cdots,g_{r-k}\in\mathcal{O}(X_{f})_{\lambda=-w}
. The condition that
a\notin\mathrm{rad}(\mathcal{O}(X_{f}))
implies that
\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(\phi)(X_{f})_{\lambda=0}\not\subseteq\mathrm{rad}(\mathcal{O}(X_{f}))
.

3.4.2
Main theorem

Recall that
U
is an additive group and the induced
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-representation on
\mathfrak{u}
has one weight, denoted
w>0
.

Theorem 3.30
.

For the linearisation
\hat{U}\curvearrowright(X,L)
, assume Condition
WUU
. Then the blow-up
\pi:\widetilde{X}\to X
and the linearisation
\hat{U}\curvearrowright\big(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{L}_{x,y}\big)
for
x\ll y\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
satisfy Condition
UU
.

Proof.

Condition
WUU
implies that there exists
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\mathrm{Fit}_{k-1}(\phi)=0
and
\dim\mathrm{Stab}_{U}(z)=k
for some closed point
z\in Z_{\min}
. We will prove that

\mathrm{Fit}_{k-1}(\widetilde{\phi})|_{\widetilde{X}^{0}_{\min}}=0,\quad\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(\widetilde{\phi})|_{\widetilde{X}^{0}_{\min}}=\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{X}^{0}_{\min}}

(165)

where
\widetilde{\phi}:\Omega_{\widetilde{X}/\Bbbk}\to\mathfrak{u}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{X}}
is the infinitesimal action of
\mathfrak{u}
on
\widetilde{X}
.

We can check the equalities of Fitting ideals locally. Recall that
\widetilde{X}^{0}_{\min}
is covered by
\tilde{X}_{f,a}
for
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
sufficiently divisible,
f\in H^{0}(X,L^{m})_{\lambda=\max}
and non-nilpotent
a\in(J_{f})_{\lambda=0}
of the form

a=\det\begin{pmatrix}\xi_{i_{1}}.g_{1}&\cdots&\xi_{i_{1}}.g_{r-k}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\xi_{i_{r-k}}.g_{1}&\cdots&\xi_{i_{r-k}}.g_{r-k}\end{pmatrix}

(166)

for
1\leq i_{1}<\cdots<i_{r-k}\leq r
and
g_{1},\cdots,g_{r-k}\in\mathcal{O}(X_{f})_{\lambda=-w}
. Without loss of generality, we can assume
(i_{1},\cdots,i_{r-k})=(1,\cdots,r-k)
. We need to show

\mathrm{Fit}_{k-1}(\widetilde{\phi})(\widetilde{X}_{f,a})=0,\quad\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(\widetilde{\phi})(\widetilde{X}_{f,a})=\mathcal{O}(X_{f})\Big[\frac{J_{f}}{a}\Big].

(167)

There is a surjective map
\mathcal{O}(\widetilde{X}_{f,a})\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathrm{d}\big(\frac{J_{f}}{a}\big)\twoheadrightarrow\Omega_{\mathcal{O}(\widetilde{X}_{f,a})/\Bbbk}
. Therefore Fitting ideals of
\mathrm{coker}(\widetilde{\phi})(\widetilde{X}_{f,a})
can be calculated from the following presentation

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}(\widetilde{X}_{f,a})\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathrm{d}\big(\frac{J_{f}}{a}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{u}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}(\widetilde{X}_{f,a})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{coker}(\widetilde{\phi})(\widetilde{X}_{f,a})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}

(168)

For the
(k-1)
th Fitting ideal, we need to show

\det\begin{pmatrix}\zeta_{1}.\frac{h_{1}}{a}&\cdots&\zeta_{1}.\frac{h_{r-k+1}}{a}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\zeta_{r-k+1}.\frac{h_{1}}{a}&\cdots&\zeta_{r-k+1}.\frac{h_{r-k+1}}{a}\end{pmatrix}=0\quad\textrm{in }\mathcal{O}(\widetilde{X}_{f,a})

(169)

for any
h_{1},\cdots,h_{r-k+1}\in J_{f}
and any
\zeta_{1},\cdots,\zeta_{r-k+1}\in\mathfrak{u}
. The above determinant times
a^{r-k+1}
is an element in
\mathrm{Fit}_{k-1}(\phi)(X_{f})=0
. Since
a\in\mathcal{O}(\widetilde{X}_{f,a})
is a non-zerodivisor, the determinant above is zero. This proves
\mathrm{Fit}_{k-1}(\widetilde{\phi})(\widetilde{X}_{f,a})=0
.

For the
k
th Fitting ideal, we first observe that for

b_{i}:=\det\begin{pmatrix}\xi_{1}.g_{1}&\cdots&\xi_{1}.g_{r-k}\\
\vdots&\cdots&\vdots\\
g_{1}&\cdots&g_{r-k}\\
\vdots&\cdots&\vdots\\
\xi_{r-k}.g_{1}&\cdots&\xi_{r-k}.g_{r-k}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathcal{O}(X_{f}),\quad 1\leq i\leq r-k

(170)

where
(g_{1},\cdots,g_{r-k})
occupies the
i
th row, we have the following equations

\xi_{i}.b_{j}=\delta_{ij}a,\quad 1\leq i,j\leq r-k.

(171)

We also have
b_{i}\in J_{f}
since they map to zero along
\mathcal{O}(X_{f})\to\mathcal{O}(U)\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}(X_{f})/I_{f}
. Then
\mathrm{d}\big(\frac{b_{i}}{a}\big)\in\mathrm{d}\big(\frac{J_{f}}{a}\big)
and then the following determinant is in
\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(\widetilde{\phi})(\widetilde{X}_{f,a})

\det\begin{pmatrix}\xi_{1}.\frac{b_{1}}{a}&\cdots&\xi_{1}.\frac{b_{r-k}}{a}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\xi_{r-k}.\frac{b_{1}}{a}&\cdots&\xi_{r-k}.\frac{b_{r-k}}{a}\end{pmatrix}=\det\begin{pmatrix}1&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
0&\cdots&1\end{pmatrix}=1.

(172)

This proves
\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(\widetilde{X}_{f,a})=\mathcal{O}(\widetilde{X}_{f,a})
.
∎

Corollary 3.31
.

For the linearisation
\hat{U}\curvearrowright(X,L)
, assume Condition
WUU
. Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
be the minimal dimension of
\mathrm{Stab}_{U}(x)
for closed points
x\in X^{0}_{\min}
. Let
\phi:\Omega_{X^{0}_{\min}/\Bbbk}\to\mathfrak{u}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{X^{0}_{\min}}
be the infinitesimal action of
\mathfrak{u}
on
X^{0}_{\min}
. Let
\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(\phi)\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{X^{0}_{\min}}
denote the
k
th Fitting ideal of
\mathrm{coker}(\phi)
. Then:

•

the subset
X^{0}_{\min}\setminus\mathbb{V}(\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(\phi))
has a quasi-projective universal geometric quotient by
U
;

•

the subset
X^{0}_{\min}\setminus\mathbb{V}(\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(\phi))\setminus UZ_{\min}
is
\hat{U}
-invariant open in
X^{0}_{\min}
and it has a quasi-projective universal geometric quotient by
\hat{U}
.

Proof.

By Theorem
3.30
, there is
\hat{U}
-equivariant blow-up
\pi:\widetilde{X}\to X
, and a linearisation
\hat{U}\curvearrowright\big(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{L}\big)
which satisfies Condition
UU
. Moreover, the centre of the blow-up is disjoint from
X^{0}_{\min}\setminus\mathbb{V}(\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(\phi))
.

By Theorem
3.24
, we have:

•

\widetilde{X}^{0}_{\min}
has a quasi-projective universal geometric quotient by
U
;

•

\widetilde{X}^{0}_{\min}\setminus U\widetilde{Z}_{\min}
is open in
\widetilde{X}^{0}_{\min}
and it has a projective universal geometric quotient by
\hat{U}
.

We have

\widetilde{X}^{0}_{\min}\cap\pi^{-1}\big(X^{0}_{\min}\setminus\mathbb{V}(\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(\phi))\big)=\pi^{-1}\big(X^{0}_{\min}\setminus\mathbb{V}(\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(\phi))\big)\cong X^{0}_{\min}\setminus\mathbb{V}(\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(\phi)).

(173)

Then
X^{0}_{\min}\setminus\mathbb{V}(\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(\phi))
has a quasi-projective universal geometric quotient by
U
, since it is isomorphic to a
U
-invariant open subset of
\widetilde{X}^{0}_{\min}
.

Similarly, we have

\big(\widetilde{X}^{0}_{\min}\setminus U\widetilde{Z}_{\min}\big)\cap\pi^{-1}\big(X^{0}_{\min}\setminus\mathbb{V}(\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(\phi))\big)=\pi^{-1}\big(X^{0}_{\min}\setminus\mathbb{V}(\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(\phi))\setminus UZ_{\min}\big)\cong X^{0}_{\min}\setminus\mathbb{V}(\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(\phi))\setminus UZ_{\min}.

(174)

Then
X^{0}_{\min}\setminus\mathbb{V}(\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(\phi))\setminus UZ_{\min}
is open and it has a quasi-projective universal geometric quotient by
\hat{U}
.
∎

3.5
Stratification by unipotent stabilisers

Let
U
be an additive group of dimension
r
over
\Bbbk
. Let
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
. Let
U
act on
T
. Let
\phi:\Omega_{T/\Bbbk}\to\mathfrak{u}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}
be the infinitesimal action of
\mathfrak{u}
on
T
.

Consider the closed subschemes

T=Z_{-1}\supset Z_{0}\supset\cdots\supset Z_{r}=\emptyset

(175)

defined by the Fitting ideals of
\mathrm{coker}(\phi)
. Then by
Lemma 05P8
, for each
\delta\in\mathbb{N}
the locally closed subscheme
Z_{\delta-1}\setminus Z_{\delta}\subseteq T
represents the functor
(\mathrm{Sch}/T)^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Set}

(T^{\prime}\to T)\mapsto\begin{cases}\{\mathrm{pt}\}&,\quad\textrm{if }\mathcal{(}T^{\prime}\to T)^{*}\mathrm{coker}(\phi)\textrm{ is locally free of rank }\delta\\
\emptyset&,\quad\textrm{otherwise. }\end{cases}

(176)

For
\delta\in\mathbb{N}
, let
S_{\delta}(T)\subseteq T
denote the locally closed subscheme that is universal with respect to the property that the pullback of
\mathrm{coker}(\phi)
is locally free of rank
\delta
. We call the following morphism the
stratification by unipotent stabilisers

\coprod_{\delta\in\mathbb{N}}S_{\delta}(T)\to T.

(177)

Lemma 3.32
.

Let
f:T_{1}\to T_{2}
be an immersion. Let
U
act on
T_{1},T_{2}
such that
f
is
U
-equivariant. Let
\coprod_{\delta\in\mathbb{N}}S_{\delta}(T_{i})\to T_{i}
be the stratification by unipotent stabilisers for
i=1,2
. Then for all
\delta\in\mathbb{N}
, the following diagram is Cartesian

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\delta}(T_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\delta}(T_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{2}.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

(178)

Proof.

Let
\phi_{i}:\Omega_{T_{i}/\Bbbk}\to\mathfrak{u}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T_{i}}
denote the infinitesimal action of
\mathfrak{u}:=\mathrm{Lie}(U)
on
T_{i}
for
i=1,2
. Recall that
S_{\delta}(T_{i})\to T_{i}
is universal with respect to the property that the pullback of
\mathrm{coker}(\phi_{i})
is locally free of rank
\delta
. By Lemma
3.3
, we have
f^{*}\mathrm{coker}(\phi_{2})\cong\mathrm{coker}(\phi_{1})
. It is then clear that the diagram is Cartesian.
∎

Let
\hat{U}\curvearrowright(X,L)
. Assume Condition
3.14
. We will apply the above stratification to the
U
-action on
X^{0}_{\min}
, and construct quotients of
S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})
for strata such that
S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})\cap Z_{\min}\neq\emptyset
.

Theorem 3.33
.

Let
\hat{U}\curvearrowright(X,L)
. Assume Condition
3.14
. Let
\coprod_{\delta\in\mathbb{N}}S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})\to X^{0}_{\min}
be the stratification by unipotent stabilisers of
U\curvearrowright X^{0}_{\min}
. Then for each
\delta\in\mathbb{N}
, we have that:

•

S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})\setminus UZ_{\min}\subseteq S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})
is an open subset in the stratum;

•

If
S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})\cap Z_{\min}\neq\emptyset
, then
S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})
has a universal quasi-projective geometric quotient by
U
;

•

If
S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})\cap Z_{\min}\neq\emptyset
, then
S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})\setminus UZ_{\min}
is open in
S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})
and it has a universal quasi-projective geometric quotient by
\hat{U}
.

Proof.

Fix
\delta\in\mathbb{N}
. Assume
S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})\neq\emptyset
. Let
\phi:\Omega_{X^{0}_{\min}/\Bbbk}\to\mathfrak{u}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{X^{0}_{\min}}
be the infinitesimal action of
\mathfrak{u}
on
X^{0}_{\min}
. Let
Z_{k}(\mathrm{coker}(\phi))\subseteq X^{0}_{\min}
denote the closed subscheme associated to
\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(\phi)\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{X^{0}_{\min}}
for every
k\in\mathbb{N}
. Then
S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})=Z_{\delta-1}(\mathrm{coker}(\phi))\setminus Z_{\delta}(\mathrm{coker}(\phi))
.

When
S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})\cap Z_{\min}=\emptyset
, we have that
S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})\cap UZ_{\min}=\emptyset
, since
S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})
is
U
-invariant. Therefore
S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})\setminus UZ_{\min}=S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})
is open in
S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})
.

Assume
S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})\cap Z_{\min}\neq\emptyset
. For simplicity write
Z_{\delta-1}:=Z_{\delta-1}(\mathrm{coker}(\phi))
. Let
\overline{Z_{\delta-1}}\hookrightarrow X
be the scheme theoretic image of
Z_{\delta-1}\hookrightarrow X^{0}_{\min}\subseteq X
. Consider
\hat{U}\curvearrowright\big(\overline{Z_{\delta-1}},L\big|_{\overline{Z_{\delta-1}}}\big)
. It is easy to see the following equalities of subschemes:

(\overline{Z_{\delta-1}})^{0}_{\min}=Z_{\delta-1},\quad\big((\overline{Z_{\delta-1}})^{0}_{\min}\big)^{\lambda}=Z_{\delta-1}\cap Z_{\min}.

(179)

Then for the open subset
S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})
of
(\overline{Z_{\delta-1}})^{0}_{\min}=Z_{\delta-1}
we have:

•

S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})\cap\big((\overline{Z_{\delta-1}})^{0}_{\min}\big)^{\lambda}=S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})\cap Z_{\min}\neq\emptyset
;

•

the cokernel of the infinitesimal action of
\mathfrak{u}
on
S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})
is locally free, since this cokernel is isomorphic to
\mathrm{coker}(\phi)\big|_{S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})}
by Lemma
3.3
.

These two conditions above imply Condition
WUU
for
\hat{U}\curvearrowright\big(\overline{Z_{\delta-1}},L\big|_{\overline{Z_{\delta-1}}}\big)
. We can apply Corollary
3.31
. The subsets in Corollary
3.31
are

Z_{\delta}\Big\backslash\mathbb{V}\Big(\mathrm{Fit}_{\delta}\big(\mathrm{coker}(\phi)\big|_{Z_{\delta-1}}\big)\Big)=S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})

(180)

and

Z_{\delta-1}\Big\backslash\mathbb{V}\Big(\mathrm{Fit}_{\delta}\big(\mathrm{coker}(\phi)|_{Z_{\delta-1}}\big)\Big)\Big\backslash U(Z_{\delta-1}\cap Z_{\min})=S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})\setminus UZ_{\min}.

(181)

The statements then follow from Corollary
3.31
.
∎

We can prove a result similar to Theorem
3.29
.

Theorem 3.34
.

Let
U\rtimes(T\times K)\curvearrowright(X,L)
. Assume Condition
3.14
for
\hat{U}\curvearrowright(X,L)
, where
\hat{U}:=U\rtimes T
. Let
Z_{\min}^{K,\mathrm{s}}\subseteq Z_{\min}
denote the
K
-stable locus of
K\curvearrowright\big(Z_{\min},L\big|_{Z_{\min}}\big)
. Let
p_{\lambda}:X^{0}_{\min}\to Z_{\min}
be the retraction. Let
\coprod_{\delta\in\mathbb{N}}S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})\to X^{0}_{\min}
be the stratification by unipotent stabilisers of
U\curvearrowright X^{0}_{\min}
. Let
\delta\in\mathbb{N}
. If
S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})\cap Z_{\min}\neq\emptyset
, then the invariant open subset of
S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})

\big(S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})\setminus UZ_{\min}\big)\cap p_{\lambda}^{-1}\big(Z_{\min}^{K,\mathrm{s}}\big)

(182)

has a quasi-projective universal geometric quotient by
U\rtimes(T\times K)
.

Proof.

The openness is from Theorem
3.33
. In the proof of Theorem
3.33
, we have seen that
\hat{U}\curvearrowright\big(\overline{Z_{\delta-1}},L\big|_{\overline{Z_{\delta-1}}}\big)
satisfies Condition
WUU
. By Theorem
3.30
, there is an equivariant blow-up of
\overline{Z_{\delta-1}}
and a linearisation such that Condition
UU
is satisfied.

The subset
\big(S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})\setminus UZ_{\min}\big)\cap p_{\lambda}^{-1}\big(Z_{\min}^{K,\mathrm{s}}\big)
is open in
\overline{Z_{\delta-1}}
and it is disjoint from the blow-up centre. So we can think it as an open subset of the blow-up. By Theorem
3.29
, there is an open subset of the blow-up which has a quasi-projective universal geometric quotient by
U\rtimes(T\times K)
. It is easy to see this open subset contains
\big(S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})\setminus UZ_{\min}\big)\cap p_{\lambda}^{-1}\big(Z_{\min}^{K,\mathrm{s}}\big)
. So
\big(S_{\delta}(X^{0}_{\min})\setminus UZ_{\min}\big)\cap p_{\lambda}^{-1}\big(Z_{\min}^{K,\mathrm{s}}\big)
has a quasi-projective universal geometric quotient by
U\rtimes(T\times K)
.
∎

4
Sheaves of length 2

Let
\tau
be a Harder-Narasimhan type of length 2. Let
P(t),P_{i}(t)\in\mathbb{Q}[t]
for
1\leq i\leq 2
be the Hilbert polynomials associated to
\tau
, in the sense that if
\mathcal{E}
is a sheaf of type
\tau
, then its Harder-Narasimhan filtration

0=\mathcal{E}_{\leq 0}\subsetneq\mathcal{E}_{\leq 1}\subsetneq\mathcal{E}_{\leq 2}=\mathcal{E}

(183)

has length 2,
P(t)
is the Hilbert polynomial of
\mathcal{E}
and
P_{i}(t)
is the Hilbert polynomial of
\mathcal{E}_{i}:=\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}/\mathcal{E}_{\leq i-1}
for
i=1,2
. Recall that
B
is a connected projective scheme over
\Bbbk
and
\mathcal{L}
is an ample line bundle on
B
. We will assume that there exists a
(\tau,\delta)
-stable sheaf for some
\delta\in\mathbb{N}
.

Recall
\mathrm{P}_{\tau}\curvearrowright\big(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}},\mathcal{M}\big|_{\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}}\big)
described at the end of Section
2.2.2
. Let
\mathrm{U}_{\tau}\subseteq\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
be the unipotent radical and let
\mathrm{K}_{\tau}\subseteq\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
be its semisimple part
\mathrm{K}_{\tau}=\prod_{i=1}^{2}\mathrm{SL}(V_{i})
. Let
\mathrm{T}_{\tau}=\mathbb{G}_{m}
and let
\mathrm{T}_{\tau}\to\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
be the following homomorphism

t\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}t^{\frac{P_{2}}{(P_{1},P_{2})}}1_{V_{1}}\\
&t^{\frac{-P_{1}}{(P_{1},P_{2})}}1_{V_{2}}\end{pmatrix}

(184)

where
P_{i}:=P_{i}(m)=\dim V_{i}
and
(P_{1},P_{2}):=\mathrm{gcd}(P_{1},P_{2})
. Then
\mathrm{U}_{\tau}\rtimes(\mathrm{T}_{\tau}\times\mathrm{K}_{\tau})\to\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
is surjective with finite kernel.

Consider the linearisation

\mathrm{U}_{\tau}\rtimes(\mathrm{T}_{\tau}\times\mathrm{K}_{\tau})\curvearrowright\big(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}},\mathcal{M}\big|_{\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}}\big).

(185)

By Lemma
3.32
we have

(1)

S_{\delta}\big((\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}})^{0}_{\min}\big)\cap Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}=S_{\delta}(Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}})
for all
\delta\in\mathbb{N}
, where
\coprod_{\delta\in\mathbb{N}}S_{\delta}(Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}})\to Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}
is the stratification by unipotent stabilisers.

By Proposition
A.2
we have

(2)

p_{\tau}^{-1}\big(Z_{\min}^{\mathrm{K}_{\tau},\mathrm{s}}(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}})\big)=Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}
.

If
S_{\delta}\big((\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}})^{0}_{\min}\big)\cap Z_{\min}(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}})\neq\emptyset
, then by Theorem
3.33
the following subscheme is invariant open in
S_{\delta}\big((\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}})^{0}_{\min}\big)
and it has a quasi-projective geometric quotient by
\mathrm{U}_{\tau}\rtimes(\mathrm{T}_{\tau}\times\mathrm{K}_{\tau})

\begin{split}&\big(S_{\delta}\big((\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}})^{0}_{\min}\big)\big\backslash\mathrm{U}_{\tau}Z_{\min}(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}})\big)\cap p_{\tau}^{-1}\big(Z_{\min}^{\mathrm{K}_{\tau},\mathrm{s}}(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}})\big)\\
=&\Big(S_{\delta}\big((\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}})^{0}_{\min}\big)\cap p_{\tau}^{-1}\big(Z_{\min}^{\mathrm{K}_{\tau},\mathrm{s}}(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}})\big)\Big)\Big\backslash\mathrm{U}_{\tau}Z_{\min}(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}})\\
\overset{(2)}{=\!=}&\Big(S_{\delta}\big((\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}})^{0}_{\min}\big)\cap Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}\Big)\Big\backslash\mathrm{U}_{\tau}Z_{\min}(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}})\\
\overset{(1)}{=\!=}&S_{\delta}(Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}})\big\backslash\mathrm{U}_{\tau}Z_{\min}(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}).\end{split}

(186)

Denote

Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}:=S_{\delta}(Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}})\setminus\mathrm{U}_{\tau}Z_{\min}(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}).

(187)

Then we have a quasi-projective geometric quotient by
\mathrm{P}_{\tau}

Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}\to Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}/\mathrm{P}_{\tau}.

(188)

The condition
S_{\delta}\big((\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}})^{0}_{\min}\big)\cap Z_{\min}(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}})\neq\emptyset
is satisfied if
Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}\neq\emptyset
by Lemma
2.13
. The condition
Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}\neq\emptyset
is equivalent to the existence of a
(\tau,\delta)
-stable sheaf on
B
. We will always assume
(\tau,\delta)
is such that there exists a
(\tau,\delta)
-stable sheaf.

Similar to
Y_{\tau}
and
Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}
, the schemes
S_{\delta}(Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}})
and
Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}
also have moduli-theoretic descriptions. We will describe their functors of points (Lemma
4.2
) in Section
4.1
. The relation between
\mathrm{P}_{\tau}\curvearrowright Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}
and the moduli functor
\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta}
(Lemma
4.3
) will be discussed in Section
4.2
.

4.1
Unipotent endomorphisms

Let
\mathrm{U}_{\tau}\subseteq\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
be the unipotent radical and let
\mathfrak{u}
denote its Lie algebra. Then
\mathfrak{u}:=\mathrm{Hom}_{\Bbbk}(V_{2},V_{1})
.

Let
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
. Recall that a morphism
T\to Y^{\mathrm{s}}_{\tau}
is equivalent to an isomorphism class
[(\mathcal{E},q)]
such that

q:V(-m)_{T}\to\mathcal{E}

(189)

is surjective, each
\mathcal{E}_{i}
if flat over
T
with Hilbert polynomial
P_{i}(t)
, and fibres of each
\mathcal{E}_{i}
at closed points of
T
are stable, where
\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}:=q\big(V_{\leq i}(-m)_{T}\big)
and
\mathcal{E}_{i}:=\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}/\mathcal{E}_{\leq i-1}
.

Recall Definition
3.8
and Definition
3.9
for scheme-valued infinitesimal transformations and when they stabilise scheme-valued points.

Lemma 4.1
.

When
m\gg 0
, we have the following commutative diagram of
\Gamma(T,\mathcal{O}_{T})
-modules for any
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
and any
T
-valued point
[(\mathcal{E},q)]
in
Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Big\{\begin{matrix}T\textrm{-valued infinitesimal}\\
\textrm{transformations in }\mathrm{U}_{\tau}\end{matrix}\Big\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{T}}\big(V_{2}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T},V_{1}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left\{\begin{matrix}\rho:\rho\textrm{ is a }T\textrm{-valued}\\
\textrm{infinitesimal}\\
\textrm{transformation in }\mathrm{U}_{\tau}\\
\textrm{and }\rho\textrm{ stabilises }[(\mathcal{E},q)]\end{matrix}\right\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{B_{T}}}(\mathcal{E}_{2},\mathcal{E}_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}

(190)

where the horizontal maps are isomorphisms and the vertical maps are inclusions. Moreover, the top map is natural in
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
and the bottom map is natural in
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}
.

Proof.

Let
f:B\to\mathrm{Spec}(\Bbbk)
denote the structure morphism. For
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
, let
f_{T}:B_{T}\to T
denote the base change of
f
. For a coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on
B_{T}
, let
\mathcal{F}(m)
denote
\mathcal{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{B_{T}}}\mathcal{L}_{T}^{m}
. For the right vertical arrow, let
\varphi:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}
be
\mathcal{O}_{B_{T}}
-linear such that
\varphi(\mathcal{E}_{\leq i})\subseteq\mathcal{E}_{\leq i-1}
for
i=1,2
.

There is an exact sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{B_{T}}}(\mathcal{E}_{2},\mathcal{E}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{B_{T}}}(\mathcal{E}_{\leq 2},\mathcal{E}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{B_{T}}}(\mathcal{E}_{\leq 1},\mathcal{E}_{1}).}

(191)

We can think
\varphi\in\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{B_{T}}}(\mathcal{E}_{2},\mathcal{E}_{1})
as a morphism
\varphi:\mathcal{E}_{\leq 2}\to\mathcal{E}_{1}
such that
\varphi(\mathcal{E}_{\leq 1})=0
. Consider the diagram on
B_{T}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\leq 2}(-m)_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{1}(-m)_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{\leq 2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{1}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{\leq 2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}

(192)

Apply the functor
\mathcal{F}\mapsto\mathcal{F}(m)\mapsto f_{T,*}(\mathcal{F}(m))
to the diagram above to obtain the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\leq 2}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{1}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{T,*}(\mathcal{E}_{\leq 2}(m))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{T,*}(\mathcal{E}_{1}(m)).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{T,*}(q_{\leq 2}(m))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{T,*}(q_{1}(m))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{T,*}(\varphi(m))}

(193)

When
m\gg 0
is large, we have

•

f_{T,*}(\mathcal{E}_{j}(m))
is locally free of rank
P_{j}(m)
for
j=1,2
;

•

f_{T,*}(\mathcal{E}_{\leq j}(m))
is locally free of rank
P_{\leq j}(m)
for
j=1,2

for all
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
and
[(\mathcal{E},q)]
. So
f_{T,*}(q_{1}(m))
is a morphism between locally free sheaves of the same rank
P_{1}(m)
. For any closed point
\{t\}\hookrightarrow T
, the morphism
f_{T,*}(q_{1}(m))
pulls back to an isomorphism
V_{1}\cong H^{0}(B,\mathcal{E}_{1}(m)|_{B_{t}})
. By
Lemma 00O0
, we have that
f_{T,*}(q_{1}(m))
is an isomorphism since its pullbacks at closed points are isomorphisms. Similarly
f_{T,*}(q_{\leq 2}(m))
is an isomorphism. Therefore the morphism
\eta:V_{\leq 2}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}\to V_{1}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}
exists in diagram (
193
). Since
\varphi(\mathcal{E}_{\leq 1})=0
, we have
\eta(V_{\leq 1}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T})=0
, i.e.
\eta
defines a morphism
\bar{\eta}:V_{2}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}\to V_{1}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}
. The map
\varphi\mapsto\bar{\eta}
defines the right vertical map in (
190
).

The right vertical map in diagram (
190
) is injective, since if
\varphi\mapsto\bar{\eta}
, then
\varphi,\bar{\eta}
satisfy the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V(-m)_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V(-m)_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta(-m)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}

(194)

where
\eta
is the composition
V_{\leq 2}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}\to V_{2}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}\xrightarrow{\bar{\eta}}V_{1}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}
and
\eta(-m):=\big((f_{T})^{*}\eta\big)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{B_{T}}}1_{\mathcal{L}_{T}^{-m}}
.

For the top map in diagram (
190
), let
\rho:T[\epsilon]\to\mathrm{U}_{\tau}
be a
T
-valued infinitesimal transformation in
\mathrm{U}_{\tau}
. A morphism
\rho:T[\epsilon]\to\mathrm{U}_{\tau}
is equivalent to a morphism of coherent sheaves on
T[\epsilon]

u:V\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T[\epsilon]}\to V\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T[\epsilon]}

(195)

such that:

(1)

u(V_{\leq i}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T[\epsilon]})\subseteq V_{\leq i}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T[\epsilon]}
, and we denote
u_{\leq i}:V_{\leq i}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T[\epsilon]}\to V_{\leq i}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T[\epsilon]}
;

(2)

the induced morphism
u_{i}:V_{i}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T[\epsilon]}\to V_{i}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T[\epsilon]}
is the identity

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\leq i-1}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T[\epsilon]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\leq i}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T[\epsilon]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{i}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T[\epsilon]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\leq i-1}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T[\epsilon]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\leq i}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T[\epsilon]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{i}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T[\epsilon]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{\leq i-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{\leq i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{i}=1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}

(196)

Note that the underlying topological spaces of
T
and
T[\epsilon]
are the same, and
\mathcal{O}_{T[\epsilon]}\cong\mathcal{O}_{T}\oplus\epsilon\mathcal{O}_{T}
as
\mathcal{O}_{T}
-modules. The morphism
u:V\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T[\epsilon]}\to V\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T[\epsilon]}
is equivalent to two
\mathcal{O}_{T}
-linear morphisms

\mu:V\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}\to V\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T},\quad\eta:V\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}\to V\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}

(197)

in the sense that according to the decomposition
V\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T[\epsilon]}\cong(V\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T})\oplus\epsilon(V\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T})
, we have

u=\begin{pmatrix}\mu&\\
\epsilon\eta&\mu_{\epsilon}\end{pmatrix}

(198)

where
\mu_{\epsilon}:\epsilon V\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}\to\epsilon V\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}
is the morphism equivalent to
\mu
.

For a morphism
\rho:T[\epsilon]\to\mathrm{U}_{\tau}
to be an infinitesimal transformation, the diagram
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T[\epsilon]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Spec}(\Bbbk)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{U}_{\tau}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
has to commute, which is equivalent to the following condition for
u
:

(3)

\mu=1_{V\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}}
, i.e.
u=\begin{pmatrix}1&\\
\epsilon\eta&1_{\epsilon}\end{pmatrix}
.

Condition
(3)
for
u
says the map
\rho\mapsto\eta
is injective when
\rho
is a
T
-valued infinitesimal transformation in
\mathrm{U}_{\tau}
. With
(3)
, condition
(1)
and
(2)
together for
u
are equivalent to the following

\eta(V_{\leq i}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T})\subseteq V_{\leq i-1}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{T},\quad i=1,2.

(199)

Such
\eta
is equivalent to
\bar{\eta}:V_{2}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}\to V_{1}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}
. Therefore the association
\rho\mapsto\bar{\eta}
defines the top map in diagram (
190
), which is bijective from its construction.

For the bottom map in diagram (
190
), let
\rho:T[\epsilon]\to\mathrm{U}_{\tau}
be a
T
-valued infinitesimal transformation in
\mathrm{U}_{\tau}
and assume
\rho
is mapped to
\bar{\eta}:V_{2}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}\to V_{1}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}
along the top map in diagram (
190
). Let
\eta:V\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}\to V\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}
be the composition
V\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}\twoheadrightarrow V_{2}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}\xrightarrow{\bar{\eta}}V_{1}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}\subseteq V\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}
. We have
\eta(V_{\leq i}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T})\subseteq V_{\leq i-1}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}
for
i=1,2
.

Let
h:T\to Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}
be the morphism corresponding to
[(\mathcal{E},q)]
. Then
\rho:T[\epsilon]\to\mathrm{U}_{\tau}
stabilises
[(\mathcal{E},q)]
if and only if the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T[\epsilon]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{U}_{\tau}\times_{\Bbbk}Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\rho,h\circ\mathrm{pr}_{T})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma}

(200)

where
\sigma:\mathrm{U}_{\tau}\times_{\Bbbk}Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}\to Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}
is the action morphism of
\mathrm{U}_{\tau}\curvearrowright Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}
. There are two families parametrised by
T[\epsilon]
of quotients corresponding to two morphisms
h\circ\mathrm{pr}_{T}
and
\sigma\circ(\rho,h\circ\mathrm{pr}_{T})
. The diagram above commutes if and only if
h\circ\mathrm{pr}_{T}=\sigma\circ(\rho,h\circ\mathrm{pr}_{T})
, if and only if the two families over
T[\epsilon]
are equivalent.

The family over
T[\epsilon]
corresponding to
h\circ\mathrm{pr}_{T}
is the following
