{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V(-m)_{T}\oplus\epsilon V(-m)_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}\oplus\epsilon\mathcal{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}q&\\
&q_{\epsilon}\end{pmatrix}}

(201)

where
q_{\epsilon}:\epsilon V(-m)_{T}\to\epsilon\mathcal{E}
is equivalent to
q
. The family corresponding to
\sigma\circ(\rho,h\circ\mathrm{pr}_{T})
is the composition

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V(-m)_{T}\oplus\epsilon V(-m)_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V(-m)_{T}\oplus\epsilon V(-m)_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}\oplus\epsilon\mathcal{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}1&\\
-\epsilon\eta(-m)&1_{\epsilon}\end{pmatrix}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}q&\\
&q_{\epsilon}\end{pmatrix}}

(202)

where
\eta(-m):V(-m)_{T}\to V(-m)_{T}
denotes
\eta(-m):=\big((f_{T})^{*}\eta\big)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{B_{T}}}1_{\mathcal{L}_{T}^{-m}}
. It is easy to see the two families are equivalent if and only if
\eta(-m)(\ker q)\subseteq\ker q
, which is equivalent to the existence of
\varphi:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}
such that the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V(-m)_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V(-m)_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta(-m)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}

(203)

which is equivalent to that
\bar{\eta}
is in the image of the right vertical map in diagram (
190
), i.e. the association
\rho\mapsto\varphi
defines the bottom map in diagram (
190
).

We have now defined all the maps in diagram (
190
), with the horizontal maps being bijective and the vertical maps being injective. It is easy to check that they are all
\Gamma(T,\mathcal{O}_{T})
-linear and natural in
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
and
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}
respectively.
∎

Recall that
Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}
is defined in (
187
), and
f:B\to\mathrm{Spec}(\Bbbk)
is the structure morphism. We describe the functors of points of
Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}\subseteq S_{\delta}(Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}})
.

Lemma 4.2
.

The scheme
S_{\delta}(Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}})
represents the functor which sends
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
to the set of isomorphism classes
[(\mathcal{E},q)]
such that:

(1)

q:V(-m)_{T}\to\mathcal{E}
is a surjective morphism of coherent sheaves on
B_{T}
, and
\mathcal{E}_{i}
is flat over
T
with Hilbert polynomial
P_{i}(t)
for
i=1,2
;

(2)

fibres of
\mathcal{E}_{i}
at closed points are stable for
i=1,2
;

(3)

for any closed subscheme
T^{\prime}\hookrightarrow T
, the coherent sheaf

f_{T^{\prime},*}\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{O}_{B_{T^{\prime}}}}\big(\mathcal{E}_{2}|_{B_{T^{\prime}}},\mathcal{E}_{1}|_{B_{T^{\prime}}}\big)

(204)

is locally free of rank
\delta
on
T^{\prime}
.

The open subscheme
Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}\subseteq S_{\delta}(Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}})
represents the sub-functor which sends
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
to the set of isomorphism classes
[(\mathcal{E},q)]
such that
(1),(2),(3)
above hold and:

(4)

fibres of
\mathcal{E}
at closed points
t\in T
are non-split, in the sense that
\mathcal{E}|_{B_{t}}\not\cong\mathcal{E}_{1}|_{B_{t}}\oplus\mathcal{E}_{2}|_{B_{t}}
.

Proof.

Recall in Section
2.2.2
, the schemes
Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}\subseteq Y_{\tau}
are defined by functors of points. Let
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
. Then
(1)
is equivalent to that
[(\mathcal{E},q)]
corresponds to a
T
-valued point in
Y_{\tau}
, and
(2)
is equivalent to that
[(\mathcal{E},q)]
corresponds to a
T
-valued point in
Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}
.

By definition of the stratification by unipotent stabilisers, the morphism
T\to Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}
factors through
S_{\delta}(Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}})
if and only if
\mathrm{coker}(\phi)|_{T}
is locally free of rank
\delta
, where
\phi:\Omega_{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}/\Bbbk}\to\mathfrak{u}^{*}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}
is the infinitesimal action. By Lemma
3.5
and Lemma
3.12
this is further equivalent to

(3’)

for any closed subscheme
T^{\prime}\hookrightarrow T
, the following defines a locally free sheaf of rank
\delta
on
T^{\prime}

\begin{split}W^{\prime}&\mapsto\Big\{\begin{matrix}\rho:\rho\textrm{ is a }W^{\prime}\textrm{-valued infinitesimal transformation in }\mathrm{U}_{\tau}\\
\textrm{and }\rho\textrm{ stabilises }W^{\prime}\subseteq T^{\prime}\hookrightarrow T\to Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}\end{matrix}\Big\}\\
&\textrm{for open subsets }W^{\prime}\subseteq T^{\prime}.\end{split}

(205)

By Lemma
4.1
, the sheaf in
(3^{\prime})
is isomorphic to the following sheaf on
T^{\prime}

\begin{split}W^{\prime}\mapsto&\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{B_{W^{\prime}}}}\big(\mathcal{E}_{2}|_{B_{W^{\prime}}},\mathcal{E}_{1}|_{B_{W^{\prime}}}\big)\\
\cong&\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{O}_{B_{T^{\prime}}}}\big(\mathcal{E}_{2}|_{B_{T^{\prime}}},\mathcal{E}_{1}|_{B_{T^{\prime}}}\big)\big((f_{T^{\prime}})^{-1}(W^{\prime})\big)\\
=&\Big(f_{T^{\prime},*}\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{O}_{B_{T^{\prime}}}}\big(\mathcal{E}_{2}|_{B_{T^{\prime}}},\mathcal{E}_{1}|_{B_{T^{\prime}}}\big)\Big)(W^{\prime}).\end{split}

(206)

So
(3)
and
(3^{\prime})
are equivalent. This proves the moduli description of
S_{\delta}(Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}})
.

We have that
Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}\subseteq S_{\delta}(Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}})
is an open subscheme, since
Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}
is open in a larger scheme
S_{\delta}\big((\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}})^{0}_{\min}\big)
by Theorem
3.33
. The morphism
T\to S_{\delta}(Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}})
factors through
Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}
if and only if any closed point
t\in T
is mapped into
Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}
, which is equivalent to:

(4’)

fibres of
\mathcal{E}
at closed points
t\in T
, denoted by
\mathcal{E}|_{B_{t}}
, satisfy the condition that the following two closed points in
Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}
are not in one
\mathrm{U}_{\tau}
-orbit

\big[\big(\mathcal{E}|_{B_{t}},q|_{B_{t}}\big)\big],\quad\Big[\Big(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{2}\mathcal{E}_{i}|_{B_{t}},\;\bigoplus_{i=1}^{2}q_{i}|_{B_{t}}\Big)\Big]\in Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}.

(207)

If
(4)\iff(4^{\prime})
, then the moduli description of
Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}
is proved, hence the lemma.

Obviously we have
(4)\implies(4^{\prime})
. Conversely, it suffices to prove that if
[(\mathcal{E},q)]\in Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}
is a closed point such that
\mathcal{E}\cong\bigoplus_{i=1}^{2}\mathcal{E}_{i}
, then
\Big[\Big(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{2}\mathcal{E}_{i},\bigoplus_{i=1}^{2}q_{i}\Big)\Big]
is in the
\mathrm{U}_{\tau}
-orbit of
[(\mathcal{E},q)]
. Let
[(\mathcal{E},q)]\in Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}
be such a closed point. Then the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{1}(-m)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V(-m)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{2}(-m)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{E}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{1}(-m)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V(-m)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{2}(-m)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{1}\oplus q_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{2}}

(208)

Apply the functor
\mathcal{F}\mapsto\mathcal{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{B}}\mathcal{L}^{m}\mapsto f_{*}(\mathcal{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{B}}\mathcal{L}^{m})
to the diagram above, and we have the following

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}(\mathcal{E}_{1}(m))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}(\mathcal{E}(m))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}(\mathcal{E}_{2}(m))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}(\mathcal{E}_{1}(m))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}(\mathcal{E}_{1}(m))\oplus f_{*}(\mathcal{E}_{2}(m))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}(\mathcal{E}_{2}(m))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}(q_{1}(m))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}(q(m))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}(q_{2}(m))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}(q_{1}(m))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}(q_{1}(m))\oplus f_{*}(q_{2}(m))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}(q_{2}(m))}

(209)

where
\mathcal{E}(m):=\mathcal{E}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{B}}\mathcal{L}^{m}
and
q(m):=q\otimes 1_{\mathcal{L}^{m}}
and similarly for
\mathcal{E}_{i}(m)
,
q_{i}(m)
. We denote by
\mu:f_{*}(\mathcal{E}(m))\to f_{*}(\mathcal{E}_{1}(m))\oplus f_{*}(\mathcal{E}_{2}(m))
the map induced from
\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{E}_{2}
. We have that
f_{*}(q(m))
and
f_{*}(q_{i}(m))
are isomorphisms.

Let
g:V\to V
be the composition

g:=\big(f_{*}(q_{1}(m))^{-1}\oplus f_{*}(q_{2}(m))^{-1}\big)\circ\mu\circ f_{*}(q(m)).

(210)

Then
g\in\mathrm{GL}(V)
and the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V(-m)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V(-m)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{E}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g\otimes 1_{\mathcal{L}^{-m}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{1}\oplus q_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

(211)

i.e.
g.[(\mathcal{E},q)]=\Big[\Big(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{2}\mathcal{E}_{i},\bigoplus_{i=1}^{2}q_{i}\Big)\Big]
. Finally we have
g\in\mathrm{U}_{\tau}
, since the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

(212)

This proves
(4)\iff(4^{\prime})
.
∎

4.2
The moduli functor
\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta}

Recall that
(\tau,\delta)
-stability is defined in Definition
2.10
and families of
(\tau,\delta)
-stable sheaves are defined in Definition
2.3
. Recall that
f:B\to\mathrm{Spec}(\Bbbk)
is the structure morphism.

Recall that the moduli functor
\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta}
is defined in section
2.1
which sends
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
to the set

\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta}(T):=\Big\{\begin{matrix}\textrm{isomorphism classes of flat families}\\
\textrm{over }T\textrm{ of }(\tau,\delta)\textrm{-stable sheaves on }B\end{matrix}\Big\}.

(213)

Let
m\gg 0
. Consider
Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}
defined in (
187
). The Yoneda functor is a fully faithful embedding
\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk\to\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk)
, and we will not distinguish between a scheme and its functor of points. We will define a
\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
-invariant natural transformation

\rho:Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}\to\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta}.

(214)

Let
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
, and let
T\to Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}
be a morphism. By Lemma
4.2
, the morphism
T\to Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}
corresponds to an isomorphism class
[(\mathcal{E},q)]
satisfying
(1)-(4)
in Lemma
4.2
. Then the isomorphism class of
\mathcal{E}
is an element in
\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta}(T)
. This defines a map

\rho_{T}:\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk}(T,Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}})\to\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta}(T)

(215)

It is easy to see
\rho_{T}
is
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk}(T,\mathrm{P}_{\tau})
-invariant and it is natural in
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
. This defines a
\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
-invariant natural transformation
\rho:Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}\to\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta}
.

Consider the quotient presheaf

Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}/_{p}\mathrm{P}_{\tau}:(\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk)^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Set},\quad T\mapsto\frac{\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk}(T,Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}})}{\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk}(T,\mathrm{P}_{\tau})}

(216)

where
/_{p}
indicates it is a quotient presheaf. Since
\rho:Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}\to\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta}
is
\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
-invariant, it factors through
Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}/_{p}\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
, i.e. there exists a unique natural transformation
\theta
such that the following diagram in
\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk)
commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}/_{p}\mathrm{P}_{\tau}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varpi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}

(217)

Lemma 4.3
.

When
m\gg 0
is large, the natural transformation
\theta:Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}/_{p}\mathrm{P}_{\tau}\to\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta}
satisfies:

(1)

\theta:Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}/_{p}\mathrm{P}_{\tau}\to\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta}
is injective.

(2)

For any
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
and any
x\in\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta}(T)
, there exists an open covering
T=\bigcup_{j\in J}T_{j}
such that
x|_{T_{j}}\in\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta}(T_{j})
is in the image of
\theta_{T_{j}}:\big(Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}/_{p}\mathrm{P}_{\tau}\big)(T_{j})\to\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta}(T_{j})
for each
j\in J
, where
x|_{T_{j}}
is the pullback of
x
along
T_{j}\to T
.

Moreover, the morphism
\theta
induces an isomorphism of sheaves on
(\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk)_{\acute{e}tale}

\theta^{\sharp}:\big(Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}/_{p}\mathrm{P}_{\tau}\big)^{\sharp}\cong(\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta})^{\sharp}

(218)

where
♯
refers to the sheafification with respect to the étale topology.

Proof.

For
i=1,2
the collection of stable sheaves of Hilbert polynomial
P_{i}(t)
on
B
is bounded and then regularities of such stable sheaves are bounded. Let
m\gg 0
be larger than the regularity of any stable sheaf of Hilbert polynomial in
\{P_{1}(t),P_{2}(t)\}
. We will prove the lemma for this
m
. For
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
, recall that
f_{T}:B_{T}\to T
is the projection. Denote
\mathcal{E}(m):=\mathcal{E}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{B_{T}}}\mathcal{L}_{T}^{m}
. For any morphism
T^{\prime}\to T
in
\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
, denote by
\mathcal{E}|_{B_{T^{\prime}}}
the pullback of
\mathcal{E}
along
B_{T^{\prime}}\to B_{T}
.

For
(1)
, let
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
. It suffices to prove that if
\rho_{T}(h)=\rho_{T}(h^{\prime})
for two elements
h,h^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk}(T,Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}})
, then
h,h^{\prime}
are in one
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk}(T,\mathrm{P}_{\tau})
-orbit. By Lemma
4.2
, elements in
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk}(T,Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}})
correspond to isomorphism classes
[(\mathcal{E},q)]
satisfying
(1)-(4)
in Lemma
4.2
. Let
[(\mathcal{E},q)]
and
[(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},q^{\prime})]
be two isomorphism classes corresponding to
h
and
h^{\prime}
respectively

q:V(-m)_{T}\to\mathcal{E},\quad q^{\prime}:V(-m)_{T}\to\mathcal{E}^{\prime}.

(219)

The equality
\rho_{T}(h)=\rho_{T}(h^{\prime})
implies
\mathcal{E}\cong\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
on
B_{T}
and the isomorphism preserves the filtrations
\{\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}\}_{i=0}^{2}
and
\{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{\leq i}\}_{i=0}^{2}
.

Consider the following commutative diagram with exact rows

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\leq i-1}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\leq i}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{i}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{T,*}(\mathcal{E}_{\leq i-1}(m))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{T,*}(\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}(m))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{T,*}(\mathcal{E}_{i}(m))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{T,*}(q_{\leq i-1}(m))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{T,*}(q_{\leq i}(m))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{T,*}(q_{i}(m))}

(220)

Recall that
m
is larger than the regularity of
\mathcal{E}_{i}
for
i=1,2
. We have that
f_{T,*}(\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}(m))
and
f_{T,*}(\mathcal{E}_{i}(m))
are locally free of ranks
P_{\leq i}(m)
and
P_{i}(m)
on
T
for
i=1,2
respectively.
Therefore
f_{T,*}(q_{\leq i}(m))
and
f_{T,*}(q_{i}(m))
in diagram (
220
) are morphisms between locally free sheaves. For any closed point
t\in T
, we have that
[(\mathcal{E}|_{B_{t}},q|_{B_{t}})]
corresponds to a closed point in
Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}
, so

\begin{split}f_{t,*}(q_{i}|_{B_{t}}(m)):&V_{i}\to f_{t,*}(\mathcal{E}_{i}|_{B_{t}}(m))\\
f_{t,*}(q_{\leq i}|_{B_{t}}(m)):&V_{\leq i}\to f_{t,*}(\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}|_{B_{t}}(m))\end{split}

(221)

are isomorphisms, i.e. fibres at
t\in T
of
f_{T,*}(q_{\leq i}(m))
and
f_{T,*}(q_{i}(m))
in diagram (
220
) are isomorphisms. This proves that
f_{T,*}(q_{\leq i}(m))
and
f_{T,*}(q_{i}(m))
are isomorphisms by
Lemma 00O0

\begin{split}f_{T,*}(q_{\leq i}(m)):&V_{\leq i}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}\cong f_{T,*}(\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}(m))\\
f_{T,*}(q_{i}(m)):&V_{\leq i}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}\cong f_{T,*}(\mathcal{E}_{i}(m)).\end{split}

(222)

Similarly, we have that
f_{T,*}(q^{\prime}_{\leq i}(m))
and
f_{T,*}(q^{\prime}_{i}(m))
are isomorphisms for
i=1,2
.

Consider the morphism
g:V\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}\to V\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}
defined as the following composition on
T

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{T,*}(\mathcal{E}(m))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{T,*}(q(m))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{T,*}(q^{\prime}(m))^{-1}}

(223)

It is easy to check:

•

the morphism
g:V\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}\to V\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T}
preserves the filtration
\{V_{\leq i}\}_{i=0}^{2}
, thus it defines a morphism
g\in\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk}(T,\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{GL},\tau})
, where
\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{GL},\tau}\subseteq\mathrm{GL}(V)
is the parabolic subgroup stabilising the flag
\{V_{\leq i}\}_{i=0}^{2}
;

•

the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V(-m)_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V(-m)_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g(-m)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\prime}}

(224)

that is
g.[(\mathcal{E},q)]=[(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},q^{\prime})]
.

Thus
h,h^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk}(T,Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}})
are in one
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk}(T,\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{GL},\tau})
-orbit, which is a
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk}(T,\mathrm{P}_{\tau})
-orbit, since the centre
\mathbb{G}_{m}\subseteq\mathrm{GL}(V)
acts trivially. This proves
(1)
.

For
(2)
, let
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
and let
x\in\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta}(T)
. Let
\mathcal{E}
represent the isomorphism class
x\in\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta}(T)
. Note that
\mathcal{E}
and
\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}
are coherent sheaves on
B_{T}
and
\mathcal{E}_{i}:=\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}/\mathcal{E}_{\leq i-1}
is flat over
T
with Hilbert polynomial
P_{i}(t)
.

Since
m
is larger than any regularity, we have that:

•

f_{T,*}(\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}(m))
and
f_{T,*}(\mathcal{E}_{i}(m))
are locally free of ranks
P_{\leq i}(m)
and
P_{i}(m)
on
T
for
i=1,2
respectively;

•

R^{j}f_{T,*}(\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}(m))=0
and
R^{j}f_{T,*}(\mathcal{E}_{i}(m))=0
for
i=1,2
and
j>0
;

•

(f_{T})^{*}f_{T,*}(\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}(m))\to\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}(m)
and
(f_{T})^{*}f_{T,*}(\mathcal{E}_{i}(m))\to\mathcal{E}_{i}(m)
are surjective for
i=1,2
.

There is an open covering
T=\bigcup_{j\in J}T_{j}
such that
f_{T,*}(\mathcal{E}_{i}(m))|_{T_{j}}
is free of rank
P_{i}(m)
for
i=1,2
. Then
f_{T,*}(\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}(m))|_{T_{j}}
is free of rank
P_{\leq i}(m)
for
i=1,2
. On each
T_{j}
, we can choose generating global sections compatible with the filtration
\big\{f_{T,*}(\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}(m))\big\}_{i=0}^{2}
and obtain isomorphisms in the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\leq 1}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T_{j}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\leq 2}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T_{j}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T_{j}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{T,*}(\mathcal{E}_{\leq 1}(m))|_{T_{j}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{T,*}(\mathcal{E}_{\leq 2}(m))|_{T_{j}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{T,*}(\mathcal{E}(m))|_{T_{j}}\;.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}

(225)

On each
T_{j}
and
i=1,2
we have a surjective morphism

\begin{split}V_{\leq i}(-m)_{T_{j}}:=&(f_{T_{j}})^{*}\big(V_{\leq j}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{O}_{T_{j}}\big)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{B_{T_{j}}}}\mathcal{L}_{T_{j}}^{-m}\\
\cong&(f_{T_{j}})^{*}\big(f_{T,*}(\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}(m))|_{T_{j}}\big)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{B_{T_{j}}}}\mathcal{L}_{T_{j}}^{-m}\\
\cong&(f_{T})^{*}f_{T,*}(\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}(m))\big|_{B_{T_{j}}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{B_{T_{j}}}}\mathcal{L}_{T_{j}}^{-m}\\
\twoheadrightarrow&\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}(m)\big|_{B_{T_{j}}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{B_{T_{j}}}}\mathcal{L}_{T_{j}}^{-m}\\
\cong&\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}|_{B_{T_{j}}}.\end{split}

(226)

When
i=2
, let
q_{j}:V(-m)_{T_{j}}\to\mathcal{E}|_{B_{T_{j}}}
denote the surjective morphism. Then we have
q_{j}\big(V_{\leq i}(-m)_{B_{T_{j}}}\big)=\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}|_{B_{T_{j}}}
for
i=1,2
. Therefore
[(\mathcal{E}|_{B_{T_{j}}},q_{j})]
defines a morphism
T_{j}\to Y_{\tau}
. It is easy to check that it defines a morphism
h_{j}:T_{j}\to Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}
, for example by Lemma
4.2
. We have that
\rho_{T_{j}}
maps
h_{j}
to
x|_{T_{j}}=[\mathcal{E}|_{B_{T_{j}}}]\in\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta}(T_{j})
. Since the image of
\rho_{T_{j}}
and the image of
\theta_{T_{j}}
coincide, we have that
x|_{T_{j}}
is in the image of
\theta_{T_{j}}
. This proves
(2)
.

Let
\theta^{\sharp}:\big(Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}/_{p}\mathrm{P}_{\tau}\big)^{\sharp}\to(\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta})^{\sharp}
be sheafification of
\theta
on
(\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk)_{\acute{e}tale}
. We have that
\theta^{\sharp}
is injective since
\theta
is by
(1)
. Zariski coverings are étale coverings, so
\theta^{\sharp}
is surjective as a morphism of sheaves by
(2)
. Then
\theta^{\sharp}
is an isomorphism.
∎

4.3
Local properties of the quotient

Recall in Corollary
3.26
, with Condition
UU
, the quotient sheaf is represented by the geometric quotient, and the representability commutes with base change. We will prove the quotient
\pi:Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}\to Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}/\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
represents the associated quotient presheaf. Therefore
(\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta})^{\sharp}\cong Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}/\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
by Lemma
4.3
.

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
m\gg 0
. Let
[(\mathcal{E},q)]
be a closed point of
Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}
. Assume
\mathcal{E}\not\cong\mathcal{E}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{E}_{2}
. If
(u,t,k)\in\mathrm{U}_{\tau}\rtimes(\mathrm{T}_{\tau}\times\mathrm{K}_{\tau})
stabilises
[(\mathcal{E},q)]
, then the image of
(t,k)
along
\mathrm{T}_{\tau}\times\mathrm{K}_{\tau}\to\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
is a scalar multiplication of
1_{V}\in\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
.

Proof.

Let
q:V(-m)\to\mathcal{E}
be a quotient in the class
[(\mathcal{E},q)]
. Let
p_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}:Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}\to\prod_{i=1}^{2}R_{i}^{\mathrm{s}}
be the retraction. See Theorem
2.5
for
R_{i}^{\mathrm{s}}
. Let
x:=[(\mathcal{E},q)]
denote the point of
Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}
.

We have that
p_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}
is
\mathrm{U}_{\tau}
-invariant and
\mathrm{T}_{\tau}\times\mathrm{K}_{\tau}
-equivariant. Since
(u,t,k)
stabilises
x\in Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}
, we have

p_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}(x)=p_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}\big((u,t,k).x\big)=p_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}\big((t,k).x\big)=(t,k).p_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}(x).

(227)

Thus
(t,k)
stabilises
p_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}(x)
.

Let
\begin{pmatrix}g_{1}&\\
&g_{2}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
be the image of
(t,k)\in\mathrm{T}_{\tau}\times\mathrm{K}_{\tau}
. Then
g_{i}\in\mathrm{GL}(V_{i})
stabilises
[(\mathcal{E}_{i},q_{i})]\in R_{i}^{\mathrm{s}}
for
i=1,2
. By
[
9
]
Lemma 4.3.2, we have that
g_{i}
is in the image of
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{E}_{i})\to\mathrm{GL}(V_{i})
. Since
\mathcal{E}_{i}
is stable, we have
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{E}_{i})\cong\Bbbk^{\times}
. Then
g_{i}=c_{i}1_{V_{i}}
for some
c_{i}\in\Bbbk^{\times}
.

We need to prove
c_{1}=c_{2}
when
\mathcal{E}\not\cong\mathcal{E}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{E}_{2}
. Suppose otherwise
c_{1}\neq c_{2}
. Let
\begin{pmatrix}1_{V_{1}}&A\\
&1_{V_{2}}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
be the image of
u\in\mathrm{U}_{\tau}
, where
A:V_{2}\to V_{1}
is a linear map. The image of
(u,t,k)
in
\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
is
\begin{pmatrix}c_{1}1_{V_{1}}&c_{2}A\\
&c_{2}1_{V_{2}}\end{pmatrix}
. Let
g:V\to V
denote this linear map. Consider another linear map

\sigma:V_{2}\to V,\quad v_{2}\mapsto\frac{c_{2}Av_{2}}{c_{2}-c_{1}}+v_{2}.

(228)

If
\mathrm{pr}_{2}:V\to V_{2}
denotes the projection, then

\sigma\circ\mathrm{pr}_{2}=\frac{1}{c_{2}-c_{1}}g-\frac{c_{1}}{c_{2}-c_{1}}1_{V},\quad\textrm{in }\mathrm{End}_{\Bbbk}(V).

(229)

The linear maps
g,\sigma,\mathrm{pr}_{2}
induce morphisms

\begin{split}g(-m):&V(-m)\to V(-m)\\
\sigma(-m):&V_{2}(-m)\to V(-m)\\
\mathrm{pr}_{2}(-m)&:V(-m)\to V_{2}(-m)\end{split}

(230)

where
g(-m):=g\otimes_{\Bbbk}1_{\mathcal{L}^{m}}
and
\sigma(-m),\mathrm{pr}_{2}(-m)
are defined similarly. They satisfy

\sigma(-m)\circ\mathrm{pr}_{2}(-m)=\frac{1}{c_{2}-c_{1}}g(-m)-\frac{c_{1}}{c_{2}-c_{1}}1_{V(-m)}.

(231)

Since
g.x=x
, we have
g(-m)(\ker q)\subseteq\ker(q)
by
[
9
]
Lemma 4.3.2. By the above equation we have

\sigma(-m)\big(\mathrm{pr}_{2}(-m)(\ker q)\big)\subseteq\ker q.

(232)

Since
\mathrm{pr}_{2}(-m)(\ker q)=\ker q_{2}
, we have
\sigma(-m)(\ker q_{2})\subseteq\ker q
. Then
\sigma(-m)
induces a morphism
\bar{\sigma}:\mathcal{E}_{2}\to\mathcal{E}
fitting in the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{2}(-m)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V(-m)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma(-m)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\sigma}}

(233)

Since
\mathrm{pr}_{2}\circ\sigma=1_{V_{2}}
, we have
\overline{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}\circ\bar{\sigma}=1_{\mathcal{E}_{2}}
, where
\overline{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}_{2}
is the quotient. Then
\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}_{2}
has a section, i.e.
\mathcal{E}\cong\mathcal{E}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{E}_{2}
, which is a contradiction.
∎

Lemma 4.5
.

The quotient
\pi:Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}\to Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}/\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
represents the quotient sheaf
\big(Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}/_{p}\mathrm{P}_{\tau}\big)^{\sharp}
on
(\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk)_{\acute{e}tale}
.

Proof.

Let
Z(\mathrm{P}_{\tau})\subseteq\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
denote the centre. Then
Z(\mathrm{P}_{\tau})=\{t1_{V}\in\mathrm{P}_{\tau}:t\in\mathbb{G}_{m}\}
. The action of
\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
on
Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}
factors through
\mathrm{P}_{\tau}/Z(\mathrm{P}_{\tau})
. Therefore
Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}
has a quasi-projective universal geometric quotient by
\mathrm{P}_{\tau}/Z(\mathrm{P}_{\tau})
.

We can think the quotient of
Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}
by
\mathrm{P}_{\tau}/Z(\mathrm{P}_{\tau})
as a composition of universal geometric quotients

Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}\to Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}/\mathrm{U}_{\tau}\to\big(Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}/\mathrm{U}_{\tau}\big)\big/\big(\mathrm{P}_{\tau}/\mathrm{U}_{\tau}Z(\mathrm{P}_{\tau})\big).

(234)

These two quotients both represent their quotient sheaves:

(1)

The first quotient
Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}\to Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}/\mathrm{U}_{\tau}
represents the quotient sheaf
\big(Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}/_{p}\mathrm{U}_{\tau}\big)^{\sharp}
by Corollary
3.26
;

(2)

The second quotient
Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}/\mathrm{U}_{\tau}\to\big(Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}/\mathrm{U}_{\tau}\big)\big/\big(\mathrm{P}_{\tau}/\mathrm{U}_{\tau}Z(\mathrm{P}_{\tau})\big)
is a geometric quotient of a reductive group, and closed points have trivial stabilisers by Lemma
4.4
. We can apply Luna’s étale slice theorem to conclude that it is a principal
\mathrm{P}_{\tau}/\mathrm{U}_{\tau}Z(\mathrm{P}_{\tau})
-bundle by
[
9
]
Corollary 4.2.13. In particular, it also represents the associated quotient sheaf.

As a composition of
(1)
and
(2)
, we have that
\pi:Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}\to Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}/\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
induces an isomorphism
Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}/\mathrm{P}_{\tau}\cong\big(Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}/_{p}\mathrm{P}_{\tau}\big)^{\sharp}
.
∎

Theorem 4.6
.

The sheafification of
\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta}
on
(\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk)_{\acute{e}tale}
is representable by
Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}/\mathrm{P}_{\tau}

(\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta})^{\sharp}\cong Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}/\mathrm{P}_{\tau}.

(235)

In particular, the moduli functor
\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta}
has a coarse moduli space
Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}/\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.3
, the sheafification of
\theta
is an isomorphism
\theta^{\sharp}:\big(Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}/_{p}\mathrm{P}_{\tau}\big)^{\sharp}\cong(\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta})^{\sharp}
. By Lemma
4.5
, we have
\big(Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}/_{p}\mathrm{P}_{\tau}\big)^{\sharp}\cong Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}/P_{\tau}
. Then
(\mathbf{M}^{\prime}_{\tau,\delta})^{\sharp}\cong Y_{\tau,\delta}^{\mathrm{s}}/\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
.
∎

Appendix A
On the boundaries of
Y_{\tau}
and
Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}

Let
\tau
be a Harder-Narasimhan type of length 2. Let
P_{i}(t),P(t)
be the Hilbert polynomials associated to
\tau
. For
1\leq i\leq 2
, let
P_{\leq i}(t):=P_{1}(t)+\cdots+P_{i}(t)
. Recall that
\mathrm{P}_{\tau}\subseteq\mathrm{SL}(V)
is the parabolic subgroup stabilising the flag
\{V_{\leq i}\}_{i=0}^{2}
(See the first paragraph of Section
2.2.2
). Recall that
Y_{\tau}\subseteq\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
is the locally closed subscheme that represents the sub-functor
\mathbf{Y}_{\tau}
(See Lemma
2.9
). There is a retraction
p_{\tau}:Y_{\tau}\to\prod_{i=1}^{2}\mathrm{Quot}_{V_{i}(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P_{i}(t),\mathcal{L}}
(See diagram (
34
)), and
Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}\subseteq Y_{\tau}
is the preimage of
\prod_{i=1}^{2}R_{i}^{\mathrm{s}}\subseteq\prod_{i=1}^{2}\mathrm{Quot}_{V_{i}(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P_{i}(t),\mathcal{L}}
under
p_{\tau}
(See diagram (
36
)).

We want to consider the
\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
-quotient of
Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}
. However,
Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}
is not projective and our non-reductive GIT requires a linear action on a projective scheme. Therefore we need to consider the
\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
-action on a suitable projective completion
\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}
. We will take
\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}
to be the scheme theoretic image of
Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}\to\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
. The main result of this section is Proposition
A.2
, which says that we do not need to worry about the boundary of
Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}\subseteq\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}
.

For
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
and a coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on
B
, recall the notation

\begin{split}&B_{T}:=B\times_{\Bbbk}T\\
&f_{T}:B_{T}\to T\quad\textrm{the projection}\\
&\mathcal{F}_{T}\quad\textrm{the pullback of }\mathcal{F}\textrm{ along }B_{T}\to B.\end{split}

(236)

For a family
(\mathcal{E},q)
of quotients parametrised by
T
, recall the notation

\begin{split}&\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}:=q\big(V_{\leq i}(-m)_{T}\big)\\
&\mathcal{E}_{i}:=\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}/\mathcal{E}_{\leq i-1}\\
&q_{i}:V_{i}(-m)_{T}\to\mathcal{E}_{i}\quad\textrm{the induced surjective morphism}.\end{split}

(237)

Recall that there exist closed immersions

\begin{split}\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}&\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Grass}\big(V\otimes_{\Bbbk}H,P(M)\big)\\
&\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}\bigg(\Big(\bigwedge^{P(M)}(V\otimes_{\Bbbk}H)\Big)^{*}\bigg)\end{split}

(238)

where
H:=H^{0}(B,\mathcal{L}^{M-m})=f_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{M-m})
. Note that points in the Grassmannian are quotients while points in the projective space are one-dimensional subspaces. Recall that
\mathcal{M}
denotes a very ample line bundle on
\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
, which is the pullback of
\mathcal{O}(1)
along the immersions above.

Let
\overline{Y_{\tau}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
be the scheme theoretic image of
Y_{\tau}\to\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
.

Proposition A.1
.

For
M\gg m\gg 0
, there exists a closed subscheme

\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}\bigg(\Big(\bigwedge^{P(M)}(V\otimes_{\Bbbk}H)\Big)^{*}\bigg)

(239)

such that:

(1)

\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}
is a
\mathrm{T}_{\tau}
-invariant linear subspace;

(2)

the immersion
Y_{\tau}\to\mathbb{P}\Big(\big(\bigwedge^{P(M)}(V\otimes_{\Bbbk}H)\big)^{*}\Big)
factors through
\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}
, i.e. there exists a closed immersion
\overline{Y_{\tau}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}
;

(3)

\big(\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}\big)^{0}_{\min}\cap\overline{Y_{\tau}}=Y_{\tau}
, where
\lambda:\mathrm{T}_{\tau}\to\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
.

Proof.

Only in this proof, for simplicity we denote

P:=P(M),\quad P_{i}:=P_{i}(M),\quad P_{\leq i}:=P_{\leq i}(M)

(240)

and we simplify
(-)\otimes_{\Bbbk}(-)
to
(-)\otimes(-)

We first construct the linear subspace
\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}
, which is determined by a quotient space of
\bigwedge^{P}(V\otimes H)
. We have

\begin{split}\bigwedge^{P}(V\otimes H)=&\bigwedge^{P}\Big(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{2}(V_{i}\otimes H)\Big)\\
=&\bigoplus_{(m_{1},m_{2})\in\Lambda}\bigwedge^{m_{1}}(V_{1}\otimes H)\otimes\;\bigwedge^{m_{2}}(V_{2}\otimes H)\end{split}

(241)

where the direct sum is over the index set
\Lambda

\Lambda:=\big\{(m_{1},m_{2})\in\mathbb{N}^{2}:m_{1}+m_{2}=P(M)\big\}.

(242)

Let
\Lambda_{0}\subseteq\Lambda
be the subset

\Lambda_{0}:=\big\{(m_{1},m_{2})\in\Lambda:m_{1}>P_{1}(M)\big\}.

(243)

and let
W\subseteq\bigwedge^{P}(V\otimes H)
be the following subspace

W:=\bigoplus_{(m_{1},m_{2})\in\Lambda_{0}}\bigwedge^{m_{1}}(V_{1}\otimes H)\otimes\;\bigwedge^{m_{2}}(V_{2}\otimes H).

(244)

Then the following quotient space of
\bigwedge^{P}(V\otimes H)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigwedge^{P}(V\otimes H)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigwedge^{P}(V\otimes H)\Big/W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

(245)

defines a linear subspace

\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}\Big(\big(\bigwedge^{P}(V\otimes H)\big)^{*}\Big).

(246)

Since
W\subseteq\bigwedge^{P}(V\otimes H)
is invariant under the torus
\mathrm{T}_{\tau}
, we have that
\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}
is invariant under
\mathrm{T}_{\tau}
. This proves
(1)
.

Next, we prove
(2)
. Assume first we have proved that
Y_{\tau}\to\mathbb{P}\Big(\big(\bigwedge^{P}(V\otimes H)\big)^{*}\Big)
factors through
\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}
, then we have the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{\tau}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}\Big(\big(\bigwedge^{P}(V\otimes H)\big)^{*}\Big).}

(247)

It is easy to see that if we have morphisms
T\to Z_{1}\hookrightarrow Z_{2}
where
Z_{1}\hookrightarrow Z_{2}
is a closed immersion, then the scheme theoretic image of
T\to Z_{1}
is canonically isomorphic to the scheme theoretic image of
T\to Z_{2}
. Apply this observation to the diagram above. Then
\overline{Y_{\tau}}
is the scheme theoretic image of all three morphisms from
Y_{\tau}
in the diagram. In particular, we have
\overline{Y_{\tau}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}
, which is the second part of
(2)
.

The first part of
(2)
is equivalent to the following

(2’)

for any
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
and any
T\to Y_{\tau}
, the composition
T\to Y_{\tau}\to\mathbb{P}\Big(\big(\bigwedge^{P}(V\otimes H)\big)^{*}\Big)
factors through
\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}
.

Let
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
and let
T\to Y_{\tau}
be a morphism. Let
(\mathcal{E},q)
be a family over
T
of quotients whose classifying morphism is
T\to Y_{\tau}
. The composition
T\to Y_{\tau}\to\mathbb{P}\Big(\big(\bigwedge^{P}(V\otimes H)\big)^{*}\Big)
is the classifying morphism of the following surjective morphism

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigwedge^{P}(V\otimes H)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigwedge^{P}f_{T,*}\big(\mathcal{E}(M)\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigwedge^{P}\rho}

(248)

where

•

\mathcal{E}(M):=\mathcal{E}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{B_{T}}}\mathcal{L}_{T}^{M}
;

•

\rho:(V\otimes H)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{T}\to f_{T,*}\big(\mathcal{E}(M)\big)
is the morphism

\rho:=f_{T,*}\Big(q\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{B_{T}}}1_{\mathcal{L}_{T}^{M}}\Big);

(249)

•

f_{T,*}\big(\mathcal{E}(M)\big)
is locally free of rank
P=P(M)
on
T
when
M
is sufficiently large.

The morphism
T\to\mathbb{P}\Big(\big(\bigwedge^{P}(V\otimes H)\big)^{*}\Big)
factors through
\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}
if and only if the following composition is zero

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W\otimes\mathcal{O}_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigwedge^{P}(V\otimes H)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigwedge^{P}f_{T,*}\big(\mathcal{E}(M)\big).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigwedge^{P}\rho}

(250)

Since the isomorphism class of
(\mathcal{E},q)
corresponds to a morphism to
Y_{\tau}
, we have that
\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}
is flat over
T
with Hilbert polynomial
P_{\leq i}(t)
. We can enlarge
M
and assume that
f_{T,*}\big(\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}(M)\big)
is locally free of rank
P_{\leq i}
for all
1\leq i\leq 2
and all
T\in\mathrm{Sch}/\Bbbk
and all families over
T
of quotients
(\mathcal{E},q)
.

Let
(m_{1},m_{2})\in\Lambda
. Choose

\begin{split}s^{(1)}_{1},&\cdots,s^{(1)}_{m_{1}}\in V_{1}\otimes H\\
S^{(2)}&\in\bigwedge^{m_{2}}(V_{2}\otimes H).\end{split}

(251)

Then vectors of the following form span the vector space
\bigwedge^{m_{1}}(V_{1}\otimes H)\otimes\;\bigwedge^{m_{2}}(V_{2}\otimes H)

v:=\big(s^{(1)}_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge s^{(1)}_{m_{1}}\big)\otimes S^{(2)},\quad(m_{1},m_{2})\in\Lambda.

(252)

If we consider all
(m_{1},m_{2})\in\Lambda_{0}
, then vectors of the above form span
W
. Therefore to show
\Big(\bigwedge^{P}\rho\Big)\big(W\otimes\mathcal{O}_{T}\big)=0
, it suffices to show that
v\otimes 1\in W\otimes\mathcal{O}_{T}
maps to zero under
\bigwedge^{P}\rho
.

The image of
v\otimes 1
under
\bigwedge^{P}\rho
is

\Big(\rho\big(s^{(1)}_{1}\otimes 1\big)\wedge\cdots\wedge\rho\big(s^{(1)}_{m_{1}}\otimes 1\big)\Big)\wedge\;\rho\big(S^{(2)}\otimes 1\big).

(253)

We have

\rho\big(s^{(1)}_{1}\otimes 1\big)\wedge\cdots\wedge\rho\big(s^{(1)}_{m_{1}}\otimes 1\big)=0

(254)

since it is a section of the sheaf

\bigwedge^{m_{1}}f_{T,*}\big(\mathcal{E}_{1}(M)\big)=0

(255)

which is the zero sheaf since
f_{T,*}\big(\mathcal{E}_{1}(M)\big)
is locally free of rank
P_{1}(M)<m_{1}
. This proves
(2)^{\prime}
, thus
(2)
.

For
(3)
, let
w_{1}>w_{2}
be the integers such that
w_{1}\dim V_{1}+w_{2}\dim V_{2}=0
and
\lambda(t)=t^{w_{1}}1_{V_{1}}\oplus t^{w_{2}}1_{V_{2}}
. By
Lemma 01QV
and
Lemma 01R8
, we have that
Y_{\tau}\subseteq\overline{Y_{\tau}}
is an open subscheme. Then both sides of
(3)
are open subschemes of
\overline{Y_{\tau}}
, so it suffices to show that they contain the same closed points.

Recall that

\begin{split}H^{0}\big(\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}},\mathcal{O}(1)\big)\cong&\bigwedge^{P}(V\otimes H)\Big/W\\
\cong&\bigoplus_{(m_{1},m_{2})\in\Lambda\setminus\Lambda_{0}}\bigwedge^{m_{1}}(V_{1}\otimes H)\otimes\;\bigwedge^{m_{2}}(V_{2}\otimes H)\end{split}

(256)

The weights for
\lambda\curvearrowright H^{0}\big(\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}},\mathcal{O}(1)\big)
are

\sum_{i=1}^{2}w_{i}m_{i}\dim V_{i}\dim H,\quad(m_{1},m_{2})\in\Lambda\setminus\Lambda_{0}.

(257)

When
(m_{1},m_{2})\in\Lambda\setminus\Lambda_{0}
, we have
m_{1}\leq P_{1}(M)
and
m_{1}+m_{2}=P(M)
and

\begin{split}&\sum_{i=1}^{2}w_{i}m_{i}\dim V_{i}\dim H\\
=&w_{2}P\dim V\dim H+(w_{1}-w_{2})m_{1}\dim V_{1}\dim H\\
\leq&w_{2}P\dim V\dim H+(w_{1}-w_{2})P_{1}\dim V_{1}\dim H\\
=&\sum_{i=1}^{2}w_{i}P_{i}\dim V_{i}\dim H.\end{split}

(258)

So the maximal
\lambda
-weight on
H^{0}\big(\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}},\mathcal{O}(1)\big)
is
\sum_{i=1}^{2}w_{i}P_{i}\dim V_{i}\dim H
and

H^{0}\big(\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}},\mathcal{O}(1)\big)_{\lambda=\max}\cong\bigwedge^{P_{1}}(V_{1}\otimes H)\otimes\;\bigwedge^{P_{2}}(V_{2}\otimes H).

(259)

For one direction of inclusion
Y_{\tau}\subseteq\big(\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}\big)^{0}_{\min}\cap\overline{Y_{\tau}}
, let
[(\mathcal{E},q)]\in Y_{\tau}
be a closed point. By
[
9
]
Lemma 4.4.3, we have when considered in
\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}

\lim_{t\to 0}\lambda(t).[(\mathcal{E},q)]=\Big[\Big(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{2}\mathcal{E}_{i},\bigoplus_{i=1}^{2}q_{i}\Big)\Big]\in\prod_{i=1}^{2}\mathrm{Quot}_{V_{i}(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P_{i}(t),\mathcal{L}}.

(260)

The limit point is still in
Y_{\tau}
and its image in
\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}
represents the one-dimensional quotient

\begin{split}\bigwedge^{P}(V\otimes H)\Big/W\twoheadrightarrow&\bigwedge^{P}\Big(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{2}H^{0}\big(B,\mathcal{E}_{i}(M)\big)\Big)\\
\cong&\bigwedge^{P_{1}}H^{0}\big(B,\mathcal{E}_{1}(M)\big)\otimes\;\bigwedge^{P_{2}}H^{0}\big(B,\mathcal{E}_{2}(M)\big).\end{split}

(261)

The point
\lim\lambda(t).[(\mathcal{E},q)]
is in
\big(\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}\big)^{0}_{\min}
if and only if the following composition is not zero

\begin{split}&\bigwedge^{P_{1}}(V_{1}\otimes H)\otimes\;\bigwedge^{P_{2}}(V_{2}\otimes H)\\
\subseteq&\bigwedge^{P}(V\otimes H)\Big/W\\
\twoheadrightarrow&\bigwedge^{P_{1}}H^{0}\big(B,\mathcal{E}_{1}(M)\big)\otimes\;\bigwedge^{P_{2}}H^{0}\big(B,\mathcal{E}_{2}(M)\big).\end{split}

(262)

The composition above is surjective since it is the tensor product of exterior products of surjective morphisms

V_{i}\otimes H:=V_{i}\otimes H^{0}\big(B,\mathcal{L}^{mM^{\prime}}\big)\twoheadrightarrow H^{0}\big(B,\mathcal{E}_{i}(M)\big),\quad 1\leq i\leq 2.

(263)

This proves
\lim\lambda(t).[(\mathcal{E},q)]\in\big(\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}\big)^{0}_{\min}
and this shows
[(\mathcal{E},q)]\in\big(\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}\big)^{0}_{\min}
. This proves
Y_{\tau}\subseteq\big(\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}\big)^{0}_{\min}\cap\overline{Y_{\tau}}
.

For the other direction
\big(\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}\big)^{0}_{\min}\cap\overline{Y_{\tau}}\subseteq Y_{\tau}
, let
[(\mathcal{E},q)]\in\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}
be a closed point such that
[(\mathcal{E},q)]\in\big(\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}\big)^{0}_{\min}\cap\overline{Y_{\tau}}
. The point
[(\mathcal{E},q)]\in\mathbb{P}\Big(\big(\bigwedge^{P}(V\otimes H)\big)^{*}\Big)
corresponds to the one-dimensional quotient

\pi:\bigwedge^{P}(V\otimes H)\twoheadrightarrow\bigwedge^{P}H^{0}\big(B,\mathcal{E}(M)\big)

(264)

and
\pi(W)=0
since
[(\mathcal{E},q)]\in\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}
.

The following collection of coherent sheaves on
B
is bounded

\bigcup_{i=1}^{2}\Big\{\mathcal{F}:\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{E}_{i}\textrm{ or }\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}\textrm{ for }[(\mathcal{E},q)]\in\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}\Big\}

(265)

By
[
9
]
Lemma 1.7.6, the set of Hilbert polynomials of the above collection is finite, i.e. the following set is finite

\mathcal{P}:=\bigcup_{i=1}^{2}\Big\{P(\mathcal{F},t):\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{E}_{i}\textrm{ or }\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}\textrm{ for }[(\mathcal{E},q)]\in\mathrm{Quot}_{V(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P(t),\mathcal{L}}\Big\}.

(266)

Also by
[
9
]
Lemma 1.7.6, regularities of
\mathcal{E}_{i}
and
\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}
are bounded. We can enlarge
M
such that

•

higher cohomology groups of
\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}(M)
and
\mathcal{E}_{i}(M)
are zero;

•

the following map is order-preserving and bijective

\mathcal{P}\to\mathbb{N},\quad P(\mathcal{F},t)\mapsto P(\mathcal{F},M).

(267)

Let
m_{i}:=\dim H^{0}\big(B,\mathcal{E}_{i}(M)\big)
for
1\leq i\leq 2
. When
M
is large, higher cohomology groups of
\mathcal{E}_{i}(M)
vanish, and then

\begin{split}&m_{i}=P(\mathcal{E}_{i},M),\quad i=1,2\\
&m_{1}+m_{2}=P(M).\end{split}

(268)

We claim that
m_{1}\geq P_{1}
. Otherwise, the following composition is zero

\begin{split}&\bigwedge^{P_{1}}(V_{1}\otimes H)\otimes\;\bigwedge^{P_{2}}(V_{2}\otimes H)\\
\subseteq&\bigwedge^{P}(V\otimes H)\\
\xrightarrow{\pi}&\bigwedge^{P}H^{0}\big(B,\mathcal{E}(M)\big).\end{split}

(269)

which contradicts that
[(\mathcal{E},q)]\in\big(\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}\big)^{0}_{\min}
.

Therefore we have either
m_{1}=P_{1}
or
(m_{1},m_{2})\in\Lambda_{0}
. We exclude the case when
(m_{1},m_{2})\in\Lambda_{0}
. Otherwise, the following composition is non-zero

\begin{split}&\bigwedge^{m_{1}}(V_{1}\otimes H)\otimes\;\bigwedge^{m_{2}}(V_{2}\otimes H)\\
\subseteq&\bigwedge^{P}(V\otimes H)\\
\xrightarrow{\pi}&\bigwedge^{P}H^{0}\big(B,\mathcal{E}(M)\big)\end{split}

(270)

which contradicts
\pi(W)=0
. Therefore we have

P(\mathcal{E}_{i},M)=P_{i}(M),\quad i=1,2.

(271)

Since
P(\mathcal{E}_{i},t),P_{i}(t)\in\mathcal{P}
, we have
P(\mathcal{E}_{i},t)=P_{i}(t)
and this shows
[(\mathcal{E},q)]\in Y_{\tau}
. This proves
\big(\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}\big)^{0}_{\min}\cap\overline{Y_{\tau}}\subseteq Y_{\tau}
, thus
(3)
.
∎

Let
\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\hookrightarrow\overline{Y_{\tau}}
be the scheme theoretic image of
Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}\to\overline{Y_{\tau}}
.

Proposition A.2
.

Assume
\prod_{i=1}^{2}R_{i}^{\mathrm{s}}\neq\emptyset
. For
M\gg m\gg 0
, we have the following:

(1)

\big(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\big)^{0}_{\min}=\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\cap Y_{\tau}
;

(2)

Let
Z_{\min}\big(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\big):=\Big(\big(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\big)^{0}_{\min}\Big)^{\lambda}
for
\lambda:\mathrm{T}_{\tau}\to\mathrm{P}_{\tau}
. Then

\begin{split}Z_{\min}\big(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\big)=&\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\cap\prod_{i=1}^{2}\mathrm{Quot}_{V_{i}(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P_{i}(t),\mathcal{L}}\\
\prod_{i=1}^{2}R_{i}^{\mathrm{s}}\subseteq&Z_{\min}\big(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\big)\hookrightarrow\prod_{i=1}^{2}\overline{R_{i}};\end{split}

(272)

(3)

Let
Z_{\min}^{\mathrm{K}_{\tau},\mathrm{s}}\big(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\big)\subseteq Z_{\min}\big(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\big)
denote the stable locus for the
\mathrm{K}_{\tau}
-linearisation. Then

Z_{\min}^{\mathrm{K}_{\tau},\mathrm{s}}\big(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\big)=\prod_{i=1}^{2}R_{i}^{\mathrm{s}},\quad p_{\tau}^{-1}\big(Z_{\min}^{\mathrm{K}_{\tau},\mathrm{s}}\big(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\big)\big)=Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}.

(273)

Proof.

We consider the ample line bundle
\mathcal{M}|_{\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}}
on
\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}
, which equals the pullback of
\mathcal{O}(1)
along
\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}\Big(\big(\bigwedge^{P}(V\otimes H)\big)^{*}\Big)
, for
P:=P(M)
. By Lemma
A.1
, there is a linear subspace
\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}\Big(\big(\bigwedge^{P}(V\otimes H)\big)^{*}\Big)
containing
\overline{Y_{\tau}}
. Then
\mathcal{M}|_{\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}}(1)|_{\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}}
.

For
(1)
, both sides are open in
\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}
, so we can check that the closed points coincide. We have that
\emptyset\neq\prod_{i=1}^{2}R_{i}^{\mathrm{s}}\subseteq\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\cap\big(\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}\big)^{0}_{\min}
, and then

\big(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\big)^{0}_{\min}=\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\cap\big(\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}\big)^{0}_{\min}.

(274)

By Lemma
A.1
,
\overline{Y_{\tau}}\cap\big(\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}\big)^{0}_{\min}=Y_{\tau}
and then

\begin{split}\big(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\big)^{0}_{\min}=&\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\cap\big(\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}\big)^{0}_{\min}\\
=&\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\cap\overline{Y_{\tau}}\cap\big(\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}\big)^{0}_{\min}\\
=&\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\cap Y_{\tau}\end{split}

(275)

which proves
(1)
.

For
(2)
, let
Z_{\min}\big(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\big):=\Big(\big(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\big)^{0}_{\min}\Big)^{\lambda}
denote the fixed subscheme with respect to
\lambda
. Recall that
(Y_{\tau})^{\lambda}=\prod_{i=1}^{2}\mathrm{Quot}_{V_{i}(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P_{i}(t),\mathcal{L}}
and the intersection
\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\cap(Y_{\tau})^{\lambda}
is defined as the fibre product over
\overline{Y_{\tau}}
. For the first equality, it suffices to show the existence of the following morphisms over
\overline{Y_{\tau}}

\begin{split}Z_{\min}\big(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\big)&\to\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\cap(Y_{\tau})^{\lambda}\\
\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\cap(Y_{\tau})^{\lambda}&\to Z_{\min}\big(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\big).\end{split}

(276)

It is easy that the morphism
Z_{\min}\big(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\big)\to\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\cap(Y_{\tau})^{\lambda}
exists. The existence of
\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\cap(Y_{\tau})^{\lambda}\to Z_{\min}\big(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\big)
is from that the morphism
\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\cap(Y_{\tau})^{\lambda}\to\big(Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}\big)^{0}_{\min}
is
\lambda
-equivariant with the trivial
\lambda
-action on
\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\cap(Y_{\tau})^{\lambda}
. This proves
Z_{\min}\big(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\big)=\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\cap(Y_{\tau})^{\lambda}
. Note that the equality is between subschemes of
Y_{\tau}
.

The morphism
\prod_{i=1}^{2}R_{i}^{\mathrm{s}}\to Z_{\min}\big(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\big)
is from
\prod_{i=1}^{2}R_{i}^{\mathrm{s}}\to\big(Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}\big)^{0}_{\min}
, which is
\lambda
-equivariant with trivial action on the source. It is an open immersion, since the composition
\prod_{i=1}^{2}R_{i}^{\mathrm{s}}\to Z_{\min}\big(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\big)\to\prod_{i=1}^{2}\mathrm{Quot}_{V_{i}(-m)/B/\Bbbk}^{P_{i}(t),\mathcal{L}}
is an open immersion.

We then construct the morphism
Z_{\min}\big(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\big)\to\prod_{i=1}^{2}\overline{R_{i}}
. By
Lemma 01R8
,
\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\cap Y_{\tau}\hookrightarrow Y_{\tau}
is the scheme theoretic image of
Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}\to Y_{\tau}
. The morphism
Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}\to Y_{\tau}
factors through
p_{\tau}^{-1}\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{2}\overline{R_{i}}\Big)\hookrightarrow Y_{\tau}
, so the dashed arrow below exists by the universal property of scheme theoretic image

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\cap Y_{\tau}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{\tau}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\tau}^{-1}\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{2}\overline{R_{i}}\Big).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}

(277)

We obtain a morphism
Z_{\min}\big(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\big)\to\prod_{i=1}^{2}\overline{R_{i}}
by taking fixed point subschemes with respect to
\lambda
of
\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\cap Y_{\tau}\to p_{\tau}^{-1}\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{2}\overline{R_{i}}\Big)
. It is easy to see
Z_{\min}\big(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\big)\to\prod_{i=1}^{2}\overline{R_{i}}
is a closed immersion over
\overline{Y_{\tau}}
. This proves
(2)
.

For
(3)
, the second equality follows immediately from the first. Recall that
\mathrm{K}_{\tau}=\prod_{i=1}^{2}\mathrm{SL}(V_{i})
. We have

\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{2}\overline{R_{i}}\Big)^{\mathrm{K}_{\tau},\mathrm{s}}=\prod_{i=1}^{2}\overline{R_{i}}^{\mathrm{SL}(V_{i}),\mathrm{s}}=\prod_{i=1}^{2}R_{i}^{\mathrm{s}}

(278)

where the first equality is a formula on stable loci of product linearisations (See
[
8
]
Lemma 6.6, Proposition 6.7), and the second equality is from Theorem
2.5
when
M^{\prime}\gg m\gg 0
. By
[
12
]
, Theorem 1.19 and
(2)
proved above, we have

Z_{\min}^{\mathrm{K}_{\tau},\mathrm{s}}\big(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\big)=Z_{\min}\big(\overline{Y_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}}\big)\cap\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{2}\overline{R_{i}}\Big)^{\mathrm{K}_{\tau},\mathrm{s}}=\prod_{i=1}^{2}R_{i}^{\mathrm{s}}

(279)

which proves
(3)
.

∎
